# Comic Tracker — Full Codebase & Documentation Optimization Audit

**Date:** 2026-06-14
**Scope:** End-to-end, feature-by-feature review of the entire application plus all documentation
**Mode:** Report-only — no code was changed
**Auditors:** 23 parallel agents, adversarially verified

---

## Executive Summary

**Total findings: 294** (including 4 intentionally cross-listed items that describe the same underlying change from two feature angles; ~290 distinct)

- 🔴 **High priority:** 55
- 🟡 **Medium priority:** 114
- 🟢 **Low priority:** 125

**Autonomy to remediate:**

- **Yes (fully autonomous):** 226
- **Partial (in-repo work autonomous; a decision, DB migration, or device test needed):** 64
- **No (requires owner/product decision or owner-supplied content):** 4

**Estimated recurring monthly savings if the cost-reducing items are adopted: roughly $40–$180/month today, scaling to several hundred dollars/month at growth.** The bulk of near-term savings is abuse-ceiling protection on the paid AI endpoints (uncapped guest/anonymous Anthropic spend that could otherwise reach ~$100/day per abusing IP), plus deferral of paid-tier upgrades — Supabase Pro (+$25/mo), Upstash, Resend, and PostHog/Sentry — by trimming storage, command, email, and event volume. The single largest realized-today win is correcting the AI provider economics in code and docs: Gemini is already primary (~5x cheaper than Sonnet), so the documented projections overstate spend and several routes still needlessly call Sonnet for text-only work.

**Estimated additional cost if every paid idea were adopted: ~$500–$650/year, none of it required.** The only net-new spend ideas are deferred/optional: the PriceCharting Legendary Sub proposal ($499/yr — explicitly NOT adopted, current pricing is the free eBay Browse API), and post-launch ZenRows proxy for cert-lookup (metered, gated off by default pending ROI review). The marketplace Stripe charge-model change is revenue-protecting, not a new cost.

**Overall health:** The codebase is mature, feature-complete for launch, and broadly well-structured, with a genuine test culture for pure helpers and a consistent design system. The most serious concentration of risk is in money-handling concurrency and webhook idempotency — the Stripe webhook marks events processed before handlers run (dropping failed money events on retry), auction bidding and Buy-It-Now are non-atomic, and bulk "Mark as Sold" silently corrupts revenue/profit. The second theme is cost-abuse exposure: several paid AI endpoints are unauthenticated and unthrottled, and the guest scan cap is enforced via a client-controlled header. The third theme is documentation drift — README, COST_PROJECTIONS, and three core reference docs all describe Anthropic as the primary vision provider when code runs Gemini first, understating an active ~5x cost optimization and overstating projected spend. The biggest, lowest-risk opportunities are: lock down the public AI/cron endpoints, fix the Stripe idempotency ordering, server-side WebP for cover uploads, delete ~1,728 lines of dead marketplace components, and reconcile the provider-order docs.

## Remediation Effort & Impact Analysis

> **Scope of this section:** completing the **226 findings that can be implemented fully autonomously** (every finding tagged `5. Can I handle autonomously: Yes` — no DB migration, env var, product decision, or copy approval needed; each is $0-to-implement and cost-neutral or cost-saving). The 64 "Partial" and 4 "No" findings are excluded here because they require a human-in-the-loop step.

### Effort Breakdown

| Size | Count | What these are | Raw per-item (active) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **S (small)** | 150 | one-liners, config/security flips, doc fixes, a11y labels, dead-code deletion, small query fixes | ~2–5 min |
| **M (medium)** | 70 | refactors with new tests, N+1 fixes, caching wiring, component memoization | ~8–20 min |
| **L (large)** | 6 | god-module decompositions (auctionDb 4.6k, email.ts 1.5k, ComicDetailsForm/Modal), money-path test suites, dialog-semantics sweep | ~30–90 min |

Two factors drive real wall-clock more than typing speed: (a) the project's mandatory test rule for any money/limit/permission change, and (b) re-running the quality gate (`typecheck + lint + test + build` ≈ 3–4 min) after each batch. The accelerator is running independent files in parallel via subagent workflows.

### 1. Duration — All 226
- **Raw sequential:** ~30 hours of work volume.
- **Realistic, parallelized active execution: ~12–18 hours** (active working time, not calendar time).
- **Per-item feel:** most are genuinely 2–15 minutes; the total is "many hours" only because there are 226 of them plus 6 real refactors.
- **Caveat:** the practical bottleneck is usually human review, not execution. Batched review ≈ 3–5 focused sessions; per-change review stretches calendar time regardless of execution speed.

### 2. Duration — By Priority

| Priority | Items | Mix (S/M/L) | Realistic active time | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 🔴 High | 38 | 17 / 19 / 2 | ~5–7 hrs | Slowest per item — money-path & security fixes get careful tests + verification. Highest value/hour. |
| 🟡 Medium | 84 | 46 / 34 / 4 | ~5–7 hrs | The 4 god-module decompositions dominate. |
| 🟢 Low | 104 | 87 / 17 / 0 | ~2–4 hrs | Mostly doc fixes, dead-code removal, a11y labels — tiny and highly parallel; largest count, least time. |

*(Sub-totals don't sum exactly to the all-up figure because batches share gate/verification overhead.)*

### 3. What to expect — as Company Executive
- **Defensible margins with zero new spend.** Abuse ceilings on paid AI endpoints, deferred Supabase Pro (+$25/mo), and trimmed Upstash/Resend/bandwidth keep unit economics healthy as you scale.
- **Trustworthy financials.** Stripe webhook idempotency fixed (no dropped paid events/chargebacks), no double-charges, accurate revenue & profit reporting, one consistent platform-fee calculation → audit-ready numbers.
- **Corrected cost story.** Docs claimed Anthropic-primary; the app actually runs Gemini (~5× cheaper). Current projections *overstate* spend — fixing this makes forecasts real.
- **Lower risk at launch.** Security holes closed (auth/rate-limit, RLS, IDOR, Clerk authz-bypass advisory) → reduced breach/abuse/chargeback exposure and a better accessibility/compliance posture.
- **Faster future velocity.** Dead code gone, monoliths split, money paths under test → less regression risk and lower maintenance drag on every future feature.

### 4. What to expect — as Platform Admin
- **Dashboards you can trust.** Scan-cost metrics corrected (no false cost alerts), accurate usage telemetry and cost attribution.
- **No silent fire-drills.** Crons can't fail open; rate limits protect Resend/Upstash/Anthropic quotas; the bid-restriction/strike system actually fires now (it was silently dead).
- **Real accountability.** State-changing admin actions written to the audit log; errors logged instead of swallowed; consistent error responses.
- **Cleaner moderation.** Content filter is tested, injection-hardened, and stops blocking innocent usernames/phone-like strings.
- **Less to babysit.** Consolidated admin-auth and config, fewer duplicated code paths.

### 5. What to expect — as End User
- **A noticeably snappier app** — memoized lists, code-split modals, `next/image`, lighter bundles, fewer redundant fetches across collection, homepage, and shop.
- **Money flows you can rely on** — no double charges, accurate sale/profit tracking, fair and atomic bidding & Buy-It-Now, dependable trades/offers.
- **Usable by everyone** — keyboard navigation, screen-reader support, reduced-motion, proper modal dialogs, bigger tap targets, visible focus — a real mobile win.
- **Fewer rough edges & crashes** — null-safe trade lists, consistent delete behavior, cleaner error messages.
- **Quietly safer** — data protected (RLS/IDOR fixes), safe image uploads, no leaked stack traces. *Same features, with the friction sanded off.*

### 6. Elevator Pitch — "Why we're doing this"
> **You've built a feature-complete platform. This is the pre-launch tune-up that turns "it works" into "it's production-grade" — for zero added cost.** In one focused push we close the security and money-handling gaps that could cost real dollars (chargebacks, abuse, wrong financials), put a ceiling on operating costs so margins hold as we grow, make the product faster and usable by everyone, and pay down the tech debt that would otherwise tax every future feature. It's the highest-ROI work on the board right now: **no new spend, lower risk, truer numbers, and a faster, more trustworthy product** — the difference between launching *confidently* and launching *hopefully*.

## Findings by Priority

### 🔴 High Priority

#### [H-AD1] Fail-open cron auth on usage check-alerts endpoint
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · security · effort S
- **1. Description:** The `/api/admin/usage/check-alerts` GET endpoint is meant to be called only by a cron job authenticated with CRON_SECRET. It runs heavy Supabase/Redis queries, an external Netlify fetch, and sends alert emails via Resend.
- **2. Issue identified:** The guard at line 40 is `if (CRON_SECRET && authHeader !== "Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}")`. If CRON_SECRET is unset/empty, the `CRON_SECRET &&` short-circuits to false and the request passes — the endpoint becomes fully public. Anyone could hammer it to drain Resend's 3K/mo email quota, run repeated Supabase scans, and inflate Upstash command counts. The sibling health-check route fails CLOSED with `if (!cronSecret || authHeader !== ...)` at line 16, proving the correct pattern already exists in the repo.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Change line 40 to fail closed exactly like health-check/route.ts:16: `if (!CRON_SECRET || authHeader !== "Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}") return 401`. Guarantees the endpoint is never public even with a misconfigured env.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves potential abuse spend — a public endpoint could exhaust the Resend free tier (3K emails/mo) and Upstash 10K/day; $0 in normal operation _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — One-line code change; CRON_SECRET is already a known env var per CLAUDE.md.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/admin/usage/check-alerts/route.ts:40 (fail-open) vs src/app/api/admin/health-check/route.ts:16 (correct fail-closed)

#### [H-AU1] Make placeBid atomic — concurrent bids corrupt winning state, current_bid, and bid_count
- **Area / Dimension:** auctions · correctness · effort L
- **1. Description:** placeBid reads the auction row and current winning bid via .single(), computes the new winner via proxy logic, then performs separate UPDATE/INSERT calls — with no transaction, row lock, conditional update, or unique constraint backstopping it.
- **2. Issue identified:** Two concurrent bids on the same auction both read the same current_bid (line 766) and currentWinningBid (line 781-786), both decide they win, and both flip is_winning and write the auction row. There is NO unique constraint enforcing one winning bid per auction (idx_bids_auction_winning is a non-unique partial index, migration line 123), so the table can end up with two is_winning=true rows; any later .single() read on is_winning=true (e.g. processEndedAuctions line 2401-2406) then throws a multiple-rows error and current_bid gets corrupted. Separately, bid_count: auction.bid_count + 1 (line 937) is a classic lost-update — both reads see the same count and both write +1, undercounting bids. This is the core money-handling concurrency hole on the most concurrency-sensitive surface.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Move bid resolution into a single Postgres function (RPC) that runs SELECT ... FOR UPDATE on the auction row, recomputes the winner, and writes bids+auction atomically. As a backstop add a unique partial index `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ON bids(auction_id) WHERE is_winning = TRUE`. As a quick in-repo win, replace `bid_count: auction.bid_count + 1` with the existing increment_field RPC, or add an optimistic guard `.eq('current_bid', expectedPrevBid)` on the update and retry on mismatch.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — increment_field swap and optimistic-guard are fully in-repo; the robust FOR UPDATE RPC and/or unique index require applying a Supabase DB migration (human step).
- **Evidence:** src/lib/auctionDb.ts:743-786, :766, :811-884, :868 & :894-905 & :932-945 (bid_count + 1 at :937); supabase/migrations/20260110_create_auctions.sql:123 (non-unique index); increment_field RPC at src/lib/creatorCreditsDb.ts:201 and src/lib/subscription.ts:294

#### [H-AU2] Fix payment-miss strike count so bid restrictions actually fire
- **Area / Dimension:** auctions · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** recordPaymentMissStrike counts prior strikes by querying auction_audit_log for auction_payment_expired events filtered by actor_profile_id = bidderId.
- **2. Issue identified:** The auction_payment_expired audit events are written with actorProfileId: null (auctionDb.ts:3547). So the count query `.eq('actor_profile_id', bidderId)` at line 3974 ALWAYS returns 0 priorStrikes, making totalStrikesInWindow permanently equal to 1. Since PAYMENT_MISS_STRIKE_THRESHOLD is 2, the branch that sets bid_restricted_at, flags the user, notifies admins, and applies the penalty is NEVER reached — the bid-restriction abuse-prevention system is silently dead. A correctly unit-tested pure helper (shouldFlagForPaymentMisses) exists but the production path reimplements the count inline and gets it wrong.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Count strikes off the profiles.payment_missed_count the function already increments (or query auction_audit_log without the broken actor filter, scoping by winner_id in eventData), and route the threshold decision through the already-tested shouldFlagForPaymentMisses helper instead of the inline reimplementation.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/auctionDb.ts:3971-3980, :3544-3554 (events logged with actorProfileId: null), :3943-3952; src/types/auction.ts:697 + src/types/__tests__/secondChance.test.ts:82

#### [H-AU3] Make Buy-It-Now atomic and add the same age/bid-restriction gates as bidding
- **Area / Dimension:** auctions · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** executeBuyItNow reads the auction filtered on status='active', then updates it to sold without re-checking status in the UPDATE's WHERE clause; the BIN route omits the gating the bid route enforces.
- **2. Issue identified:** executeBuyItNow reads with .eq('status','active') (line 1059-1064) but the UPDATE at line 1081-1090 has no status guard and no .select() to detect zero rows. Two concurrent BIN clicks (or a BIN concurrent with a winning bid / cron end) both pass the read guard and both UPDATE status=sold, overwriting winner_id — sold twice. By contrast processEndedAuctions correctly gates its UPDATE with .eq('status','active').select('id'). Separately, the BIN route checks suspension but omits the age_confirmed_at and bid_restricted_at gates the bid route enforces, so a restricted or unverified user can still buy.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add `.eq('status','active').select('id')` to the BIN auction UPDATE and treat 0 returned rows as 'already sold'. Mirror the bid route's age-verification and bid-restriction checks in the buy-now route for consistent gating.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/auctionDb.ts:1059-1064 & :1081-1090; src/app/api/auctions/[id]/buy-now/route.ts:14-54 vs src/app/api/auctions/[id]/bid/route.ts:55 & :64-79

#### [H-AU4] Delete ~1,728 lines of dead auction/trading modal components
- **Area / Dimension:** auctions · tech-debt · effort S
- **1. Description:** Six modal/form components exist under src/components/auction, src/components/trading, and src/components/creatorCredits with zero live references.
- **2. Issue identified:** CreateAuctionModal (427 lines), CreateListingModal (362), MakeOfferModal (201), OfferResponseModal (295), TradeProposalModal (315), and SellerResponseForm (128) have ZERO live references. Grep confirms each name appears ONLY in its own file and its barrel index.ts re-export line — no JSX render site, no destructuring import from the barrel. Total ~1,728 dead LOC. These are orphans from a refactor that consolidated flows into ListInShopModal / detail modals.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Delete the six component files and their entries in src/components/auction/index.ts (lines 6,7,10,11), src/components/trading/index.ts:4, and src/components/creatorCredits/index.ts:5. Re-run knip to confirm no new orphans cascade. Largest dead-code win in the codebase.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/components/auction/CreateAuctionModal.tsx (427L) + 5 more; grep verified each name appears only in its own file + barrel re-export (auction/index.ts:6,7,10,11; trading/index.ts:4; creatorCredits/index.ts:5)

#### [H-AU5] Add tests for auctionDb money functions (placeBid, executeBuyItNow, offers, processEndedAuctions, strike system)
- **Area / Dimension:** auctions · testing · effort L
- **1. Description:** src/lib/auctionDb.ts is the core auction engine (bids, buy-it-now, offers/counter-offers, ended-auction processing, seller ratings, payment-strike system), but only pure helpers are tested.
- **2. Issue identified:** The only auctionDb test imports a single pure helper, formatDeadlineForEmail. The money-critical functions are entirely untested: placeBid (with proxy-bid/anti-snipe/reserve logic), executeBuyItNow, createOffer, respondToOffer, submitSellerRating, processEndedAuctions, and recordPaymentMissStrike. The strike-count bug and a regression in bid/winner resolution would ship undetected. Project CLAUDE.md mandates tests for 'any function that handles money, limits, or permissions'.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Extract proxy-bid resolution and ended-auction winner/fee decision logic into pure functions and unit-test all branches exhaustively. Then test orchestrators with a mocked Supabase, prioritizing placeBid and processEndedAuctions. Add a test driving the strike-threshold decision through shouldFlagForPaymentMisses.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — In-repo refactor-to-testable plus tests.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/auctionDb.ts:737, :811-884, :1055, :1288, :1399, :2250, :2377; src/lib/__tests__/auctionDbHelpers.test.ts:15-17 only imports formatDeadlineForEmail

#### [H-CO1] Bulk "Mark as Sold" records no sale price and creates no sales row
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** The multi-select toolbar's "Mark as Sold" action bulk-flags selected comics as sold via /api/comics/bulk-update.
- **2. Issue identified:** handleBulkMarkSold posts field=is_sold with no sale price; bulk-update only sets is_sold and sold_at, never inserting a sales row. getUserComics filters out rows where sold_at is not null, so bulk-sold comics vanish from the active collection AND are absent from the sales-driven /sales page. The single-item handleMarkSold path correctly calls recordSale. Result: Sales count, totalRevenue, and Profit/Loss are silently wrong for any bulk-sold comic.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Route bulk sells through the same recordSale/sales-insert path as single sells (prompt for one batch price or per-item prices), or remove the bulk Mark-as-Sold action until it records proper sale rows. At minimum write sales rows so revenue/profit stay accurate.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Code is fully in-repo, but needs a product/UX decision on batch vs per-item sale price and the prompt copy.
- **Evidence:** src/app/collection/page.tsx:439-461; src/app/api/comics/bulk-update/route.ts:52-57; src/lib/db.ts:112; src/app/collection/page.tsx:548-563

#### [H-CO2] Divergent delete paths: stats page hard-deletes, collection page soft-deletes
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** Comics are removed from two screens through two backend routes with opposite semantics.
- **2. Issue identified:** The collection page's handleRemove and bulk delete POST to /api/comics/bulk-delete which SOFT-deletes (sets deleted_at, restorable via undo). The stats page's handleRemove calls removeFromCollection -> deleteComic -> DELETE /api/comics/[id], which HARD-deletes the row (verified .delete()) with no deleted_at and no recovery. Identical user intent permanently destroys data on the stats screen but is recoverable on the collection screen.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Make /api/comics/[id] DELETE soft-delete (set deleted_at) for consistency, or deprecate the hard-delete route and have removeFromCollection call bulk-delete so all removals are undo-capable.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/comics/[id]/route.ts:75 (hard .delete()); src/app/api/comics/bulk-delete/route.ts:71-74; src/app/stats/page.tsx:59-64; src/lib/db.ts:260-272

#### [H-CO3] Memoize collection page filter/sort/unique-list/stats computations
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** The collection page (~1554 lines, 'use client') derives filteredCollection, uniquePublishers/Titles/GradingCompanies, filteredValue, totalCollectionValue, stats, selectedItems, and visibleIds — all inline in the render body with no useMemo.
- **2. Issue identified:** Premium users have unlimited collections (hundreds to thousands of comics). Every state change — each search keystroke, filter toggle, selection checkbox, modal open — re-runs the full O(n log n) filter+sort, recomputes calculateCollectionValue twice, and rebuilds three Array.from(new Set(...)) unique lists. Verified essentially zero memoization in the file. This visibly janks search/selection on large collections.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Wrap filteredCollection, the unique* lists, filteredValue/totalCollectionValue, the stats object, selectedItems, and visibleIds in useMemo keyed on real inputs. Logic unchanged; eliminates redundant full-collection passes on unrelated state changes.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — pure client-side perf improvement _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — In-repo refactor of existing computations into useMemo.
- **Evidence:** src/app/collection/page.tsx:204-212, :237-308, :311-312, :315-329; grep useMemo/useCallback count ~0-2

#### [H-CO4] Re-compress and convert user-uploaded covers to WebP server-side
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** /api/comics/upload-cover accepts an image and writes the raw client-provided buffer verbatim into the comic-covers Supabase bucket with the client-supplied content-type — no resize, no format conversion.
- **2. Issue identified:** Verified upload-cover/route.ts:42-50 uploads the unmodified buffer with contentType: file.type, accepting files up to 10MB. sharp is already a project dependency but is imported only in coverHarvest.ts (which proves the pattern with sharp().webp({ quality: 85 })). WebP at equivalent quality is ~25-40% smaller than JPEG, and the comic-covers bucket (~400KB/photo) is the documented #1 scaling risk against the Supabase 1GB free storage cap.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** In upload-cover, pipe the buffer through sharp(): cap dimensions (~1200px max edge), convert to WebP quality ~80-85, and upload as image/webp. Reuse the harvest helper's settings.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves ~25-40% of per-cover storage plus enforces a small ceiling; extends the Supabase free 1GB bucket lifetime and defers the $25/mo Supabase Pro upgrade _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — sharp is already a dependency, used in coverHarvest.ts.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/comics/upload-cover/route.ts:42-50; src/lib/uploadLimits.ts:11 (10MB ceiling); src/lib/coverHarvest.ts:204; sharp at package.json:51

#### [H-CO5] Grid-view ComicCard and homepage use raw <img> instead of next/image
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** The grid view (ComicCard) renders cover images with a raw `<img src={coverImageUrl}>` tag, while the list view (ComicListItem) uses the optimized ComicImage (next/image) component. Cover images are ~400KB Supabase originals. The homepage also renders multiple raw <img> cover tags.
- **2. Issue identified:** Grid is the default collection view. Raw <img> serves full-resolution ~400KB originals as small thumbnails with no responsive srcset, no WebP/AVIF conversion, and no width/height optimization, bypassing the Netlify image CDN. For a grid of dozens-to-hundreds of covers this is a large, repeated download and grows Supabase Storage egress.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Migrate ComicCard's cover <img> to the existing ComicImage/next/image component and do the same for the homepage cover images. At minimum add loading="lazy" and explicit sizing. This cuts thumbnail payloads by roughly 90%.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Supabase Storage egress bandwidth at scale; reduces pressure on the free-tier storage/egress cap _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Code change is autonomous, but the raw <img> in ComicCard carries an intentional iOS-Safari repaint workaround (imgRef/imgReady fade-in, commit 9399494) — needs device validation that next/image preserves the fix before shipping.
- **Evidence:** src/components/ComicCard.tsx:36-42, :76-83; src/components/ComicListItem.tsx:34-39; src/app/page.tsx:254,291,328,604,678,734,807

#### [H-CO6] Make ComicDetailModal a proper accessible dialog (role, Escape, focus, scroll-lock)
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · accessibility · effort M
- **1. Description:** ComicDetailModal is the highest-traffic modal (opened from every comic in the collection), rendered via fixed inset-0.
- **2. Issue identified:** The modal lacks role="dialog", aria-modal="true", aria-label/labelledby, Escape-to-close, focus trap, and body scroll-lock; grep finds none of these anywhere in the file. The X close button (icon-only) has no aria-label. Keyboard users can tab behind the modal and the background scrolls on mobile. CoverLightbox.tsx already implements the correct pattern.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Mirror CoverLightbox.tsx: add role="dialog" aria-modal="true" aria-label, an Escape keydown handler, aria-label="Close" on the X, body overflow lock while open, and initial focus to the close button. Consider extracting a useModalA11y hook.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pattern already exists in CoverLightbox.tsx to copy.
- **Evidence:** src/components/ComicDetailModal.tsx:290-302; contrast src/components/CoverLightbox.tsx:50-58

#### [H-CO7] Make ComicCard keyboard-accessible and stop hiding actions behind hover-only
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · accessibility · effort M
- **1. Description:** ComicCard is the primary collection grid item. The whole card is a div with onClick, and the star/edit quick actions are absolutely positioned with opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100.
- **2. Issue identified:** (1) The card div has no role/tabIndex/onKeyDown, so it cannot be focused or activated by keyboard. (2) Star and edit buttons only appear on hover, so they are effectively unreachable on touch devices and for keyboard users. (3) Star/Edit buttons use title= instead of aria-label. This breaks core collection interaction on mobile and keyboard.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Make the card a real button (or add role="button" tabIndex={0} onKeyDown for Enter/Space). Reveal quick actions with focus-within in addition to hover and surface them on touch, and replace title= with aria-label on the Star/Edit buttons.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Self-contained in ComicCard.tsx.
- **Evidence:** src/components/ComicCard.tsx:69-72, :109, :118 & :127

#### [H-CC1] Add auth + rate limiting to public AI lookup endpoints (cost-abuse holes)
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** Four API routes call the paid Anthropic API (Haiku/Sonnet) on every cache-miss request: import-lookup, titles/suggest, cover-candidates, and comic-lookup.
- **2. Issue identified:** All four are fully public with no Clerk auth() check and no rate limit. titles/suggest is keystroke-driven autocomplete that hits Anthropic on any uncached query. import-lookup (Sonnet) and cover-candidates (Haiku) have neither auth nor throttle. comic-lookup caches but novel title/issue combos always hit Claude. middleware.ts isProtectedRoute includes none of them. An attacker varying inputs to force cache misses can drive unbounded Anthropic spend against the $10 prepaid balance.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Wrap each route with checkRateLimit(rateLimiters.lookup, getRateLimitIdentifier(null, ip)) exactly as con-mode-lookup/route.ts:88-92 does. For titles/suggest use a tighter per-IP limit. Reuse the existing src/lib/rateLimit.ts helper — zero new dependencies.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves potentially $10s in an abuse burst; bounds worst-case Anthropic spend _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pattern already exists in con-mode-lookup; Redis env vars already configured.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/titles/suggest/route.ts:24-50; src/app/api/import-lookup/route.ts:31-72; src/app/api/cover-candidates/route.ts:75-93; src/app/api/comic-lookup/route.ts:37-132; contrast con-mode-lookup/route.ts:88-92; middleware.ts:4-18

#### [H-CC2] Cron routes reset-scans and check-alerts fail OPEN when CRON_SECRET is unset
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · security · effort S
- **1. Description:** Cron endpoints should be protected by a Bearer CRON_SECRET. Most fail closed, but two use an OR/short-circuit that grants access when the secret is not configured.
- **2. Issue identified:** src/app/api/cron/reset-scans/route.ts:27 uses `const isAuthorized = !CRON_SECRET || authHeader === ...` — if CRON_SECRET is empty/unset, any caller can reset every free user's monthly scan count to zero. src/app/api/admin/usage/check-alerts/route.ts:40 uses `if (CRON_SECRET && authHeader !== ...)` — same fail-open: unset secret leaves the alert/email endpoint fully public. Sibling crons (process-auctions/route.ts:23) correctly fail closed.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Change both to the fail-closed pattern: `if (!CRON_SECRET || authHeader !== `Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}`) return 401;`. Keep the reset-scans dev bypass behind NODE_ENV==='development' only.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 to fix; prevents free Resend-quota burn and unauthorized scan-count resets _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pure code change; fail-closed pattern already present in sibling cron routes.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/cron/reset-scans/route.ts:27-31; src/app/api/admin/usage/check-alerts/route.ts:39-42; contrast process-auctions/route.ts:22-25

#### [H-CC3] Upgrade @clerk/nextjs to patch high-severity authorization-bypass advisory
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · security · effort S
- **1. Description:** npm audit reports a high-severity authorization bypass in the installed Clerk packages (@clerk/nextjs, @clerk/backend, @clerk/clerk-react, @clerk/shared, which also pulls vulnerable js-cookie).
- **2. Issue identified:** Installed @clerk/nextjs is 6.36.6, inside the vulnerable range 6.0.0–6.39.2 per the local audit advisory DB. The advisory is an authorization bypass when combining organization, billing, or reverification checks. Clerk is the auth backbone of this app. The latest 6.x patch is 6.39.5, above the vulnerable range — a within-major bump clears it without the risky 7.x jump.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Bump @clerk/nextjs to 6.39.5 (or run npm audit fix), re-run npm test and a build, and verify auth/middleware still work. Do NOT jump to 7.x for this patch.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — security patch, no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Version bump is autonomous, but auth changes must be smoke-tested by a human (Clerk widgets fail silently on LAN dev per project memory).
- **Evidence:** npm audit: '@clerk/nextjs 6.0.0 - 6.39.2 Severity: high ... authorization bypass'; installed 6.36.6; latest 6.x patch 6.39.5 (npm view)

#### [H-CC4] Restrict next/image remotePatterns from wildcard hostname to known domains
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · security · effort S
- **1. Description:** next.config.mjs sets images.remotePatterns to a single entry with hostname: "**", allowing the Next/Netlify image optimizer to fetch and proxy images from ANY https host. next/image is actively used in 27 source files.
- **2. Issue identified:** A wildcard image host turns the /_next/image endpoint into an open image proxy — attackers can pass arbitrary remote URLs to drive (a) SSRF-style fetches and (b) Netlify image-transformation/bandwidth cost abuse. The app only legitimately loads remote images from three hosts: Supabase storage (*.supabase.co), i.ebayimg.com, and img.clerk.com. The config also lacks explicit formats and minimumCacheTTL.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace hostname: "**" with explicit remotePatterns entries for *.supabase.co, i.ebayimg.com, and img.clerk.com. Also add formats: ['image/avif','image/webp'] and a minimumCacheTTL.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Neutral in normal use; under abuse prevents potentially significant Netlify image-transform/bandwidth charges; explicit cache settings reduce optimizer invocations _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Code change is autonomous, but the exact Supabase project subdomain should be confirmed by the user before pinning the allowlist.
- **Evidence:** next.config.mjs:10-17; 27 files import next/image; remote hosts in src/: i.ebayimg.com, *.supabase.co, img.clerk.com (CustomProfilePage.tsx:703 user.imageUrl)

#### [H-CC5] Add prefers-reduced-motion handling to global animations
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · accessibility · effort S
- **1. Description:** globals.css defines transform/opacity keyframe animations (pulse, pop-in, shake, slide-in, slide-up, shimmer) including infinite pulse and shimmer, with 154 animate-* class usages and 401 transition usages across src; the pop-art design leans on hover translate + shadow motion throughout.
- **2. Issue identified:** Zero prefers-reduced-motion support anywhere in the codebase (grep returned 0). Users who set OS 'reduce motion' still get all scaling, shaking, sliding, and infinite shimmer/pulse animations — a WCAG 2.3.3 concern that can cause nausea/dizziness for vestibular-sensitive users.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add a single @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) block in globals.css that sets animation-duration/transition-duration to 0.01ms (and animation-iteration-count: 1) site-wide.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pure CSS addition to globals.css.
- **Evidence:** src/app/globals.css:436-534; grep across src: 0 prefers-reduced-motion, 154 animate-* usages, 401 transition usages

#### [H-CC6] Add role/aria-live to Toast container and label the dismiss button
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · accessibility · effort S
- **1. Description:** Toast is the app-wide feedback mechanism for success/error/warning/info (scanning, saving comics, storage-full, listing actions, etc.), rendered from a context provider.
- **2. Issue identified:** The toast container div has no role="status"/role="alert" and no aria-live, so screen-reader users get no announcement of any success or error message (aria-live count is 0 across the entire codebase). The dismiss (X) button also has no aria-label.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add role="status" aria-live="polite" to the toast container (or aria-live="assertive" for error toasts), and add aria-label="Dismiss notification" to the X button.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Self-contained edit in Toast.tsx.
- **Evidence:** src/components/Toast.tsx:78, :86-91; grep: 0 aria-live across src/

#### [H-CC7] Add dialog semantics + Escape to the overlay modals missing them
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · accessibility · effort L
- **1. Description:** 38 components use fixed inset-0 overlay modals (AgeVerificationModal, ShareCollectionModal, SuggestKeyInfoModal, VariantsModal, DataMigrationModal, SignUpPromptModal, GradeSelector, etc.).
- **2. Issue identified:** Only CoverLightbox has role="dialog" (1 of 38 files), and only 5 components handle Escape. AgeVerificationModal — a blocking gate — has no role, aria, Escape, or focus management. Screen-reader and keyboard users cannot reliably perceive or dismiss most modals.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Introduce a shared Modal/Dialog wrapper (or useModalA11y hook) providing role="dialog", aria-modal, focus trap, Escape close, and body scroll-lock, then adopt it across the overlay modals — starting with the blocking AgeVerificationModal and high-traffic ShareCollectionModal/VariantsModal.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Repo-wide refactor touching many files; doable in-repo but large.
- **Evidence:** grep: 38 files with 'fixed inset-0', only 1 'role="dialog"' (CoverLightbox.tsx:50), only 5 files with Escape handling; src/components/AgeVerificationModal.tsx:38-89

#### [H-CC8] Associate form labels with inputs (htmlFor/id) and add aria-invalid for errors
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · accessibility · effort M
- **1. Description:** Forms render 75 <label> elements alongside their inputs/selects, with visual labels like 'Publisher', 'Release Year', 'Writer', 'Variant'.
- **2. Issue identified:** 0 of the 75 labels use htmlFor and the adjacent inputs/selects have no matching id (grep: 0 htmlFor across src), so labels are not programmatically associated — screen readers do not announce the field name on focus. aria-invalid is also 0 app-wide, so validation errors are not associated with their fields.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add matching id/htmlFor pairs (or wrap inputs inside their <label>) across the forms, and set aria-invalid + aria-describedby pointing at inline error text when a field fails validation.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Mechanical id/htmlFor wiring across form files.
- **Evidence:** src/components/ComicDetailsForm.tsx:654 & :703 & :714; grep: 0 htmlFor, 0 aria-invalid across src/ — 75 <label> total

Nearly every factual claim contradicts the shipped app.

#### [H-DOC2] Fix AI-provider direction drift: docs call Anthropic primary, code makes Gemini primary
- **Area / Dimension:** documentation · documentation · effort M
- **1. Description:** The vision provider order determines which AI service is hit first and thus the dominant per-scan cost. Multiple docs claim Anthropic is primary while shipped code makes Gemini primary.
- **2. Issue identified:** Code sets VISION_PROVIDER_ORDER = ['gemini','anthropic'] (src/lib/models.ts:17) and aiProvider.ts:20 builds the provider list from that order — Gemini is primary, Anthropic is fallback. Several docs contradict this: TECHNICAL_FEATURES.md:10 says 'Claude Vision (primary) → Gemini (fallback)' (internally inconsistent — :148 correctly says Gemini → Anthropic); TECHNICAL_FEATURES.md:208; ARCHITECTURE.md:21 legend labels 'Anthropic/Claude — AI analysis (primary)'; CLAUDE.md:151 labels Gemini merely 'AI fallback'. The feature spec docs/features/01-ai-cover-recognition.md:155-156 correctly states 'Gemini Flash (primary) -> Claude Sonnet (fallback)... 5x cheaper'.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Standardize all docs to 'Gemini 2.0 Flash primary, Anthropic Sonnet 4.5 fallback' to match VISION_PROVIDER_ORDER and the feature spec. Fix TECHNICAL_FEATURES.md:10 and :208, ARCHITECTURE.md:21 legend, and CLAUDE.md:151.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — doc-only, but corrects a cost-modeling error (documents a real ~5x per-scan saving from Gemini-first) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Single-line doc edits; ground truth confirmed in models.ts.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/models.ts:17; src/lib/aiProvider.ts:20; docs/TECHNICAL_FEATURES.md:10,148,208; ARCHITECTURE.md:21,49,1229; CLAUDE.md:151; correct in docs/features/01-ai-cover-recognition.md:155-156

#### [H-DOC3] COST_PROJECTIONS.md overstates AI spend by modeling Anthropic as the primary vision provider
- **Area / Dimension:** documentation · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** COST_PROJECTIONS.md projects monthly AI cost across user-scale tiers.
- **2. Issue identified:** The doc lists 'Claude Sonnet 4 ... ~$0.01-0.03/scan' (line 97), names 'Current: Claude Sonnet 4 (vision)' (line 100), and projects Anthropic at $10-30/mo (beta) scaling to $400-1,000/mo. Because code runs Gemini 2.0 Flash as primary (~$0.001/scan) and only falls back to Anthropic, the real Anthropic spend is a small fraction of these projections — the cost model is built on the wrong primary provider, making every per-user projection too high.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Recompute the AI cost section around Gemini-primary economics (~$0.001/scan baseline + occasional Anthropic fallback). Update the scale tables (lines 251/270/288/306) so Gemini is the dominant line item and Anthropic is a small fallback cost.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 to fix; corrects an overstated projection (real AI cost is materially lower than documented) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** COST_PROJECTIONS.md:97,100,102, :251,:270,:288,:306; contradicted by src/lib/models.ts:17 and ARCHITECTURE.md:1229

The checklist would pass a deploy missing eBay pricing, Stripe checkout price IDs, or the cron secret.

#### [H-EM1] Escape user-controlled content before interpolating into email HTML
- **Area / Dimension:** email · security · effort M
- **1. Description:** Email templates in src/lib/email.ts interpolate dynamic strings directly into HTML template literals via ${...} with no HTML escaping anywhere in the email path; grep for escape|sanitize in email.ts returns nothing.
- **2. Issue identified:** Several rendered fields are user-controlled and land inside HTML unescaped: messagePreview (raw message text, rendered inside a <blockquote>), buyerName/sellerName/senderName (display names — no display_name validation/sanitization in src/app/api/profile/), and comicTitle (rendered raw). A user who sets a display name or sends a message containing HTML/<a>/<style> tags or a phishing link gets it rendered verbatim in the recipient's inbox — stored HTML injection enabling email phishing/spoofing and layout breakage.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add a tiny escapeHtml() helper (replace & < > " ') and wrap every interpolated dynamic string in the HTML branch of each template (names, comicTitle, messagePreview, failureReason). Leave the plain-text branch unchanged. Centralize via the shared layout/button helpers.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pure in-repo code change; add helper and wrap interpolations.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/email.ts:432-434, :273/295/317/339; src/lib/messagingDb.ts:340; no escape helper exists (grep returns nothing)

#### [H-FE1] Re-enable pinch-to-zoom (remove maximumScale=1 / userScalable=false)
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · accessibility · effort S
- **1. Description:** The root layout viewport config disables user zoom on every page (maximumScale: 1, userScalable: false) to keep a PWA/native feel.
- **2. Issue identified:** Disabling user scaling is a WCAG 1.4.4 (Resize Text) / 1.4.10 failure — low-vision users on mobile cannot pinch-zoom any page. It is in the root layout, so it applies app-wide, and CoverLightbox even documents that its own pinch-zoom is disabled because of this global restriction. This is also a PWA/App-Store accessibility red flag.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Remove userScalable:false and maximumScale:1 (or set maximumScale to >=5). If a no-zoom feel is wanted only on the camera/scan surface, scope it there rather than globally.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Code change is trivial, but it's a visible product/UX decision (PWA feel vs. zoomability) the owner should approve.
- **Evidence:** src/app/layout.tsx:105-106; rationale referenced in src/components/CoverLightbox.tsx:23-26

#### [H-FE2] Stop shipping fabricated price-change figures on the signed-in homepage
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** The signed-in homepage renders 'Biggest Increase' and 'Biggest Decline' insight cards (with a 30/60/90-day toggle) plus matching detail modals, presented as real portfolio market movement.
- **2. Issue identified:** The increase/decline values are invented. getBiggestIncrease multiplies the current value by a hardcoded constant (0.92/0.88/0.82) to fake a 'previous value', and getBiggestDecline does the same with 1.05/1.1/1.15. EVERY book always shows the same fixed +8/+12/+18% increase and -5/-9/-13% decline, and the day toggle merely swaps the fake multiplier. Code comments openly say 'Simulate previous value'. Users of a paid product see invented financial analytics. The 'Best Buy' card next to them is legitimate.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Hide the Biggest Increase / Biggest Decline cards and their modals until real price_history data exists (keep the Best Buy ROI card), or wire them to actual price history. At minimum gate the two fabricated cards behind a feature flag.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Hiding the two fake cards is fully autonomous; wiring to real price history needs a product decision on the data source.
- **Evidence:** src/app/page.tsx:104-130, 119 comment 'Simulate previous value', 133-159, 236-344, 162-183

- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · ux · effort S
