#### [H-MK1] Auto-reject competing offers and guard listing status when an offer is accepted
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** respondToOffer's accept path finalizes a sale but leaves other buyers' pending/countered offers on the same fixed-price listing untouched and does not re-check that the listing is still active.
- **2. Issue identified:** cancelAuction correctly flips sibling offers to auto_rejected and notifies those buyers, but the accept path does not: after the seller accepts buyer A's offer, buyer B's offer stays 'pending', so the seller could accept it too (double-sell of one comic), and buyer B keeps seeing a live offer. The accept also UPDATEs the listing to sold with no .eq('status','active') guard and no .select(), so it can clobber a listing already sold by a concurrent accept.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** In the accept path, run one UPDATE setting all other pending/countered offers on the listing to auto_rejected (and notify those buyers), and gate the listing UPDATE on status='active' returning rows (.eq('status','active').select()) before treating the sale as final.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/auctionDb.ts:1421-1485; contrast src/lib/auctionDb.ts:631-645

#### [H-MK2] Eliminate N+1 seller-profile fetches and add a limit in getUserTrades preview list
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** getUserTrades builds the Trades-page preview list (Active/History tabs), but loads the entire trade history (no .limit()) and then resolves the other participant one trade at a time.
- **2. Issue identified:** The trades query is ordered by updated_at with no .limit(), so a heavy trader loads all trades at once. The for-of loop then awaits getSellerProfile(otherUserId) serially per trade — 30 trades = 1+30 serial Supabase round-trips. The other participant is frequently the same person across trades, so the same profile is re-fetched repeatedly.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Collect the distinct otherUserId set, fetch all profiles in one `.in('id', ids)` query into a Map, assemble previews synchronously, and add a `.limit()/.range()` (e.g. 50) with pagination.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 direct at beta; saves Supabase compute/egress by converting O(N) serial queries to O(1) and bounding payload size _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/tradingDb.ts:139-140, :153-174 (getSellerProfile per trade at line 157)

#### [H-MK3] Batch comic + profile fetches in getUserMatches (N+1 explosion)
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** getUserMatches powers the Trades > Matches tab, returning matches grouped by the user's comic.
- **2. Issue identified:** For EACH match row it runs three awaited queries: two single-row comic fetches (Promise.all) plus a serial getSellerProfile(). With M matches that's ~3*M round-trips, and the matches GET also optionally runs triggerMatchFinding (full-scan RPC) first. Comics and the other user repeat across matches but are re-fetched every time. getUserMatches also has no .limit().
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Gather all distinct comicIds and otherUserIds, issue one `.in('id', comicIds)` for comics and one `.in('id', userIds)` for profiles, build Maps, then assemble groups in memory (drops ~3*M queries to 2). Add a result limit.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 direct at beta; saves Supabase round-trips / compute and defers Pro-tier pressure ($25/mo) at scale _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/tradingDb.ts:507-564, :483-499; src/app/api/trades/matches/route.ts:37-40

#### [H-MK4] Replace per-comic want-count COUNT queries in /api/trades/available with one aggregate
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** The Shop > For Trade tab loads up to 100 tradeable comics, each annotated with a wantCount (how many users have it on a hunt list).
- **2. Issue identified:** The route does Promise.all over up to 100 comics, firing a separate `count: 'exact', head: true` query against key_hunt_lists per comic — up to 100 concurrent COUNT queries on every page load. Additionally the count uses `.ilike('title_normalized', comic.title.toLowerCase().trim())` with no wildcards, so case-insensitive matching prevents the composite btree idx_key_hunt_lists_lookup from being used.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Compute want counts in one pass: fetch the matching key_hunt_lists rows for the set of (title_normalized, issue_number) pairs in a single query and tally in JS, or use a Postgres RPC that GROUP BYs. Replace `.ilike` with `.eq` on the normalized column so the existing composite index applies.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 direct at beta; saves significant Supabase compute/egress on a hot marketplace path (collapses ~100 queries to 1-2) and reduces Pro-tier pressure _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — The in-JS tally + .eq rewrite is fully autonomous; an RPC alternative would need a human-applied DB migration but is not required.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/trades/available/route.ts:63-96, .ilike at :69; composite index idx_key_hunt_lists_lookup at supabase/migrations/20260121_performance_indexes.sql:9

#### [H-MK5] for-trade comics route uses anon Supabase client under RLS — returns nothing for non-public owners
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** GET /api/comics/for-trade returns a user's own for-trade comics, and the PATCH toggle at /api/comics/[id]/for-trade verifies comic ownership — both via the anon `supabase` client.
- **2. Issue identified:** The anon client never attaches a Clerk JWT, so `auth.current_profile_id()` is NULL and the comics SELECT RLS only exposes rows from `is_public = TRUE` profiles. The 'own comics' read silently returns zero rows for any non-public profile, and the toggle's ownership SELECT returns 'Comic not found' (404) for the legitimate owner. The same anon-under-RLS class was previously confirmed and fixed in getAuctionSecondChanceState.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Use supabaseAdmin for these server-side, already-authenticated reads and scope explicitly with `.eq('user_id', profileId)` (and ownership `.eq('id', id)` then check user_id), exactly as /api/trades/available already does.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/comics/for-trade/route.ts:7,40-46; src/app/api/comics/[id]/for-trade/route.ts:7,43-61; src/lib/supabase.ts:8; supabase/migrations/20260110_production_rls_policies.sql:176-184,397

#### [H-MK6] completeTrade ownership swap is not atomic and runs N+1 hunt-list cleanup
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** completeTrade swaps comic ownership for both parties and removes matched comics from the new owner's hunt list when both confirm receipt.
- **2. Issue identified:** Each comic is updated in its own un-transactioned statement inside a JS for-loop; if the process dies mid-loop, half the comics swap owners and the trade is left inconsistent with no rollback. It also runs 2 extra serial queries per comic plus a hunt-list delete (N+1). confirmReceipt calls completeTrade BEFORE persisting its own updateData, so a swap failure ordering can mark the trade completed without a clean ownership transfer in edge cases.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Move the swap + hunt-list cleanup into a single Postgres SECURITY DEFINER function (one transaction), mirroring find_trade_matches. Call it from confirmReceipt and surface failure before marking status completed.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change (data-integrity + fewer round-trips) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — True atomicity needs a human-applied DB migration; a JS-side Promise.all + ordering fix is autonomous but won't give real atomicity.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/tradingDb.ts:334-392 (swap at 354), :307-318

#### [H-MK7] Add tests for tradingDb permission, state-transition, and transaction-shaping logic
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · testing · effort M
- **1. Description:** src/lib/tradingDb.ts implements the trade lifecycle (participant authorization, status gating, shipping, receipt confirmation, comic-ownership swap) and the /api/transactions shaper — exactly the state-transition/permission/money logic the CLAUDE.md test mandate requires — yet no tradingDb test file exists.
- **2. Issue identified:** Confirmed no tradingDb test exists. Untested logic includes permission checks ('Not a participant'), state-transition guards ('Trade must be accepted before shipping'), the both-shipped/both-received -> advance-status rules, completeTrade's owner-swap direction, the transform partitioning, and toTransactionRow's salePrice fallback chain. A regression could silently transfer the wrong comics, let a non-participant mutate a trade, or mis-price a transaction.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Extract the pure decision logic into testable helpers and add unit tests covering non-participant rejection, illegal transitions, correct owner-swap direction, both-shipped/both-received promotion, and the price fallback ordering. Mock @/lib/supabase for the orchestrators.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/__tests__/ has no tradingDb test; src/lib/tradingDb.ts:182,196,241,251,253-254,290,295,334,354; src/app/api/transactions/route.ts:55-89

#### [H-MK8] find_trade_matches RPC references nonexistent columns and uses an unindexed text-normalized self-join
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · database · effort M
- **1. Description:** find_trade_matches (invoked on every for_trade toggle and Matches refresh) joins comics to key_hunt_lists on LOWER(TRIM(title)) / TRIM(issue_number) and scores via seller_rating columns.
- **2. Issue identified:** (1) Correctness: the quality_score reads p_b.seller_rating and p_b.seller_rating_count, but those columns are never created anywhere — the real columns are positive_ratings/negative_ratings. At runtime PostgreSQL rejects the function with 'column does not exist', so trade matching likely fails silently. (2) Performance: the join keys are wrapped in LOWER(TRIM(...)), which cannot use a plain b-tree index, forcing full scans of comics as the corpus grows.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Fix the column references to positive_ratings/negative_ratings so matching works, then add functional indexes on comics (LOWER(TRIM(title)), TRIM(issue_number)) WHERE for_trade = true and mirror on key_hunt_lists. Consider debouncing the fire-and-forget trigger.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 now; fixing correctness restores trade matching; index reduces DB CPU and defers Supabase Pro ($25/mo) at scale _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Both the column fix and functional indexes require a human-applied DB migration; the toggle debounce is autonomous.
- **Evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260128_trading_phase3_matching.sql:127-129,133-134,146-147; supabase/migrations/20260110_create_auctions.sql:65-66; src/app/api/comics/[id]/for-trade/route.ts:66-71

#### [H-MK9] /api/transactions uses anon Supabase client for winner-scoped reads under RLS
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** GET /api/transactions reads auctions/bids/offers filtered by the caller's profile id (winner_id, bidder_id, buyer_id) via the anon `supabase` client.
- **2. Issue identified:** The auctions SELECT RLS only exposes rows where status='active' OR seller_id/winner_id = current_profile_id(); the anon client carries no Clerk JWT so current_profile_id() is NULL. A winner's ended/sold purchase and auction-win rows are therefore invisible, so the purchases/wins tabs can silently render empty even when the user has purchases. bids/offers join `auctions!inner`, so a filtered-out auction drops the whole row.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Switch /api/transactions reads to supabaseAdmin (the route is already authed and filters by the verified profile id), matching /api/trades/available.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/transactions/route.ts:7,118,132,147,187; supabase/migrations/20260110_create_auctions.sql:189-194

#### [H-MK10] trades/available crashes on null comic title/issue_number due to unsafe `comic: any`
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** The available-trades endpoint maps over comics and, inside Promise.all, calls comic.title.toLowerCase().trim() and comic.issue_number.trim() to build a want-count query. The row is typed as `any`.
- **2. Issue identified:** The comics table defines title and issue_number as nullable TEXT. When any for_trade comic row has a null title or issue_number, comic.title.toLowerCase() throws TypeError, and because it is inside Promise.all the whole request rejects and returns a 500 — the entire available-trades feed breaks for everyone, not just the bad row. The `comic: any` typing hides this from the compiler.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Guard the values: `const t = comic.title?.toLowerCase().trim(); if (!t || !comic.issue_number) return null/skip;` and filter nulls from the result. Replace comic: any with a typed row so the null fields surface at compile time.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/trades/available/route.ts:64, 69-70; nullable columns title/issue_number TEXT in supabase/schema.sql

#### [H-MS1] Eliminate N+1 profile queries and full-message over-fetch in getUserConversations
- **Area / Dimension:** messaging · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** getUserConversations loads up to 50 conversations with ALL nested messages (no limit on the nested select), then loops awaiting getSellerProfile once per conversation — each a separate single-row profiles query.
- **2. Issue identified:** Two compounding problems on the hot /messages inbox path and every realtime refresh: (1) the nested messages(...) select has no limit, so the entire message history of every conversation is transferred just to compute the last-message preview and unread count in JS; (2) the for-of loop awaits getSellerProfile inside the loop, so N conversations trigger 1 + N sequential round-trips (51 serial queries at the 50-conversation cap).
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Batch the other-participant profiles into one query via .in('id', otherUserIds) resolved into a Map (drops 51 round-trips to 2). Cap the nested messages select for the preview (order created_at desc, limit 1) and compute unread via a separate aggregate. Ideally push last-message + unread-count into SQL (Postgres view/RPC).
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 now — reduces Supabase egress/compute (~25x fewer DB calls on this path), defers Pro ($25/mo) pressure at scale _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Batched-profile fix and capped nested select are fully in-repo; the last-message+unread SQL view/RPC requires applying a Supabase migration.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/messagingDb.ts:49-56, :68-104, :73; src/lib/auctionDb.ts:2334-2341

#### [H-MS2] Add unit tests for contentFilter.ts blocking/flagging logic (untested anti-fraud gate)
- **Area / Dimension:** messaging · testing · effort S
- **1. Description:** src/lib/contentFilter.ts gates user messages — BLOCKED patterns (phone/email) reject the message, FLAG patterns (payment apps, off-platform contact, urgency+payment) mark it for review — and is 100% pure functions built on hand-written regexes. No test file exists.
- **2. Issue identified:** Project CLAUDE.md mandates unit tests for all validation/permissions business logic. This brittle, regression-prone module gates contact-info leakage and feeds the moderation pipeline, yet has zero coverage — a typo'd regex would silently break blocking with no signal. sendMessage() consumes this gate and is also untested. Highest-ROI untested pure module in scope.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add src/lib/__tests__/contentFilter.test.ts covering: phone/email blocked across formats plus false-positive guards (e.g. '24/7', '1-800'); each flag pattern (venmo/zelle/whatsapp/telegram + urgency-payment combos); clean pass-through; and sanitizeContent trimming. No mocks required.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/contentFilter.ts:9, :23, :54, :88; no contentFilter test in src/lib/__tests__/; src/lib/messagingDb.ts:281 consumes checkMessageContent untested

#### [H-PAY1] Webhook marks event processed BEFORE running handlers — failed money events dropped on Stripe retry
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** The Stripe webhook writes an Upstash Redis idempotency marker before the event-handler switch runs: it checks if an event was already processed and, if not, immediately marks it processed, then dispatches the handler.
- **2. Issue identified:** The duplicate-check reads the key (lines 53-56) then cacheSet(eventIdKey, true, 'webhook') runs at line 59 BEFORE the switch at line 62. If any handler throws — e.g. a transient Supabase write failure — the outer catch returns HTTP 500 so Stripe retries, but the retry hits the already-set key and is silently dropped as {duplicate:true}. The money event (subscription upgrade, marketplace sale flip to sold/paid, scan credit, seller payout) is then permanently lost. (When Upstash is unconfigured, cacheGet returns null and cacheSet is a no-op, so idempotency is silently disabled entirely — a latent issue.)
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Move the cacheSet 'mark processed' call to AFTER the switch completes successfully (just before the final NextResponse.json), keeping the duplicate read-check up front. Optionally use a two-state marker (in-progress vs done) or a short in-flight lock.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change (prevents revenue/chargeback loss, not a service cost) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pure code change in the webhook route; reorder the idempotency marker to the success path.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:53-59, 62-130, 133-135; cache no-op-when-unconfigured at src/lib/cache.ts:56,80

#### [H-PAY2] Checkout doesn't block a second subscription during the webhook-lag window — duplicate Stripe subscriptions possible
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** The subscription checkout route only rejects re-subscribe when the app DB tier is already 'premium'.
- **2. Issue identified:** checkout/route.ts:114-122 guards only on status.tier === 'premium'. The handleSubscriptionCreated webhook sets tier=premium, so once it lands the guard works — but in the lag window before customer.subscription.created arrives, the DB tier is still 'free', so a user clicking subscribe again creates a SECOND active Stripe subscription on the same customer. upgradeToPremium overwrites stripe_subscription_id, so the first subscription keeps billing the card with no DB record to manage/cancel it.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Before creating a checkout session, list the customer's existing Stripe subscriptions (stripe.subscriptions.list({ customer, status: 'all' })) and reject if an active/trialing one exists. Store and refuse duplicate subscription ids.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Prevents accidental double subscription charges to customers (chargeback / refund / trust cost); $0 to platform but high customer-harm prevention _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/billing/checkout/route.ts:113-122; src/lib/subscription.ts:540-561

#### [H-PAY3] Subscription money/limit/trial engine has almost no test coverage (only constants tested)
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · testing · effort M
- **1. Description:** subscription.ts (677 lines) carries the money/limit/trial logic but its test file only asserts numeric constants.
- **2. Issue identified:** subscription.test.ts (27 lines) tests only GUEST_SCAN_LIMIT, FREE_MONTHLY_SCAN_LIMIT, SCAN_PACK_AMOUNT and their relationships. Untested: calculatePlatformFee / getTransactionFeePercent (5% vs 8%, rounding), scansRemaining/canScan, and the webhook status mapping. The project CLAUDE.md mandates tests for 'any function that handles money, limits, or permissions'. calculatePlatformFee (Math.round, no floor) also diverges from calculateDestinationAmount (Math.floor + floor) with no test pinning either.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add unit tests for calculatePlatformFee/getTransactionFeePercent (boundary amounts, premium vs free, rounding), scansRemaining/canScan, and a test asserting calculatePlatformFee and calculateDestinationAmount agree (or documenting why they differ).
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — prevents money-math regressions _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/__tests__/subscription.test.ts:1-27; src/lib/subscription.ts:655-677

#### [H-PR1] Wire Redis ebayPrice cache into con-mode-lookup (Key Hunt's real pricing path)
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** /api/con-mode-lookup is the endpoint Key Hunt actually calls and is the highest-volume pricing route (every con-mode scan / manual lookup).
- **2. Issue identified:** Unlike /api/ebay-prices and /api/comics/[id]/refresh-value, con-mode-lookup never touches the Redis ebayPrice cache (cacheGet/cacheSet). It only checks the durable comic_metadata DB; on any DB miss it calls searchActiveListings directly. The 12h Redis ebayPrice layer that exists specifically to throttle eBay calls is bypassed for the busiest consumer, and a price fetched via the sibling routes is never reused here.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Before the eBay call (around con-mode-lookup/route.ts:172) do cacheGet(generateEbayPriceCacheKey(...), 'ebayPrice'); on hit build the result from it, and on a successful fetch cacheSet it. Reuses the exact 12h key the other two routes populate.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 cash (eBay Browse is free, rate-limited); materially reduces eBay quota burn and rate-limit/IP-block risk at scale _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — generateEbayPriceCacheKey, cacheGet/cacheSet already exist and are used by sibling routes.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/con-mode-lookup/route.ts:172-199; contrast src/app/api/ebay-prices/route.ts:96-141 and src/app/api/comics/[id]/refresh-value/route.ts:88-111

#### [H-PR2] Durable comic_metadata price cache has no freshness/TTL — stale prices served indefinitely
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** con-mode-lookup persists eBay price_data into comic_metadata and on later lookups returns that stored price_data whenever priceSource==='ebay', with no age check.
- **2. Issue identified:** There is no staleness guard: once a comic's price_data is saved it is returned indefinitely from the DB (disclaimer 'Based on current eBay listings'). The Redis layer expires (12h) but the DB layer is checked FIRST and never expires, so the DB short-circuits before any fresh fetch can happen. getComicMetadata already returns updatedAt but it is unused.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add an updated_at age check in the DB-hit branch: if comic_metadata.updatedAt is older than e.g. 14-30 days, fall through to a fresh eBay fetch (and re-save) instead of returning the stale row.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — bounded refresh cadence trades a few extra eBay calls for accuracy; net negligible _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — updatedAt is already surfaced by getComicMetadata; pure logic addition.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/con-mode-lookup/route.ts:104-156; src/lib/db.ts:547-581

#### [H-PR3] No rate-limit guard on ebay-prices, cert-lookup (both guest-reachable) or refresh-value
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · security · effort S
- **1. Description:** con-mode-lookup rate-limits correctly. The three other outbound-call endpoints in this scope do not: POST/GET /api/ebay-prices (eBay Browse API), POST /api/cert-lookup (CGC/CBCS/PGX web scraping), and POST /api/comics/[id]/refresh-value (eBay).
- **2. Issue identified:** /api/ebay-prices (POST+GET) and /api/cert-lookup have no auth and no checkRateLimit — a script varying title/issue/grade forces uncached calls that can burn the free eBay Browse quota and trigger high volumes of outbound CGC/CBCS/PGX scraping (risking IP blocks / Cloudflare challenges; and if ZENROWS_API_KEY is later activated, paid-proxy abuse). refresh-value is auth-gated but a signed-in user can still spam it across comic IDs with no rate limit.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Wrap each handler with checkRateLimit(rateLimiters.lookup, getRateLimitIdentifier(null, ip)) exactly as con-mode-lookup does. Cert-lookup especially benefits; optionally also cache negative cert-lookup results.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 cash; protects free eBay quota, reduces scraping-ban risk, and (if ZenRows activated post-launch) avoids paid-proxy abuse _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — rateLimit helpers already exist and are used by con-mode-lookup.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/ebay-prices/route.ts:71-127; src/app/api/cert-lookup/route.ts:20-27; src/app/api/comics/[id]/refresh-value/route.ts:25-42; contrast con-mode-lookup/route.ts:83-92

#### [H-PR4] cover-candidates Haiku endpoint has no auth and no rate limiter
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** cover-candidates/route.ts calls Anthropic Haiku (generateSearchQuery) to build a search string and guards only via input validation.
- **2. Issue identified:** Verified: cover-candidates/route.ts has NO auth() check AND NO checkRateLimit — it is a fully unauthenticated, unbounded endpoint that invokes Anthropic Haiku and increments a Redis usage counter on every call. By contrast the analyze route both rate-limits and auths. An attacker can spam this endpoint freely; each call is ~$0.0008 plus 2 Upstash commands, which compounds under abuse.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add auth() gating (or hCaptcha for guests) AND wire rateLimiters.api (or a new 'cover' limiter) + checkRateLimit keyed via getRateLimitIdentifier before the Anthropic call. Audit other AI-touching routes for the same gap.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Anthropic Haiku spend (~$0.0008/call) + Upstash commands by capping unauthenticated abuse _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — rateLimit and auth helpers already exist in the codebase.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/cover-candidates/route.ts:49-69,75-114; contrast src/app/api/analyze/route.ts:182-195

#### [H-SC1] Reorder slab detection to run only when the main analysis flags a slab, eliminating one vision call per raw scan
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** On every cache-miss scan, the non-cert branch first calls executeSlabDetection (a full vision AI call) before the main imageAnalysis call. For raw (unslabbed) comics — the majority of scans — slab detection returns isSlabbed=false and is immediately followed by the full imageAnalysis call.
- **2. Issue identified:** Raw-comic scans make TWO vision calls: slab-detection + imageAnalysis. The main IMAGE_ANALYSIS_PROMPT already returns isSlabbed, gradingCompany, and certificationNumber, so slab info is fully recoverable from the single call that runs anyway. The cheap detector taxes every scan but only benefits graded books.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Run imageAnalysis first (it already detects slab + cert + grade), and only branch into the cert-lookup / slab-detail-extraction path when imageResult.isSlabbed is true. This removes one vision call from every raw scan while preserving cert-first benefit for graded books.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves ~$0.0003-0.003 per raw scan; at 10K scans/mo mostly-Gemini that is roughly a few dollars to low-tens of dollars/mo; meaningfully more if Anthropic is primary _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Code change is in-repo, but reordering the cert-first vs full pipeline is a behavior change that warrants product sign-off and re-testing of the graded-book cert-first path.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/analyze/route.ts:384-397, :618-635; src/lib/providers/anthropic.ts:95-98

#### [H-SC2] Variant resolver is non-functional in production because the first-pass AI rarely returns the 5-digit UPC add-on
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · correctness · effort L
- **1. Description:** resolveVariant only runs when comicDetails.barcode.parsed.addonVariant is present. Per documented Session 46 production testing, the compressed 1200px image fed to the AI consistently yields only the 12-digit main UPC, so addonVariant is null and the entire 3-tier variant resolver never fires — the headline variant feature is dead end-to-end in production.
- **2. Issue identified:** Engaged multi-variant collectors (the target user) see all variants of an issue stored as identical rows; re-scanning reproduces the null result and erodes trust. The Haiku enrichment cost noted in CLAUDE.md is also moot until this is fixed. Related dead artifact: src/types/barcode-detector.d.ts declares the BarcodeDetector Web API but is referenced nowhere outside the declaration file.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Implement BACKLOG option D first: extract the full 17-digit UPC client-side at full camera resolution with a JS barcode library before compression, and send it as a structured field so the AI never has to OCR digits. Fall back to option B (server-side Haiku second-pass on a bottom-left crop) only if the library spike fails. Add a manual barcode-edit affordance as the final fallback. If option D ships it consumes the dead barcode-detector.d.ts type; otherwise delete that file.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Option D: $0 added AI cost and fixes the feature; option B: adds ~$0.0008/scan only when first pass misses the add-on _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** No — Launch-class product feature requiring a library-accuracy spike, a payload/flow architecture decision (D vs B), bundle-size acceptance, and device testing.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/analyze/route.ts:738; src/lib/variantResolver.ts:107-112; src/types/barcode-detector.d.ts:1-23; BACKLOG.md variant-resolver section

#### [H-SC3] Guest scan limit + captcha threshold enforced via client-controlled header (bypassable, uncapped AI spend)
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · security · effort M
- **1. Description:** For guests, the only gate on the expensive AI scan endpoint is the x-guest-scan-count request header plus IP rate limiting. The count is set by the client from localStorage, and the code comment notes 'This is validated client-side'. Both the 5-scan cap and the hCaptcha trigger (threshold=3) read this header.
- **2. Issue identified:** A guest can send x-guest-scan-count: 0 on every request to get unlimited free Sonnet vision scans (~$0.015 each) AND never trigger the captcha. The only server backstop is the IP rate limit (5/min), which still allows ~7,200 scans/day/IP. There is no server-side per-IP/per-guest scan counter in Redis, so abuse directly burns Anthropic/Gemini credits.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Track guest scan counts server-side keyed by a hard-to-rotate identifier (IP, or a server-issued signed guest cookie, optionally + lightweight fingerprint) in Redis with a monthly TTL, and enforce GUEST_SCAN_LIMIT and the captcha threshold against the stored value, treating the client header only as a hint.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves uncapped AI spend abuse — worst case up to ~$100/day per abusing IP; bounds the free-scan cost ceiling _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Server-side guest tracking needs an identifier-strategy decision (cookie vs IP+fingerprint) and Upstash key design; the Redis-keyed core is implementable in-repo once that is decided.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/analyze/route.ts:238-251, :275-280, :186-187; src/app/scan/page.tsx:184

#### [H-SC5] Route text-only AI lookups to Haiku instead of Sonnet
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** Three server routes call Anthropic for pure text completions (no image) using MODEL_PRIMARY (Claude Sonnet 4.5).
- **2. Issue identified:** con-mode-lookup (fetchKeyInfoFromAI), comic-lookup (two calls), and import-lookup all send text-only prompts to Sonnet 4.5 (~$3/$15 per Mtok). These tasks — listing key facts and filling publisher/year/creators from a known title+issue — do not need Sonnet-grade reasoning, and import-lookup fires once per CSV row, so a 200-row import is 200 Sonnet text calls. Haiku 4.5 (MODEL_LIGHTWEIGHT, already used elsewhere) is materially cheaper.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Switch these calls from MODEL_PRIMARY to MODEL_LIGHTWEIGHT (Haiku 4.5). titles/suggest, cover-candidates, cron/moderate-messages and variantResolver already use MODEL_LIGHTWEIGHT. Optionally keep Sonnet as a low-confidence retry only.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves ~60-70% of the per-call AI text cost on these routes; a 200-row import drops from ~$0.04 (Sonnet) to ~$0.013 (Haiku). Small at beta volume; material once CSV imports and Con Mode scale _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — One-line model swap per route plus a regression test; no external step.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/con-mode-lookup/route.ts:408; src/app/api/comic-lookup/route.ts:195,251; src/app/api/import-lookup/route.ts:73; precedent: src/app/api/titles/suggest/route.ts:51; src/lib/models.ts:10

#### [H-SC6] Convert user-uploaded covers to WebP server-side to cut storage cost
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** The upload-cover API route stores the user's cover photo in the comic-covers bucket exactly as received (original JPEG contentType), while the separate harvest path already uses sharp to emit WebP at quality 85.
- **2. Issue identified:** upload-cover/route.ts uploads the raw arrayBuffer with the original contentType (bucket 'comic-covers'). CLAUDE.md flags comic-covers (~400KB/photo) as the primary risk to the Supabase free 1GB cap. The client compresses to ~400KB JPEG, but the server never re-encodes to WebP, leaving ~25-35% storage savings on the table. The harvest WebP path writes to a DIFFERENT bucket ('cover-images'), so user uploads get zero re-encoding.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** In upload-cover/route.ts run the buffer through sharp().webp({ quality: 82 }).toBuffer(), upload with contentType image/webp and a .webp filename. Shrinks every stored user cover ~25-35% and normalizes formats.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Supabase storage; delays Pro upgrade ($25/mo) as cover uploads scale _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/comics/upload-cover/route.ts:42-50; src/lib/coverHarvest.ts:204

#### [H-SO1] Remove dead/double-counting increment_field RPC calls in reputation code
- **Area / Dimension:** social · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** creatorCreditsDb.ts calls a Postgres RPC named increment_field to bump positive_ratings, negative_ratings, and community_contribution_count after inserting/updating feedback and contributions.
- **2. Issue identified:** increment_field is never defined in any tracked migration. Meanwhile DB triggers trigger_update_feedback_ratings and trigger_update_contribution_count ALREADY maintain those exact columns automatically. If the RPC exists out-of-band, every rating/contribution is DOUBLE-counted. If it does not exist, the four .rpc() calls error out and are silently swallowed (the result error is never checked) — the manual code is dead and misleading. Compounding: subscription.ts passes param 'amount' while creatorCreditsDb passes 'increment_value' for the same RPC name — conflicting signatures.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Delete the four increment_field RPC calls in creatorCreditsDb.ts (lines 201, 262, 269, 750) since the DB triggers already keep counts correct. Verify against the live DB whether counts are currently inflated; if so, run a one-time reconciliation UPDATE from COUNT(*).
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — removes redundant Supabase round-trips _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Code deletion is autonomous; verifying live-DB count integrity and running any reconciliation needs DB access / human confirmation.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/creatorCreditsDb.ts:201,262,269,750; supabase/migrations/20260130_reputation_system.sql:83-106, :66-81; src/lib/subscription.ts:294-299; no CREATE FUNCTION increment_field anywhere (grep)

#### [H-SO2] Replace naive substring profanity match that blocks innocent usernames
- **Area / Dimension:** social · ux · effort M
- **1. Description:** usernameValidation.isProfane/containsProfanity flags a username if any blocklist word is a substring (.includes) of the name or its leetspeak-normalized form.
- **2. Issue identified:** Substring matching produces large numbers of false positives. Empirically verified by running the live PROFANITY_LIST: 'classic', 'grass', 'bassist' blocked by 'ass'; 'shell' by 'hell'; 'essex'/'sussex' by 'sex'; 'title'/'competitor' by 'tit'; 'peacock'/'cockpit' by 'cock'; 'analysis'/'canal' by 'anal'; 'homophone' by 'homo'; 'gaylord' by 'gay'. Comic-relevant handles like 'classic_comics' are also rejected, all with a generic 'inappropriate content' error.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Use word-boundary / whole-token matching (split on underscores/digits, match exact tokens, plus optional leet-normalized token comparison) instead of raw .includes(). Keep a short always-block list for true slurs but stop substring-matching short fragments. Add unit tests asserting the innocent examples are allowed.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — pure logic change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/usernameValidation.ts:182-185, :190-211; PROFANITY_LIST entries at :9-76. Confirmed empirically: 'classic','classic_comics','analysis' all BLOCKED.

### 🟡 Medium Priority

#### [M-AD1] Confidential CLZ sales playbook reachable by any logged-in user
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · security · effort S
- **1. Description:** The /admin/clz-comparison page is a fully static internal competitive sales playbook with verbatim talking points.
- **2. Issue identified:** Middleware only enforces auth.protect() (authentication) for /admin(.*), never is_admin. The page is "use client" with ZERO interactivity guard — grep for useState/useEffect/fetch/useUser returns 0 across all 512 lines, so there is no fetch-403 fallback. Any signed-in free/guest-upgraded user who navigates to /admin/clz-comparison sees the full confidential playbook.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Gate server-side: convert to a server component that calls getAdminProfile() and redirect()s non-admins, or wrap content in an admin-status check. Best paired with the layout-level fix below.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Code is doable in-repo, but converting a static client page to server-gating is a structural change worth a quick confirmation.
- **Evidence:** src/app/admin/clz-comparison/page.tsx:1, :13-37; src/middleware.ts:20-24

#### [M-AD2] No server-side admin-role gate on admin pages (defense-in-depth)
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · security · effort M
- **1. Description:** Admin pages under /admin/* rely on their client-side data fetches receiving a 403 from the API to discover the user isn't an admin. The API routes are the real security boundary, but the page shells render for any authenticated user until the fetch resolves.
- **2. Issue identified:** Middleware only calls auth.protect() — it verifies authentication, never is_admin. So a non-admin can load any /admin/* shell, briefly see loading/empty UI, and any fully static content is exposed entirely. Gating is ad-hoc per page and absent on static pages. No single enforced server gate exists.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add admin-role enforcement once in src/app/admin/layout.tsx as a server component awaiting getAdminProfile() and redirect()ing non-admins, OR extend middleware to check is_admin for /admin paths.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — layout.tsx is currently a "use client" component; converting to a server gate or splitting into a server wrapper is a structural change worth confirming.
- **Evidence:** src/middleware.ts:20-24; src/app/admin/layout.tsx:1; fetch-403 reliance at src/app/admin/users/page.tsx:165

#### [M-AD3] Scan cost metric overcounts by using comics.created_at instead of scan_analytics
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** The 'Estimated API Cost (30 days)' usage metric and both alert paths estimate Anthropic spend as (count of comics rows created in 30d) × $0.015.
- **2. Issue identified:** comics rows are created by many non-scan paths (manual adds, bulk-add, undo-delete re-inserts), so counting comics overstates AI scan volume, and the flat $0.015 ignores that Gemini-fallback scans are far cheaper. Meanwhile the accurate, provider-aware source (scan_analytics.estimated_cost_cents) already exists and is correctly used by getScanAnalytics in the SAME file. Result: inflated cost numbers and false-positive cost alerts.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace the comics-count proxy with a sum of scan_analytics.estimated_cost_cents over the window (or the existing get_avg_scan_cost RPC × scan count). Apply in all three places: usage/route.ts, check-alerts/route.ts §3, and alert-status/route.ts §3.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 direct; prevents false cost alerts that waste Resend emails and admin attention, and improves cost-tracking accuracy _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — scan_analytics table and get_avg_scan_cost RPC already exist and are used in the same file.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/admin/usage/route.ts:281-306; check-alerts/route.ts:115-120; alert-status/route.ts:103-108; accurate source at usage/route.ts:74-98

#### [M-AD4] Cover-haiku cost aggregation does 30 sequential Redis GETs per usage/alert call
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** Both the usage dashboard route and the check-alerts cron sum Cover Search (Haiku) cost over 30 days by looping i=0..29 and issuing one redis.get per day.
- **2. Issue identified:** Each call fires 30 sequential Redis GET commands (the usage route also adds an incr + expire). On the Upstash free tier (10K commands/day) this is wasteful and sequential GETs add latency. The usage route has no caching, so every admin dashboard load multiplies the command count.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace the 30-iteration loop with a single redis.mget(keys) (one round-trip) in both files, or maintain a rolling 30-day counter updated at write time.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Upstash commands — ~30 GETs/call collapsed to 1; meaningful against the 10K/day free cap _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — redis.mget is a drop-in.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/admin/usage/route.ts:318-326; src/app/api/admin/usage/check-alerts/route.ts:151-159

#### [M-AD5] Heavy usage dashboard route has no caching and writes Redis on every load
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** GET /api/admin/usage aggregates everything: 3 Supabase count queries, get_database_size RPC, get_avg_scan_cost RPC, a full scan_analytics fetch, 30 Redis GETs, a Redis incr+expire, and a live Netlify bandwidth API call with a 5s timeout.
- **2. Issue identified:** There is no caching (unlike alert-status which caches 5 min). Every dashboard view re-runs all of it, including the blocking external Netlify fetch. It also performs a redis.incr on usage:commands every load, polluting the very Upstash metric the dashboard measures.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Wrap the assembled metrics payload in a short TTL cache (60-120s) using the cacheGet/cacheSet helpers alert-status already uses, and remove the self-incrementing redis.incr from this read endpoint.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Supabase/Upstash/Netlify-API calls per dashboard view; modest, free, reduces pressure on the Upstash 10K/day cap _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — cacheGet/cacheSet helpers exist and are already used by alert-status.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/admin/usage/route.ts:136-407, 249-250, 350-356; contrast alert-status/route.ts:56-59,129

#### [M-AD6] State-mutating admin actions are not written to the audit log
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · security · effort M
- **1. Description:** admin_audit_log exists 'for accountability' and is written for user-management actions only.
- **2. Issue identified:** logAdminAction is called ONLY in users/* and message-reports/* routes. Several content-mutating admin routes perform writes with NO logAdminAction: barcode-reviews PATCH (approve/correct/reject — also DELETES catalog rows), cover-queue PATCH (approval awards Creator Credits to users), and key-comics POST/PATCH/DELETE. The trail is asymmetric: read-only views are logged, real mutations are not.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add logAdminAction calls to all mutating admin routes. Extend the AdminAction union in adminAuth.ts (the DB action column is free TEXT — no migration needed) with actions like approve_barcode, reject_barcode, approve_cover, edit_key_comic, delete_key_comic.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — admin_audit_log.action is TEXT (no enum), so only the TS union + call sites change.
- **Evidence:** logAdminAction present only in users/* and message-reports/* (grep); absent in barcode-reviews/route.ts:127-199, cover-queue/route.ts:49-86, key-comics/route.ts:25,56

#### [M-AD7] Consolidate duplicated inline isAdmin() helpers and remove unused adminAuth exports
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** adminAuth.ts exports isAdmin(profileId), isAdminByClerkId(clerkUserId), getAdminProfile(), and requireAdmin(). Two admin API routes nevertheless re-implement their own local async isAdmin(clerkUserId).
- **2. Issue identified:** isAdminByClerkId and requireAdmin have zero importers anywhere in src/, and isAdmin(profileId) is effectively unused by routes. Meanwhile flagged-users/route.ts and barcode-reviews/route.ts each define a local async isAdmin(clerkUserId) functionally identical to the shared isAdminByClerkId, and use auth()+isAdmin() instead of getAdminProfile(). barcode-reviews PATCH then does a THIRD redundant profiles lookup to fetch profile.id that getAdminProfile() already returns.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace the local isAdmin helpers with getAdminProfile() (returns id, clerk_user_id, is_admin), removing the redundant 3rd lookup. After consolidation, delete isAdmin(profileId), isAdminByClerkId, and requireAdmin if still unused.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves one extra Supabase query per barcode-review PATCH; negligible but free _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — getAdminProfile already exists and returns the needed id.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/adminAuth.ts:31-53, :58, :75-87, :116; flagged-users/route.ts:16-23; barcode-reviews/route.ts:28-36, :104-108

#### [M-AD8] Community Variant-Name Approval Queue admin UI/route does not exist
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · cost · effort L
- **1. Description:** The 3-tier variant resolver writes user-typed variant names to barcode_catalog with variant_name_status='pending'. Per design these stay invisible to Tier-1 (free catalog) lookups until an admin approves them. BACKLOG.md specs an admin queue + PATCH /api/admin/variant-names/:id.
- **2. Issue identified:** No such admin page or route exists in src/app/admin/* or src/app/api/admin/* (confirmed by grep). Until it ships, pending community variant names never get promoted to Tier 1, so every variant scan for those books keeps falling through to the paid Tier-2 Haiku enrichment (~$0.0008/call). The crowd-sourced catalog can only grow via manual SQL.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Build the admin queue page (list barcode_catalog rows where variant_name_status='pending' AND variant_name IS NOT NULL, using the existing idx_barcode_catalog_variant_pending index) plus PATCH /api/admin/variant-names/:id with admin-auth + audit logging and Approve/Reject/Edit+Approve actions.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves recurring Anthropic Haiku spend (~$0.0008/variant scan, ~$0.02-0.07/day at beta, rising after the barcode-OCR fix) by promoting approved names to free Tier-1 catalog lookups _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Fully buildable in-repo, but it is a new feature gated by the global CLAUDE.md brainstorm/plan/approval flow.
- **Evidence:** BACKLOG.md:313-336; confirmed absent by grep; index from supabase/migrations/20260509_variant_name_catalog_guard.sql

#### [M-AU6] Replace .single() with .maybeSingle() on zero-row-expected lookups
- **Area / Dimension:** auctions · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** Many helpers use .single() on queries that legitimately return zero rows; Supabase returns a PGRST116 error in that case instead of null.
- **2. Issue identified:** hasActiveListing uses .single() to check if a comic has a listing — the common 'no listing' case returns a PGRST116 error, but the code only reads `data` and ignores the error, so it coincidentally works (fragile). expireListings's notification check uses .single() similarly. There are 39 .single() vs only 5 .maybeSingle() calls in this file; the sibling by-comic route already does this correctly.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Audit the 39 .single() calls and switch every 'might-not-exist' lookup to .maybeSingle(); reserve .single() for queries guaranteed to return exactly one row.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/auctionDb.ts:93-103, :2864-2869; contrast src/app/api/auctions/by-comic/[comicId]/route.ts:28; 39 .single() vs 5 .maybeSingle()

#### [M-AU7] Cap unbounded batch sizes in processEndedAuctions and cron candidate fetches
- **Area / Dimension:** auctions · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** processEndedAuctions selects ALL active auctions past end_time with no limit and processes them serially with per-auction queries.
- **2. Issue identified:** processEndedAuctions does select('*') of every ended auction with no limit, then for each one runs a winning-bid query and downstream notification/email work in a serial await loop. If the cron lags (the code comment notes an observed ~3h42m lag) a backlog of ended auctions all process in one tick and the per-auction round-trips multiply, risking partial completion on a Netlify function timeout.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add a LIMIT (e.g. 100) to the candidate select so each tick drains a bounded batch. Consider bounded-concurrency processing (mapWithConcurrency) for the per-auction work.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change (reduces risk of Netlify function timeout) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/auctionDb.ts:2388-2392, :2398-2406

#### [M-AU8] Shop feed over-fetches full comic rows with comics(*)
- **Area / Dimension:** auctions · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** getActiveAuctions (the Shop/marketplace grid) selects auctions with joined comics!auctions_comic_id_fkey(*), pulling every comic column for up to 50 listings per page, plus a count:exact total.
- **2. Issue identified:** The listing grid needs only a handful of comic fields but fetches all columns including heavy fields like price_data JSONB and notes, inflating Supabase egress on the most public, highest-traffic feed. The transactions route already uses a lean column list (LISTING_SELECT).
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace comics(*) with an explicit column list matching what the card renders. Consider whether count:exact is needed on every page or only the first.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 direct; saves Supabase egress on the busiest feed, increasingly valuable as price_data JSONB grows _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/auctionDb.ts:417-423; lean pattern at src/app/api/transactions/route.ts:91-97

#### [M-AU9] Decompose the 4,594-line auctionDb.ts god-module along its existing section boundaries
- **Area / Dimension:** auctions · tech-debt · effort L
- **1. Description:** src/lib/auctionDb.ts is the largest source file at 4,594 lines, with 47 exported functions, holding the entire marketplace data layer.
- **2. Issue identified:** The file mixes ~47 exported functions across at least 8 distinct concerns and already contains clear banner-comment section dividers. A 4.6K-line module is hard to navigate (both the strike-count bug and the placeBid race hid here), slows editor/typecheck, and forces test files to import the whole DB-coupled module.

Recommended optimization:** Split into focused modules under src/lib/auction/ using the existing section dividers as cut lines: auctions.ts, bidding.ts, offers.ts, watchlist.ts, notifications.ts, ratings.ts, cronJobs.ts, secondChance.ts — re-exported from an index. Mechanical move, no logic change.

#### [M-CO8] Memoize ComicCard with React.memo and stabilize parent handlers to stop full grid re-renders
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** ComicCard is the grid-view cell rendered via filteredCollection.map() in the collection page. It is exported as a plain function with no React.memo.
- **2. Issue identified:** Because the parent recomputes filteredCollection every render and passes fresh inline handler props, every grid card re-renders on any parent state change (search keystroke, selection toggle, modal open). For a large collection this is hundreds of needless re-renders per interaction.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Wrap ComicCard in React.memo and stabilize the parent's handler props with useCallback. Combined with memoizing filteredCollection, this cuts grid re-render cost dramatically.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — pure client-side perf improvement _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Requires also stabilizing parent handler props with useCallback for memo to be effective.
- **Evidence:** src/components/ComicCard.tsx:21; src/app/collection/page.tsx:1169-1180

#### [M-CO9] Full collection fetched with SELECT * and no pagination
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · database · effort M
- **1. Description:** getUserComics loads the entire collection in one query for the collection page.
- **2. Issue identified:** The query is select("*, comic_lists(list_id)") with no range/limit. Every column is pulled including the large price_data JSON blob and key_info, even though the grid/list only need title, issue, publisher, cover URL, grade, and a single estimated value. Payload grows unbounded with collection size on every load/refresh.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Select only the columns the grid/list render (trim price_data to estimatedValue if feasible) and add keyset/range pagination; keep the heavy price_data fetch lazy in the detail modal.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Supabase egress/bandwidth at scale; negligible today ($0 now) _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Column trimming is autonomous; pagination changes the UX contract and warrants a quick product sign-off.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/db.ts:102-113, :675

#### [M-CO10] Collection load orders by unindexed date_added column
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · database · effort S
- **1. Description:** getUserComics and getPublicComics filter by user_id and order by date_added DESC, but the existing performance index is on (user_id, created_at DESC); date_added has no index.
- **2. Issue identified:** The comics table has both created_at and date_added. idx_comics_user_created targets created_at, while the collection queries sort on date_added, so Postgres performs a separate sort step after the user_id filter. getUserComics also filters deleted_at IS NULL AND sold_at IS NULL, which the existing non-partial index does not cover.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add CREATE INDEX idx_comics_user_date_added ON comics(user_id, date_added DESC) (optionally partial on deleted_at IS NULL), or change ORDER BY to created_at to reuse the existing index. Confirm semantics first.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 direct; saves Supabase compute on collection loads at scale _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Adding the index requires a Supabase migration; the ORDER BY swap is autonomous but changes user-visible sort semantics.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/db.ts:113, :819; index at supabase/migrations/20260121_performance_indexes.sql:15; date_added unindexed (supabase/schema.sql:70)

#### [M-CO11] CSV import inserts comics one-by-one in a sequential await loop
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** After CSV parsing, every imported comic is saved individually.
- **2. Issue identified:** onImportComplete runs `for (const item of items) { await addToCollection(item); }` — each iteration is a separate Supabase insert plus a separate comic_lists insert inside addComic. A large import becomes hundreds-to-thousands of serialized round-trips with no transaction and risk of partial failure, despite bulk endpoints already existing.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add a /api/comics/bulk-import route that validates ownership/limits server-side and does chunked supabase.from('comics').insert(rows) plus one batched comic_lists insert. Replace the client for-await loop with one call.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change (fewer round-trips marginally reduces Supabase request volume) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/scan/page.tsx:808-812; src/lib/db.ts:121-164

#### [M-CO12] migrateLocalDataToCloud does serial N+1 inserts per list, comic, and sale
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** The localStorage-to-cloud migration loops over each custom list, each comic, and each sale, awaiting an individual insert per item on guest signup.
- **2. Issue identified:** A per-custom-list insert loop, a per-comic loop (one comics insert + one comic_lists insert sequentially), and a per-sale insert loop, each making one Supabase round-trip at a time. A user migrating 200 comics fires 400+ serialized round-trips, slow and risks Netlify function timeout. Current impact is low because guests are scan-capped, but it scales poorly if limits rise.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Convert the independent loops (sales, custom lists) to single insert([...]) calls. For comics, batch-insert with returning, then build all comic_lists rows in one follow-up insert. mapWithConcurrency is available if bounded-parallel is preferred.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — fewer Supabase round-trips, lower timeout risk _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/db.ts:452-467, :470-487, :490-503

#### [M-CO13] Grid/list views render every comic with no virtualization
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · performance · effort L
- **1. Description:** The grid maps filteredCollection directly to ComicCard, and the list maps directly to row divs.
- **2. Issue identified:** filteredCollection.map renders one ComicCard (each with an image) or one table row per item with no windowing. A large collection mounts hundreds of DOM nodes and Image components at once, hurting first render, scroll, and mobile memory. With no React.memo on ComicCard, any state change re-renders the whole list.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Introduce list virtualization (e.g. @tanstack/react-virtual or react-window) for grid and list once collections exceed ~100 items, rendering only the visible window. Pairs with pagination.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Adds a dependency, and virtualizing the responsive pop-art CSS grid needs design care.
- **Evidence:** src/app/collection/page.tsx:1168-1181, :1194-1291

#### [M-CO14] Dynamically import heavy collection modals/forms (ComicDetailModal, ComicDetailsForm, ShareCollectionModal)
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · bundle · effort M
- **1. Description:** The collection page statically imports ComicDetailModal (1234 lines), ComicDetailsForm (1472 lines), and ShareCollectionModal (395 lines), but each renders only conditionally.
- **2. Issue identified:** These ~3100 lines of modal/form JS are bundled into the collection page's initial chunk even though most users never open them on first paint. The next/dynamic pattern is already proven in the repo (profile page) but is used in only one place.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Convert ComicDetailModal, ComicDetailsForm, and ShareCollectionModal to next/dynamic imports (ssr:false, lightweight loading fallback) in collection/page.tsx so their code downloads only when the user actually opens them.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — reduces initial JS download for the collection route _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pattern already used in profile/[[...profile]]/page.tsx.
- **Evidence:** src/app/collection/page.tsx:48-53, :1296-1311, :1314-1357, :1360; src/app/profile/[[...profile]]/page.tsx:3,9

#### [M-CO15] ComicDetailsForm (1472) and ComicDetailModal (1234) are oversized monolith components
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · tech-debt · effort L
- **1. Description:** Two core collection components each exceed ~1200 lines in a single file.
- **2. Issue identified:** ComicDetailsForm bundles ~32 useState/useEffect declarations in one render scope mixing grading, pricing, cover handling, list management, and validation. ComicDetailModal similarly bundles seller actions, value-refresh, for-trade, listing lookup, and rendering. These monoliths re-render broadly, are hard to test, and raise regression risk.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Decompose into focused subcomponents (GradingFieldset, PricingFieldset, CoverEditor; SellerActions, ValueRefresh, ListMembership) with scoped state. No behavior change required.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/components/ComicDetailsForm.tsx (1472 lines, ~32 useState/useEffect); src/components/ComicDetailModal.tsx (1234 lines)

#### [M-CO16] Clean up orphaned covers in storage when a comic is hard-deleted
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** Hard-deleting a single comic removes the DB row but leaves its uploaded cover in the comic-covers bucket.
- **2. Issue identified:** The single-comic delete route hard-deletes the row with no storage.remove() call, so the associated cover file is orphaned permanently. (bulk-delete is a SOFT delete and does NOT orphan covers.) Over time each hard-deleted comic leaves a ~400KB object consuming the 1GB Supabase free quota with no referencing row. Community-harvested covers are shared and must be excluded from any cleanup.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** On single-comic hard delete, parse the storage path from cover_image_url when it points to the comic-covers bucket (and is user-uploaded, not shared) and call supabaseAdmin.storage.from('comic-covers').remove([...]). Optionally add a reconciliation cron.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves cumulative orphaned storage; prevents slow-leak growth toward the 1GB Supabase free cap and the $25/mo Pro upgrade. $0 today _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Code is doable in-repo, but must confirm bucket policy and that only user-uploaded covers are removed.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/comics/[id]/route.ts:75; src/app/api/comics/bulk-delete/route.ts:72

#### [M-CO17] Cover/image upload trusts client Content-Type and filename extension — stored-XSS risk
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · security · effort M
- **1. Description:** /api/comics/upload-cover and /api/messages/upload-image validate uploads only via file.type.startsWith('image/') and store the file with that same client-supplied contentType into public Supabase buckets.
- **2. Issue identified:** file.type is the browser/attacker-supplied Content-Type and is spoofable. An authenticated user can upload an SVG containing script as image/svg+xml, or an HTML/JS payload labeled image/png, into the public comic-covers / message-images buckets. The stored contentType is echoed from the client and the extension is derived from the attacker filename, so opening the public URL directly can execute script (stored XSS). No magic-byte sniffing is performed.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Sniff the actual file signature server-side and reject anything that isn't a real JPEG/PNG/WebP, then force a safe stored contentType + extension. sharp can re-encode/validate the image. Optionally serve uploads from a sandboxed storage-only domain.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — sharp is already in package.json and used in coverHarvest.ts.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/comics/upload-cover/route.ts:28-49; src/app/api/messages/upload-image/route.ts:29-54

#### [M-CO18] Test csvExport.collectionToCSV transform (untested export correctness)
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · testing · effort S
- **1. Description:** src/lib/csvExport.ts converts a user's CollectionItem[] into CSV for the export feature.
- **2. Issue identified:** grep for 'collectionToCSV' across test files returns nothing. collectionToCSV is a transform function — on the project's mandatory-test list — and is untested. The complementary parser IS tested, so import is covered but export is not. CSV-injection-prone fields (titles with commas/quotes/newlines) and money/grade formatting could regress silently.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add tests for collectionToCSV: correct header row, comma/quote/newline escaping in title fields, null/empty value handling, currency/grade formatting. generateExportFilename is a cheap date-format stability test.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pure transform; no mocks needed.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/csvExport.ts:32, :123; counterpart src/lib/__tests__/csvParsing.test.ts only tests parseCurrencyValue

#### [M-CO19] Add a visible keyboard focus indicator to collection filter controls
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · accessibility · effort S
- **1. Description:** Collection toolbar selects/inputs use focus:outline-none paired only with hover:shadow effects (or nothing), and globals.css has no global :focus-visible fallback that applies to these controls.
- **2. Issue identified:** 9 controls set focus:outline-none with no keyboard-triggered focus replacement. Keyboard users tabbing the collection sort/filter/list selects get no visible focus indicator — a WCAG 2.4.7 failure on a high-traffic surface. (The search input is fine — it adds focus:shadow.)
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Remove focus:outline-none on these controls, or add focus-visible:shadow/focus-visible:ring, or add a single global *:focus-visible outline rule in globals.css as a safety net.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — CSS/class change.
- **Evidence:** src/app/collection/page.tsx:870,916,959,972,986,1005,1474,1493,1513; globals.css only has .input-pop:focus (:350) and .select-pop:focus (:374)

#### [M-CO20] Make the search input and view-toggle buttons accessible (label + aria-pressed)
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · accessibility · effort S
- **1. Description:** The collection search field relies on a placeholder, and the grid/list view toggles are icon-only buttons that convey active state visually via title=.
- **2. Issue identified:** (1) The search input has only placeholder text and no aria-label/<label> — placeholder is not an accessible name and disappears on input. (2) The grid/list toggle buttons have no aria-pressed (aria-pressed count is 0 app-wide), so screen-reader users can't tell which view is active.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add aria-label="Search collection" to the search input and aria-pressed={viewMode === 'grid'} / aria-label="Grid view" (and list) to the toggle buttons. Apply the aria-pressed pattern to other toggles across the app.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Self-contained.
- **Evidence:** src/app/collection/page.tsx:819-825, :829-849; grep: 0 aria-pressed across src/

#### [M-CO21] Enlarge under-44px tap targets (SelectionCheckbox) and give them an accessible name
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · accessibility · effort S
- **1. Description:** SelectionCheckbox renders a 24x24px (w-6 h-6) toggle used for multi-select on every comic card.
- **2. Issue identified:** The 24x24px hit area is well below the 44x44px recommended minimum (WCAG 2.5.5 / Apple HIG), making bulk selection error-prone on mobile. The button has role="checkbox"/aria-checked but no accessible name (no aria-label).
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Increase the interactive area to at least 44x44px (e.g. add padding while keeping the visual box small) and add aria-label="Select comic". Audit other small icon buttons for the same minimum.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Self-contained.
- **Evidence:** src/components/collection/SelectionCheckbox.tsx:19, :24-25

#### [M-CO22] Cover upload route has no server-side compression even though sharp is available
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** /api/comics/upload-cover accepts an image up to 10MB and writes the unmodified buffer straight to the comic-covers Supabase bucket; sharp is a project dependency but imported only in coverHarvest.ts.
- **2. Issue identified:** Distinct from the WebP-conversion finding, the broader concern is the absence of any dimension cap. The route accepts files up to MAX_IMAGE_UPLOAD_BYTES (10MB) and uploads the raw buffer. Scan-flow photos are client-compressed, but any future caller posting a large image stores the full file with no resize ceiling.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Resize uploaded buffers to ~1200px max edge via sharp before storage.upload. Combine with the WebP conversion recommendation for one transform step.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Supabase storage growth; delays/avoids Supabase Pro ($25/mo) by keeping the cover bucket under the 1GB cap _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/comics/upload-cover/route.ts:42-50; src/lib/uploadLimits.ts:11; sharp imported only in src/lib/coverHarvest.ts:1

#### [M-CC9] Clerk webhook profile upsert/update ignore returned Supabase errors
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** The Clerk webhook handler wraps the profiles upsert (user.created) and update (user.updated) in try/catch but the Supabase client returns errors rather than throwing, so the catch never fires; the handler always returns 200 received:true.
- **2. Issue identified:** supabase.from('profiles').upsert(...)/.update(...) return { error } rather than throwing. The try/catch only catches network-level exceptions, not Supabase row-level errors. A failed upsert/update returns success to Clerk, which then will NOT retry (svix retries only on non-2xx), so a new social-OAuth user could end up with no profile row (or stale email).
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Destructure { error } from the upsert/update calls and, if error is set, log it and return a 500 so Clerk's webhook retry kicks in.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — reliability fix _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/webhooks/clerk/route.ts:98-112, :150-156

#### [M-CC10] Deduplicate Upstash Redis client instantiation across modules
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** Each module that needs Redis constructs its own new Redis() / Redis.fromEnv() client instead of sharing one.
- **2. Issue identified:** There are 8 separate Redis constructions. They fragment config and the env-presence guard is inconsistent: cache.ts/rateLimit.ts gracefully degrade to null when env is missing, but cover-candidates uses Redis.fromEnv() at module top-level which THROWS at import if env vars are absent.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Export a single shared `redis` instance (and `isRedisAvailable()`) from one module (e.g. src/lib/redis.ts) and import it everywhere. This also removes the cover-candidates import-time throw risk.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change (maintainability + robustness win) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/cache.ts:5-11; src/lib/rateLimit.ts:7-13; src/app/api/cover-candidates/route.ts:27; src/app/api/admin/usage/route.ts:236,313

#### [M-CC11] supabaseAdmin silently falls back to anon client when service key is missing
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · security · effort S
- **1. Description:** supabaseAdmin falls back to the RLS-bound anon client when SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY is absent, only emitting a console.warn.
- **2. Issue identified:** src/lib/supabase.ts:5 defaults the service key to "", and lines 13-21 only console.warn before constructing the client. If the service key is missing at runtime, admin operations across the app silently run as anon and behave unexpectedly under RLS rather than failing loudly. The warning fires server-side only and is easily missed.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** In production (NODE_ENV === 'production' && typeof window === 'undefined'), throw on missing service key instead of falling back, so a misconfigured deploy fails fast at boot.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — fail-fast safety _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/supabase.ts:5, :13-21

#### [M-CC12] Redundant dual session recording: PostHog recording + Sentry Replay both enabled
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** PostHogProvider enables session recording (disable_session_recording: false) and sentry.client.config.ts enables Sentry Session Replay (replaysSessionSampleRate 0.1, replaysOnErrorSampleRate 1.0). Both initialize rrweb-style DOM recorders.
- **2. Issue identified:** Two DOM recorders is redundant client overhead. PostHog session recordings are the single largest event-volume consumer, so they can erode the 1M-events/mo free tier as traffic grows. The actual PostHog capture rate is governed by dashboard sampling settings, so the precise burn depends on dashboard config.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Pick one recorder. Recommended: keep PostHog with an explicit sampled session_recording config (or restrict to conversion paths), and disable Sentry Replay (drop replaysSessionSampleRate, optionally keep replaysOnErrorSampleRate).
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves PostHog event-quota burn (session recording is the top event consumer; protects the 1M/mo free tier) and reduces Sentry replay quota; defers paid tiers _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Code change is autonomous; deciding which recorder to keep and at what sample rate is a product/analytics decision.
- **Evidence:** src/components/PostHogProvider.tsx:32-36; sentry.client.config.ts:10-11

#### [M-CC13] Disable Upstash Ratelimit analytics on all limiters
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** All four Upstash rate limiters are configured with analytics: true.
- **2. Issue identified:** Ratelimit.analytics writes per-window usage data into an extra Redis sorted set on each limit check, increasing the Upstash command count per rate-limited request. With the free tier capped at 10K commands/day, the analytics writes are overhead the app does not consume anywhere (no dashboard reads them).
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Set analytics: false on all four limiters (analyze, api, bidding, lookup). Re-enable selectively only if a rate-limit dashboard is later built.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves ~1+ Upstash command per rate-limited request across all four limiters; delays any paid Upstash plan. $0 today _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Flip a boolean on four limiter configs.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/rateLimit.ts:22,32,42,52

#### [M-CC14] Remove unused barcode/AI dependencies (@zxing/browser, @zxing/library, html5-qrcode, openai)
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · dependencies · effort S
- **1. Description:** package.json declares three barcode/QR scanning libraries and the openai SDK as runtime dependencies, but barcode scanning is implemented entirely with the browser-native BarcodeDetector Web API and the AI provider layer is Anthropic + Gemini only.
- **2. Issue identified:** grep across src/ and scripts/ for zxing/html5-qrcode/openai returns ZERO references. The only barcode code is a native-API type shim. src/lib/providers/ contains anthropic.ts, gemini.ts, types.ts — no openai. The four libs occupy ~42MB in node_modules (@zxing 26M, openai 13M, html5-qrcode 3.3M), slowing installs/CI and bloating the audit surface.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Run npm uninstall @zxing/browser @zxing/library html5-qrcode openai and commit the regenerated lockfile. No code changes needed.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no recurring cost change; reduces node_modules ~42MB, install size, CI time, and npm-audit surface _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Verified unused via grep across src/ and scripts/.
- **Evidence:** package.json:39-40,42,45; grep returns zero import references; src/types/barcode-detector.d.ts:15; du: @zxing=26M, openai=13M, html5-qrcode=3.3M

#### [M-CC15] Prune unused exports flagged by knip (csvExport, certLookup, imageOptimization, subscription helpers)
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · code-quality · effort M
- **1. Description:** Multiple lib modules export internal helpers that no caller imports, inflating the public surface.
- **2. Issue identified:** csvExport.ts exports collectionToCSV/downloadCSV/generateExportFilename but only exportCollectionToCSV is imported; certLookup.ts exports lookupCGCCert/CBCS/PGX/detectGradingCompany but only lookupCertification is consumed; imageOptimization.ts exports optimizeImage/estimateBase64Size with no importers; subscription.ts exports canUserScan/incrementScanCount/endTrial/getActiveListingCount/calculatePlatformFee with ZERO non-test production callers.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Demote genuinely-internal helpers (csvExport, certLookup) to non-exported. For subscription.ts functions that have tests but no production caller, decide per-function whether they are intended future hooks vs orphaned dead logic before deleting. Re-run knip after each batch.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — subscription.ts functions are tested but have zero production callers — need a product/logic decision before deletion.
