#### [M-CC16] Test validation.ts Zod schemas — untested API input-validation boundary
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · testing · effort S
- **1. Description:** src/lib/validation.ts exports validateBody/validateQuery/validateParams plus a central `schemas` object used to validate API request inputs.
- **2. Issue identified:** No validation test file exists. This is a security/correctness boundary with no tests. If a schema is too loose (missing min/max, allowing negative prices or oversized strings) or a validate* wrapper mishandles a Zod error shape, bad data passes silently. CLAUDE.md mandates tests for validation helpers.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add validation.test.ts asserting each schema rejects representative invalid inputs and accepts valid ones, and that validateBody/Query/Params return the expected shape on Zod failure. Also cover getRateLimitIdentifier precedence and notificationsCache version-mismatch discard.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pure Zod schemas; testable directly.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/validation.ts:25, :39, :58, :72; no validation test file exists

#### [M-CC17] Bump @sentry/nextjs within-major to clear transitive uuid vuln
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · dependencies · effort S
- **1. Description:** @sentry/nextjs is installed at 10.32.1 while 10.57.0 is available in the same major. The current version pulls a vulnerable transitive uuid via @sentry/webpack-plugin.
- **2. Issue identified:** npm audit flags @sentry/webpack-plugin -> uuid (<11.1.1, moderate: missing buffer bounds check) reaching the app through @sentry/nextjs. A within-major bump is low risk and is also a prerequisite to drop the deprecated disableLogger config.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Upgrade @sentry/nextjs to 10.57.0, run npm audit to confirm the uuid chain clears, and rebuild.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** npm audit uuid<11.1.1 chain; installed 10.32.1, latest 10.57.0

#### [M-CC18] Declare @typescript-eslint plugin/parser as direct devDependencies (phantom dependency)
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · dependencies · effort S
- **1. Description:** eslint.config.mjs imports @typescript-eslint/eslint-plugin and @typescript-eslint/parser directly, but neither is listed in package.json.
- **2. Issue identified:** These packages resolve only transitively through eslint-config-next -> typescript-eslint@8.52.0. This is a phantom dependency: if eslint-config-next changes its tree, `npm run lint` breaks with no version control on the plugin. The depcheck script even hard-codes both into its --ignores list, masking the gap.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add both as explicit devDependencies pinned to the resolved 8.52.x line, then remove them from the depcheck --ignores list.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** eslint.config.mjs:4-5; package.json:28; npm ls shows both deduped under eslint-config-next@16.1.1

#### [M-CC19] Add aria-expanded/aria-haspopup to disclosure widgets and dropdowns
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · accessibility · effort S
- **1. Description:** The app has multiple expand/collapse and dropdown patterns: the FAQ accordion in Navigation/AskProfessor, the desktop 'More' dropdown, and the mobile 'More' button.
- **2. Issue identified:** aria-expanded and aria-haspopup are 0 across the entire codebase. Accordion triggers and menu buttons don't announce their open/closed state to screen readers.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add aria-expanded={isOpen} to the FAQ accordion buttons and the 'More' menu toggles, plus aria-haspopup="menu" on the dropdown triggers.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_

Issue identified:** All four legal pages render the brand as 'Collector&apos;s Chest', and the terms page mixes both forms — heading uses the apostrophe while body text uses 'Collectors Chest'. Two admin alert routes also use the apostrophe form. These are legally binding documents naming the operating product, so the inconsistency creates ambiguity.

#### [M-DOC6] Fix collectCoverageFrom in jest.config.js — coverage reports only 3 files
- **Area / Dimension:** documentation · testing · effort S
- **1. Description:** jest.config.js restricts collectCoverageFrom to exactly three files: src/types/auction.ts, src/lib/subscription.ts, src/hooks/useGuestScans.ts.
- **2. Issue identified:** `npm run test:coverage` reports coverage for ONLY those 3 files and nothing for the ~70 other source files (auctionDb, tradingDb, contentFilter, etc.). This gives a misleading 'coverage looks fine' signal and hides untested money/permission logic.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Broaden collectCoverageFrom to ['src/lib/**/*.ts','src/types/**/*.ts','src/hooks/**/*.ts','!**/*.d.ts','!**/__tests__/**'].

#### [M-DOC7] ARCHITECTURE env var names don't match code (EBAY_CERT_ID vs EBAY_CLIENT_SECRET; HCAPTCHA_SECRET vs HCAPTCHA_SECRET_KEY)
- **Area / Dimension:** documentation · documentation · effort S

ARCHITECTURE.md:1208 (and :1008) lists 'HCAPTCHA_SECRET' but code reads HCAPTCHA_SECRET_KEY. Anyone provisioning env from the doc would set the wrong keys and break eBay pricing and hCaptcha.

#### [M-DOC9] Fix provider-order drift in TECHNICAL_FEATURES.md (line 10 says Claude primary; code is Gemini primary)
- **Area / Dimension:** documentation · documentation · effort S
- **1. Description:** TECHNICAL_FEATURES.md is the canonical system-overview doc. Section 1, line 10 describes the scan pipeline as 'Claude Vision (primary) → Gemini (fallback)'.
- **2. Issue identified:** The shipped code is the reverse (VISION_PROVIDER_ORDER = ['gemini','anthropic']). The feature spec correctly states 'Gemini Flash (primary)... 5x cheaper.' Line 10 contradicts both the code and the feature spec, and misrepresents an active cost optimization. The SAME doc is internally inconsistent — line 148 says 'Gemini → Anthropic fallback'.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Edit TECHNICAL_FEATURES.md line 10 to read 'Gemini Flash (primary) → Claude Sonnet (fallback)'.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — doc fix (documents a real ~5x per-scan cost saving) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** docs/TECHNICAL_FEATURES.md:10 vs src/lib/models.ts:17 and aiProvider.ts:20; correct in docs/features/01-ai-cover-recognition.md:155-156; self-contradiction at :148

#### [M-DOC10] ARCHITECTURE.md 'Last Updated' is Session 46; TECHNICAL_FEATURES/EVALUATION advanced to Session 47

Per the memory note feedback_architecture_doc, ARCHITECTURE.md must be updated every close-up-shop and was flagged as critical — it has again fallen a session behind, so any Session 47 system changes are undocumented in the system map.

#### [M-EM2] Batch follower emails on new-listing fan-out instead of one Resend call per follower
- **Area / Dimension:** email · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** notifyFollowersOfNewListing sends a separate sendNotificationEmail call for every email-enabled follower in an unbounded for-loop.
- **2. Issue identified:** Notifications are batch-inserted (good), but emails are sent one Resend request per follower in a fire-and-forget loop. A seller with hundreds of followers generates hundreds of individual Resend API requests per listing, risking Resend rate limits and adding latency to the createAuction/createFixedPriceListing path. The cron paths already solved this with sendNotificationEmailsBatch.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Reuse sendNotificationEmailsBatch (already imported) to send follower emails via Resend's batch API, matching the cron pattern.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Resend API-request volume / rate-limit headroom at scale; does not change the 3K/mo recipient-email quota _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/auctionDb.ts:2002-2018; contrast :3204, :3425, :3718; import at :41

#### [M-EM3] Avoid per-auction re-fetch of comic/profile data in cron email-prep paths
- **Area / Dimension:** email · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** Cron email-prep paths call getProfileForEmail and getListingComicData per auction/recipient, each a fresh Supabase round-trip, even though the candidate fetch already loaded the auction rows.
- **2. Issue identified:** getListingComicData re-queries auctions+comics by id for each claimed auction; the candidate fetch already selected the auction rows and could have joined comic title/issue. Same for getProfileForEmail. With mapWithConcurrency the fan-out is bounded but it is still N extra Supabase round-trips per cron tick across multiple cron jobs.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Join comics(title, issue_number) into the candidate select queries, and batch-fetch recipient profiles with a single profiles.in('id', recipientIds) lookup, building email params from in-memory maps.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Supabase read volume at scale — modest, helps stay under the free-tier read budget _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/email.ts:237-259; src/lib/auctionDb.ts:2455-2457, 3157-3160; deferral comment at :2760

#### [M-EM4] Add List-Unsubscribe / List-Unsubscribe-Post headers to bulk emails
- **Area / Dimension:** email · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** Resend sends in email.ts set no List-Unsubscribe or List-Unsubscribe-Post headers, and emailFooter() has no unsubscribe link.
- **2. Issue identified:** Gmail/Yahoo bulk-sender rules (Feb 2024) require a one-click List-Unsubscribe header for marketing/bulk mail; missing it can hurt deliverability for the high-volume non-transactional categories. Downgraded from high: transactional mail is exempt, those bulk types are already preference-gated, and a per-category opt-out exists at /settings.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** On non-transactional sends, pass List-Unsubscribe and List-Unsubscribe-Post headers and add a visible 'Manage email preferences' link to emailFooter(). Resend supports custom headers on emails.send and batch.send.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change (better deliverability marginally reduces wasted retries against the 3K/mo Resend free cap) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Code is doable; needs the unsubscribe route URL and a mailto inbox confirmed by product.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/email.ts:1059-1065, :1151-1157, :207-216

#### [M-EM5] Decompose the 1551-line email.ts: extract shared layout + button into helpers
- **Area / Dimension:** email · tech-debt · effort L
- **1. Description:** src/lib/email.ts is 1551 lines holding ~29 templates, each repeating the same outer wrapper div and CTA button styling inline.
- **2. Issue identified:** The identical wrapper div (max-width: 600px) appears 29 times and the CTA button string box-shadow: 4px 4px 0 #000 appears 29 times. Any brand tweak must be hand-edited in 29 places, and the file is hard to review.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add helpers: emailLayout(soundEffect, bodyHtml) and emailButton(href, label, {variant}); refactor each template to call them (also the natural place to centralize escapeHtml). Optionally split per-domain template files re-exported from an index.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Mechanical refactor; existing email tests guard output.

#### [M-EM6] Batch feedback-reminder cron sends instead of per-row sendNotificationEmail loop
- **Area / Dimension:** email · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** The feedback-reminder cron loops eligible reminders (query limited to 100) and calls sendNotificationEmail() one at a time, awaiting each, with a per-call Supabase update afterward.
- **2. Issue identified:** The route iterates eligibleReminders and awaits sendNotificationEmail per row then runs a per-row Supabase .update(). Since feedback_reminder is non-transactional and profileId is not passed, every recipient also triggers an extra resolveProfileIdByEmail Supabase query. A run near 100 risks Resend's 10 req/sec limit and a Netlify function timeout.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Build a FeedbackEmailData array and call sendNotificationEmailsBatch once, then bulk-update reminder_count/last_reminder_at. Add `id` to the profiles select and pass it as profileId to skip the per-recipient resolveProfileIdByEmail lookup.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change (cuts Supabase query count and Resend request count per cron run) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/cron/send-feedback-reminders/route.ts:76, :111, :121; select omits id at :34-38; batch helper at email.ts:1100; category at notificationPreferences.ts:49

- **2. Issue identified:** No emailValidation test file exists. The pure parts — format regex and disposable-domain Set lookup — gate account creation. A regression either rejects valid users (lost signups) or lets throwaway emails through (abuse/cost vector). generateVerificationToken is also untested.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add emailValidation.test.ts for the synchronous pieces: valid/invalid format cases, known disposable domains blocked, normal domains allowed, token shape/uniqueness. Mock the DNS/MX call.

- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pure helpers testable; async MX needs a DNS mock.

#### [M-FE4] useOffline polls the offline queue with setInterval every 1 second
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** useOffline runs setInterval(updatePendingCount, 1000) to keep the pending-actions badge in sync.
- **2. Issue identified:** A 1s interval reads localStorage (JSON.parse of the queue) every second for the entire lifetime of any mounted component using the hook, even when idle and online with an empty queue. Unnecessary main-thread work and battery drain on mobile.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace polling with event-driven updates: dispatch a custom 'offline-queue-changed' event from offlineCache.ts mutators and listen for it. At minimum increase the interval to 5-10s and pause it when the queue is empty.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — CPU/battery on client _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/hooks/useOffline.ts:104-113

#### [M-FE5] Memoize homepage full-collection insight computations
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** The homepage (page.tsx, 'use client') computes calculateCollectionValue(collection), stats, and runs getBiggestIncrease/getBiggestDecline/getBestBuy — each filtering and looping the full collection — inline on every render with no useMemo.
- **2. Issue identified:** Every render (duration-toggle state, modal open, any setState) re-iterates the entire collection multiple times. For large collections this is wasted work on the most-visited page.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Memoize collectionValue, stats, biggestIncrease, biggestDecline, and bestBuy with useMemo keyed on collection (and the relevant duration state).
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — pure client-side perf improvement _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/page.tsx:92, :97, :104-130, :133-159, :162-183, :185-187; grep useMemo/useCallback = 0

#### [M-FE6] Redirect signed-in users before the heavy client homepage renders
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** src/app/page.tsx is a 'use client' page (~840 lines) that, on mount, redirects signed-in users to /collection via router.replace inside useEffect.
- **2. Issue identified:** Signed-in users still download and execute the entire client homepage bundle only to be immediately client-redirected. They briefly see the guest hero flash before Clerk resolves and the redirect fires. Wasted render + bundle on every signed-in homepage hit, plus a visible flicker.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Move the signed-in redirect to middleware or a server component; or split the guest hero (static server component) from the signed-in dashboard so each path only loads what it needs.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change (perf/UX win) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/page.tsx:1, 71-75, 203

#### [M-FE7] Convert homepage cover thumbnails from raw <img> to next/image
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** The homepage renders insight-card covers, modal covers, and the Recently Viewed grid with plain <img> tags loading remote Supabase cover URLs (~400KB each).
- **2. Issue identified:** Seven raw <img> tags load full-size remote covers with no width/height optimization, no responsive srcset, no AVIF/WebP transcoding, and no lazy loading on the grid. next/image is already configured and would auto-optimize on a key landing page.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace the homepage <img> tags with next/image (fill for the aspect-[2/3] grid; explicit width/height for the small/modal thumbnails).
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Netlify bandwidth — optimized/lazy covers transfer far fewer bytes per homepage load; reduces bandwidth pressure on the $9.54/mo plan at scale _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/page.tsx:254, 291, 328, 604, 678, 734, 807; next.config.mjs:10-17

#### [M-FE8] Lock down next/image remote host wildcard ('**') to an explicit allowlist
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · security · effort S
- **1. Description:** The Next image optimizer is configured with a single remote pattern matching every HTTPS hostname.
- **2. Issue identified:** remotePatterns uses hostname: '**', turning the image optimizer into an open proxy: anyone can pass arbitrary external URLs through your optimizer (SSRF surface, bandwidth abuse, serving unvetted third-party content under your origin).
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace '**' with an explicit allowlist of the actual hostnames in use (Supabase storage domain, Clerk avatar domain, any eBay image CDN).
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves potential Netlify optimizer bandwidth abuse — prevents third parties using your image optimizer as a free proxy _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Need to confirm the exact set of remote image hosts before locking the allowlist.
- **Evidence:** next.config.mjs:11-16 (duplicate of H-CC4; tracked once cross-cutting, once frontend)

#### [M-FE9] Hoist messaging fetch + Supabase realtime into one shared hook used by both nav bars
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** Navigation.tsx (desktop) and MobileNav.tsx (mobile) are both always present in the DOM — only hidden via CSS — and each independently fetches profile + unread count and opens a Supabase realtime channel.
- **2. Issue identified:** On every signed-in page load, each component fetches /api/username/current, fetches /api/messages/unread-count twice, and opens its own Supabase realtime channel `user:${profileId}:messages`. Net: 2x username calls, 4x unread-count calls, and two duplicate realtime subscriptions. Every inbound message broadcast triggers both channels to re-fetch simultaneously.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Move profileId + unread-count + the realtime subscription into a single shared context/provider (or a module-singleton hook) consumed by both nav components.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Upstash/Supabase load — roughly halves unread-count + username requests and removes one realtime connection per active session _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/components/Navigation.tsx:246-300; src/components/MobileNav.tsx:63-117; mounted via layout.tsx:135 and Providers.tsx:13

#### [M-FE10] Remove 'use client' from the four static legal pages and add per-page metadata
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** terms (618 lines), privacy (428), cookies (273), and acceptable-use (355) are static legal text pages.
- **2. Issue identified:** All four start with 'use client' yet contain zero interactivity (0 matches for useState/useEffect/onClick/onChange/onSubmit/export const metadata in each). Marking them client-side forces React hydration of ~1670 lines of static legal text, ships needless client JS, and makes `export const metadata` impossible.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Drop 'use client' to make them server components and add `export const metadata` per page.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change (small bundle/perf win) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/terms/page.tsx:1; privacy/cookies/acceptable-use page.tsx:1 (all 'use client', 0 interactivity)

#### [M-FE11] Add experimental.optimizePackageImports for lucide-react
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · bundle · effort S
- **1. Description:** lucide-react is imported via named imports in 116 files, including the always-loaded Navigation/MobileNav/homepage shell.
- **2. Issue identified:** next.config.mjs sets no experimental.optimizePackageImports. Without it, named imports from the lucide barrel can pull more of the icon set than needed into route chunks. Next.js explicitly recommends optimizePackageImports for lucide-react.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add experimental: { optimizePackageImports: ['lucide-react'] } (and consider '@clerk/nextjs') to next.config.mjs.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Netlify bandwidth slightly via smaller JS chunks; $0 direct _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** next.config.mjs:9-18; 116 files import from 'lucide-react'

#### [M-FE12] Switch client cover compression output from JPEG to WebP
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** Client-side compression (compressToTarget) always encodes to image/jpeg.
- **2. Issue identified:** compressToTarget calls canvas.toDataURL('image/jpeg', quality) for both the full image and thumbnail, and ImageUpload renames the output File to .jpg. All modern target browsers support canvas WebP encoding, which is ~25-35% smaller at equal visual quality.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Encode to image/webp with a JPEG fallback for unsupported browsers (feature-detect via a one-pixel toDataURL test); update the produced File extension/content-type.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves ~25-35% on cover storage and upload bandwidth per image; compounds with the server-side WebP re-encode finding _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Self-contained in imageOptimization.ts plus ImageUpload handling.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/imageOptimization.ts:91,96; src/components/ImageUpload.tsx:77-79

#### [M-FE13] Decompose oversized React page/form components and code-split profile tabs
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · tech-debt · effort L
- **1. Description:** Several client components are very large with heavy local state: CustomProfilePage.tsx (1578 lines, 42 useState, 13 useEffect), collection/page.tsx (1554 lines), ComicDetailsForm.tsx (1472 lines, 28 useState), ComicDetailModal.tsx (1234 lines).
- **2. Issue identified:** Components this large with that many useState hooks are hard to reason about, prone to stale-closure/re-render bugs, and bloat the client bundle. CustomProfilePage renders all three tabs inline, so Security/Billing markup ships even for users who never open them. ComicDetailsForm's 28 useState is a strong signal to extract a reducer.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Extract sub-components and move pure helpers to lib. For CustomProfilePage: pull out ProfileTab/SecurityTab/BillingTab and lazy-load non-active tabs via next/dynamic. For ComicDetailsForm: convert 28 useState to useReducer or tabbed sub-forms. Tackle ComicDetailsForm and collection/page.tsx first.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — code-split/extraction reduces shipped JS per route; no service cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pure refactor; behavior preserved, manual smoke test recommended.
- **Evidence:** src/components/CustomProfilePage.tsx:1-1578; src/app/collection/page.tsx; src/components/ComicDetailsForm.tsx; src/components/ComicDetailModal.tsx

#### [M-MK11] Dead column estimated_value selected in 4 trade query sites
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · database · effort S
- **1. Description:** Several trade-related queries select an `estimated_value` column from comics and map it to estimatedValue on the response cards.
- **2. Issue identified:** The canonical comics table defines average_price and condition_grade but NO estimated_value, and no migration ever adds it (grep across all supabase/*.sql returned nothing). cloneSoldComic.ts treats average_price as canonical. So the column is either erroring the query or undocumented schema drift; the mapped estimatedValue silently surfaces as undefined/null in the UI cards.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Confirm the real value column (average_price) and replace estimated_value across these sites, OR add a migration creating estimated_value if intended.

#### [M-MK12] Duplicated trade-status state machine across markAsShipped/confirmReceipt/updateTradeStatus diverges
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · code-quality · effort M
- **1. Description:** Three functions each independently re-fetch the trade, re-verify participant, branch on isProposer, and write proposer_*/recipient_* columns.
- **2. Issue identified:** The 'which side am I / which columns do I write' plus 'both sides done -> advance status' logic is copy-pasted three times. Real divergence: updateTradeStatus's trackingNumber branch sets proposer_shipped_at but does NOT run the both-shipped -> status:'shipped' promotion that markAsShipped does, so the same logical action yields different state depending on which API path is hit.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Extract one helper taking (trade, userId, sideField, timestampField) that centralizes proposer/recipient column resolution and the both-sides completion check, and route updateTradeStatus tracking updates through markAsShipped.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
