#### [M-MK13] Missing pagination / result limits on trades, matches, watchlist and transactions queries
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** Several list endpoints return entire result sets with no LIMIT: getUserTrades, getUserMatches, getUserWatchlist, and the purchases/wins/offers branches of /api/transactions.
- **2. Issue identified:** getUserTrades and getUserMatches have no cap and amplify the N+1 issues; getUserWatchlist and the transactions purchases/wins/offers branches have no limit and join full comic + seller rows. Only the bids branch caps at 100. Payloads and round-trips grow unbounded as a power user accumulates history.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add a sane LIMIT (e.g. 50-100) plus cursor/offset pagination and wire 'load more', reusing the 100 cap already on the bids branch.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 direct at beta; saves Supabase egress / load for power users _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/tradingDb.ts:116-146, 483-499; src/lib/auctionDb.ts:1244-1254; src/app/api/transactions/route.ts:116-142, 185-213

#### [M-MK14] account.updated handler writes without confirming a row matched and is documented as never validated
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** The account.updated handler updates profiles.stripe_connect_onboarding_complete by stripe_connect_account_id but doesn't verify a row was matched.
- **2. Issue identified:** The handler fires .update().eq('stripe_connect_account_id', account.id) with no .select()/row-count check. Per project notes, Supabase .update() with no matching rows returns NO error, so a missing account id silently no-ops. BACKLOG.md explicitly records this handler was never validated because stripe listen wasn't forwarding connected-account events. Stale seller bank/business data would then never sync.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add .select('id') to confirm a row updated and log a warning when zero rows match. Confirm account.updated is subscribed on the production webhook endpoint. Optionally also store charges_enabled/payouts_enabled.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — correctness/payout integrity _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Code change is autonomous; verifying the production webhook event subscription + live Connect test needs dashboard access.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:117-127; BACKLOG.md:216-227

#### [M-MK15] Untyped trade DB transforms (`db: any`) defeat schema-drift detection
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · tech-debt · effort M
- **1. Description:** tradingDb transform helpers and inline item filters all take db: any / (i: any) and hand-map snake_case DB columns to camelCase fields.
- **2. Issue identified:** Per the project's own logged lesson ('join queries fail silently with wrong column names'), any-typed transforms mean a renamed/missing column (e.g. proposer_tracking_carrier) silently maps to undefined with no compile or runtime error. The trades feature moves real inventory between users, so silent field loss is especially damaging.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Define a Row type matching the select() shape (or generate types from Supabase) and type the transforms as db: TradeRow.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/tradingDb.ts:398,420,429,432,437; inline filters at 161-162

#### [M-MK16] Marketplace transfer uses transfer_data without on_behalf_of — platform bears Stripe fee on full buyer total
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** Marketplace Buy Now / auction checkout charges the buyer on the platform account and transfers sellerAmount to the connected account via payment_intent_data.transfer_data.destination.
- **2. Issue identified:** checkout/route.ts sets transfer_data.destination + amount with no on_behalf_of and no application_fee. In this 'separate charges and transfers' shape, Stripe's 2.9%+$0.30 fee is charged to the PLATFORM on the full unit_amount, while the platform fee floor is only MIN_PLATFORM_FEE_CENTS=75. On a $5 sale Stripe takes ~$0.445 from the platform and the platform retains $0.75 — netting only ~$0.30. The break-even comment omits the $0.30 fixed fee.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Either (a) switch to destination charges with on_behalf_of: sellerProfile.stripe_connect_account_id and application_fee_amount = platformFee so Stripe's fee settles on the seller, or (b) raise MIN_PLATFORM_FEE_CENTS to cover 2.9%+$0.30. Recompute the break-even comments.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves real money on every small marketplace sale — platform currently eats 2.9%+$0.30 on the full buyer amount; on a $5 sale ~$0.45 vs $0.75 retained _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** No — Changing the charge model is a money/product decision (merchant of record, refund flows, statement descriptor) needing owner approval.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/checkout/route.ts:150-154, 185-190; src/lib/stripeConnect.ts:6-38

#### [M-MK17] No webhook reconciliation for refunds / disputes / transfer failures — sale recorded regardless of transfer settlement
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** The buyer charge and seller transfer are bundled in one PaymentIntent, but the webhook flips the listing to sold/paid and records the sale without any post-charge reconciliation.
- **2. Issue identified:** handleMarketplacePayment marks status='sold', inserts a sales row, and increments completed_sales with no check that funds reached the seller. There is no handler for charge.refunded, charge.dispute.created, transfer.reversed, or payout.failed — the webhook switch default silently swallows them. The Buy Now race-refund path can also fire a refund while a transfer is already in flight.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add webhook handlers for charge.refunded, charge.dispute.created, and transfer.reversed/failed to reverse the sale/ownership/sales-row state, and log to the existing auction audit trail.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves dispute/refund losses and prevents seller-credited-but-unfunded states; $0 direct platform cost _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/checkout/route.ts:185-190; src/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:129-130, 203-340

#### [M-MK18] Grade-independent durable cache + grade-in-query eBay search can persist a grade-skewed base price
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** searchActiveListings injects the requested grade into the eBay keyword query; convertBrowseToPriceData divides the grade-specific median by that grade's multiplier to derive a 9.4 base. con-mode-lookup saves that single price_data into comic_metadata keyed only on title+issue (no grade).
- **2. Issue identified:** The first grade ever looked up for a title+issue determines the stored base price for ALL future grades, because the durable comic_metadata key omits grade. If the first lookup was a thin/odd low-grade listing set, the derived 9.4 base can be distorted and every later high-grade lookup inherits it. The Redis ebayPrice key DOES include grade, so the two cache layers disagree on granularity.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Either include grade in the comic_metadata price-branch decision, or only persist a price derived from a near-9.4 query. Add a test for convertBrowseToPriceData base-derivation across low vs high requested grades.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Logic + test change in-repo; the 'persist only near-9.4 base' option is lower-risk.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/ebayBrowse.ts:305-317; src/lib/cache.ts:161-163 vs 142-156; src/app/api/con-mode-lookup/route.ts:277-298

#### [M-MS3] Collapse the two-query unread-message-count and drop the redundant call in /api/messages GET
- **Area / Dimension:** messaging · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** getUnreadMessageCount first selects all of the user's conversation IDs, then runs a second count query with an unbounded .in(conversationIds). It is also called redundantly inside /api/messages GET alongside getUserConversations.
- **2. Issue identified:** Two round-trips plus an IN list that grows with the user's conversation count, on a hot path. In /api/messages GET the total is recomputed even though getUserConversations already produced per-conversation unreadCount, doubling the DB work.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** In /api/messages GET, derive totalUnread by summing the previews' unreadCount instead of a second DB scan. For the standalone badge path, replace the two-step pattern with a single RPC/count over a participant-filtered join.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 now — removes round-trips on a hot path, lowers Supabase/Upstash free-tier pressure _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Deriving totalUnread from existing previews is in-repo; the single-query count needs a DB function/migration.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/messagingDb.ts:112-134; redundant call at src/app/api/messages/route.ts:37-38

#### [M-MS4] Deduplicate and cache unread-count fetches and realtime channels from Navigation and MobileNav
- **Area / Dimension:** messaging · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** Navigation.tsx and MobileNav.tsx are both mounted for signed-in users; each has two useEffects that call /api/messages/unread-count AND each opens its own Supabase realtime channel user:{profileId}:messages.
- **2. Issue identified:** A single signed-in page load can fire multiple unread-count requests, and every unread-update broadcast triggers two identical fetches (one per nav component) — each running getUnreadMessageCount's two DB queries with no caching. Two duplicate subscriptions to the same channel are also held open per client.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Hoist unread count into a single shared context/provider (or one hook with a module-level cache) consumed by both Navigation and MobileNav. Optionally add a short Redis cache (30-60s keyed by profile id, invalidated on broadcast).
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 now — halves unread-count requests and Realtime fan-out per signed-in user; saves Supabase query volume and Upstash command volume _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/components/Navigation.tsx:255-272 and 274-300; src/components/MobileNav.tsx:72-89 and 91-117; getUnreadMessageCount at messagingDb.ts:112-134

#### [M-MS5] Delimit and harden user content in the AI moderation prompt against prompt injection
- **Area / Dimension:** messaging · security · effort S
- **1. Description:** The hourly AI moderation cron interpolates raw user message text directly into the Claude prompt: Message content: "${content}".
- **2. Issue identified:** User-controlled content is injected into the moderator prompt with no delimiting/escaping. A crafted message can attempt prompt injection (e.g. closing the quote then emitting a fake JSON verdict) to make the moderator self-clear an abusive message. Impact is bounded — phone/email are already hard-blocked at send time and the queue still exists for human review.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Wrap user content in a clearly delimited block (e.g. <message_to_review>...</message_to_review>), instruct the model to treat its contents purely as data, neutralize delimiter sequences in content, keep max_tokens low, and validate the parsed JSON shape.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/cron/moderate-messages/route.ts:41-43

#### [M-MS6] Parallelize moderation AI calls and add an explicit route maxDuration
- **Area / Dimension:** messaging · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** The moderation cron processes up to 50 flagged messages strictly sequentially (for...of with await analyzeMessage), and the route declares no maxDuration.
- **2. Issue identified:** 50 sequential Anthropic calls at ~1-3s each can run 50-150s — well past typical serverless caps. The Netlify scheduled function only fires a fetch to the route, so the work runs inside the Next.js route function with no maxDuration export, risking a mid-batch timeout that leaves messages partially moderated.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Process in bounded-concurrency chunks (Promise.allSettled over chunks of 5-10) and add export const maxDuration. Optionally lower the per-run limit.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — same call count; avoids wasted partial runs _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/cron/moderate-messages/route.ts:156-159; netlify/functions/moderate-messages.ts:16

#### [M-MS7] Tighten phone-number BLOCK regex to reduce false positives
- **Area / Dimension:** messaging · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** The phone-number BLOCKED pattern is broad and matches many non-phone digit runs.
- **2. Issue identified:** The regex matches any 10-digit run including legitimate comic context (ISBN fragments, cert numbers, year+price/quantity strings written with spaces). Because it hard-BLOCKS, false positives silently prevent legitimate marketplace messages from sending with only a generic reason — a real trust/UX hit.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Constrain with word boundaries and require phone-like separators or a leading + / parenthesis, and/or downgrade ambiguous matches to FLAG rather than BLOCK. Cover with the new contentFilter tests.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/contentFilter.ts:11-14

#### [M-MS8] Strip stack traces and debug fields from message API error responses
- **Area / Dimension:** messaging · security · effort S
- **1. Description:** The /api/messages GET and POST handlers return verbose error responses on 500s — error.stack as `details`, plus internal debugStep markers, errorType, and errorConstructor name directly in the JSON response body.
- **2. Issue identified:** Returning stack traces and internal control-flow markers to clients is information disclosure — they reveal file paths, library versions, table/column names, and internal control flow useful for reconnaissance. The debugStep/errorConstructor/errorType instrumentation is leftover debugging shipped to production.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Return only a generic { error: 'Failed to send message' } plus a stable error code to the client, and log full detail server-side via console.error/Sentry only. Remove debugStep, details (stack), errorType, and errorConstructor.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/messages/route.ts:44-60, :105-128

#### [M-MS9] Add debounce/dedup to message_received email — burst conversations send one email per message
- **Area / Dimension:** messaging · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** sendMessage() fires a message_received notification email (fire-and-forget) on every single message insert when the recipient has msg_email_enabled.
- **2. Issue identified:** There is no debounce, batching, or unread-aware suppression. A rapid 20-message exchange sends 20 separate emails. This is the single highest-frequency email trigger (one per message) and at scale burns the Resend free tier (3K/mo) fast while spamming users.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add a per-conversation cooldown (skip if a message_received notification was sent to this recipient for this conversation within the last X minutes) or notify only on the first new message since last read. Store last_notified_at on the conversation or a lightweight Upstash key.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Resend free-tier headroom (3K/mo) — can cut message-email volume an estimated 70-90% in active chats; defers a paid Resend tier _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Code is doable; needs a product call on the dedup rule and possibly a small schema column.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/messagingDb.ts:320-355, :263; category 'social' at notificationPreferences.ts:66; grep for cooldown|debounce|throttle returns nothing

#### [M-MS10] getConversationMessages loads entire thread with no limit
- **Area / Dimension:** messaging · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** Opening a conversation selects all messages with messages.* plus two nested auction+comic joins, ordered ascending, with no .limit() or pagination.
- **2. Issue identified:** A long-running conversation returns its full message history on every open, each row joining auctions->comics twice. With no upper bound, payload and join work grow unbounded with thread length.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add pagination (e.g. last 50 messages via .range, load-older on scroll) and replace messages.* with an explicit column list. The existing idx_messages_conversation supports paged reads.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 direct at beta; saves Supabase egress/compute proportional to thread length _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/messagingDb.ts:185-209; supporting index supabase/migrations/20260127_messaging.sql:54

#### [M-MS11] Replace brittle hand-chained Supabase mock in messagingDb.test.ts; cover sendMessage
- **Area / Dimension:** messaging · testing · effort M
- **1. Description:** messagingDb.test.ts mocks Supabase by manually re-chaining mockUpdate/mockEq/mockNeq in beforeEach and asserts mostly that the right table/chain is targeted; it only covers the trivial markMessagesAsRead.
- **2. Issue identified:** The test hard-codes the exact `.update().eq().neq().eq()` chain, so any benign query refactor breaks the test without a real bug. The higher-risk function — sendMessage, which enforces contentFilter blocking, conversation membership, block checks, and message persistence — has NO test.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Refactor the mock to a reusable fluent-builder helper. Add coverage for sendMessage: blocked-content rejection, self-message rejection, blocked-by-recipient rejection, happy-path insert payload.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/__tests__/messagingDb.test.ts:15-37; src/lib/messagingDb.ts:263, :281

#### [M-PAY4] Webhook idempotency TTL (1 hour) is shorter than Stripe's ~3-day retry window
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** The processed-event idempotency key expires after 1 hour (CACHE_TTL.webhook).
- **2. Issue identified:** cache.ts:35 sets webhook TTL to 60*60 (1 hour); Stripe retries failed deliveries with backoff for up to ~3 days. Once the marker placement is fixed, a legitimately-redelivered-but-already-completed event arriving more than 1 hour later would re-run a non-idempotent handler (e.g. addPurchasedScans double-credits). The dedup window must cover Stripe's full retry horizon.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Increase the webhook idempotency TTL to at least 3 days (259200s) — bump CACHE_TTL.webhook or pass ttlOverride in the route's cacheSet call.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — negligible Upstash storage; well within free tier _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/cache.ts:35; src/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:59

#### [M-PAY5] Scan-pack credit (addPurchasedScans) is non-idempotent read-modify-write — double-credit risk
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** On checkout.session.completed for a scan pack, the webhook adds 10 scans via a select-then-update that is not idempotent per checkout session.
- **2. Issue identified:** handleScanPackPurchase calls addPurchasedScans which does select purchased_scans then update — a read-modify-write with no per-session marker. Combined with the marker-placement bug and the short TTL, a redelivered event or two concurrent deliveries can credit 20 scans for one purchase.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Make the credit idempotent at the data layer: record processed checkout session IDs and skip if already credited, or use an atomic SQL increment RPC gated on session id. At minimum, fixing the marker-placement and TTL findings removes the redelivery vector.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves AI scan cost from abuse — each erroneous extra 10-scan grant is ~$0.15 of Sonnet vision given away free _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — A processed-sessions marker needs a DB column/migration; an atomic RPC needs a Supabase function migration.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:162-167; src/lib/subscription.ts:38, 478-494

#### [M-PAY6] Trial checkout sets trial_period_days but no trial_settings / payment-method-collection policy
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** The 7-day and 30-day promo trials are created via subscription_data.trial_period_days only.
- **2. Issue identified:** checkout/route.ts sets only trial_period_days inside subscription_data. There is no trial_settings.end_behavior.missing_payment_method and no explicit payment_method_collection policy. Combined with handleSubscriptionUpdated mapping 'incomplete' to 'active', a subscription that fails to charge at trial end can leave the user with premium access while never paying.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Set subscription_data.trial_settings.end_behavior.missing_payment_method to 'cancel' (or 'pause') and confirm payment_method_collection: 'always'. Pair with explicit handling of 'incomplete'/'incomplete_expired'.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves lost subscription revenue — recovers premium conversions that would otherwise lapse uncharged at trial end _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/billing/checkout/route.ts:151-161; src/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:455-469

#### [M-PAY7] handleSubscriptionUpdated maps incomplete / incomplete_expired / paused to 'active'
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** The webhook status mapping handles trialing/past_due/canceled/unpaid explicitly; everything else defaults to 'active'.
- **2. Issue identified:** The default branch maps any unlisted Stripe status to 'active'. Stripe statuses 'incomplete', 'incomplete_expired', and 'paused' therefore read as 'active'. A subscription that never completed first payment, or expired without paying, would grant active-premium incorrectly, and a paused subscription keeps premium access.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Handle 'incomplete'/'incomplete_expired' as canceled (or a distinct non-active state) and 'paused' as past_due/canceled. Only true 'active' should map to active.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves giving away premium access (unlimited ~$0.015/scan vision calls + premium features) to users whose payment never cleared _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:455-469

#### [M-PAY8] getProfileByStripeCustomerId uses .single() — duplicate/missing customer rows fail subscription webhooks silently
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** Webhook handlers resolve the profile by Stripe customer id using Supabase .single().
- **2. Issue identified:** subscription.ts uses .eq('stripe_customer_id', id).single(). .single() errors on zero OR more than one match and the function returns null, which every subscription handler treats as 'no profile' and drops the event. Nothing enforces uniqueness on stripe_customer_id, and the customer-creation path is a read-then-write with no concurrency guard, so concurrent checkouts can produce duplicate customer rows.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Use .maybeSingle() and handle the multi-row case explicitly (log + pick most recent). Add a UNIQUE index on profiles.stripe_customer_id. Guard the customer-creation read-modify-write against concurrent checkout calls.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — correctness/reliability; prevents lost upgrades requiring manual support _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — The UNIQUE index requires a Supabase migration; the .maybeSingle() change is autonomous.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/subscription.ts:524-535; src/app/api/billing/checkout/route.ts:88-100

#### [M-PAY9] downgradeToFree forces status='active' and never reads cancel_at_period_end — no pending-cancel grace state
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** On customer.subscription.deleted the user is downgraded to free with status forced to 'active' and period end nulled.
- **2. Issue identified:** subscription.ts sets subscription_tier='free', subscription_status='active', and subscription_current_period_end=null on subscription.deleted. The code never reads cancel_at_period_end anywhere (grep confirms zero references), so there is no UI signal of a pending cancellation, and forcing status='active' on a canceled subscription is semantically wrong. If subscription.deleted ever arrives early the user loses paid access they were entitled to through period end.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Handle cancel_at_period_end in handleSubscriptionUpdated to surface a 'canceling' state and keep access until period end. On downgradeToFree set status to 'canceled', not 'active'. Expose pending-cancel/period-end so the UI can show 'Premium until <date>'.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — correctness/UX; prevents premature loss of paid access (refund/support risk) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/subscription.ts:567-578; src/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:474-485; no cancel_at_period_end references in src

#### [M-PAY10] Three divergent platform-fee calculations risk seller/platform accounting mismatch
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** The platform fee is computed in three places with different rounding and floor rules.
- **2. Issue identified:** stripeConnect.ts (calculateDestinationAmount, used by checkout) uses Math.floor on the percent-fee and enforces MIN_PLATFORM_FEE_CENTS. subscription.ts (calculatePlatformFee) uses Math.round on dollars with NO minimum floor. The connect/status route computes total fees paid with a third inline Math.round, no-floor formula. For sub-$10 sales the displayed 'fees paid' and the actually-deducted transfer fee will not match.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Consolidate to a single cents-based fee function (with floor) used by checkout, the seller status display, and analytics.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — correctness/trust; aligns displayed vs charged fees _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/stripeConnect.ts:26-38; src/lib/subscription.ts:668-677; src/app/api/connect/status/route.ts:39-46

#### [M-PAY11] Stripe webhook and Connect status updates never invalidate the 5-min profile cache
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** getProfileByClerkId caches the entire profiles row (subscription_tier, status, stripe_connect_onboarding_complete, is_admin) under a 5-minute TTL, but the mutation paths that change those fields do not bust the cache.
- **2. Issue identified:** The Stripe webhook updates the profiles row with subscription/connect fields, and connect/status updates stripe_connect_onboarding_complete — neither calls invalidateProfileCache. invalidateProfileCache is only called from admin suspend and age-verification. Result: after a subscription upgrade/downgrade or Connect onboarding completion, a user can see stale state for up to 5 minutes.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Call invalidateProfileCache(clerkUserId) at the end of every profiles mutation in the Stripe webhook and in connect/status. Optionally drop the profile TTL to 60s for these volatile fields.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — correctness/authz hardening _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/db.ts:65-95; src/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:122,234,428; src/app/api/connect/status/route.ts:71

#### [M-PAY12] Unauthenticated Stripe Connect onboarding-return can flip another user's onboarding flag (IDOR)
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · security · effort S
- **1. Description:** GET /api/connect/onboarding-return is a Stripe redirect handler that takes an account_id query param, retrieves the Connect account, and updates the profile row matching that account_id.
- **2. Issue identified:** The route has no auth() check and updates profiles.stripe_connect_onboarding_complete WHERE stripe_connect_account_id = <attacker-supplied account_id>. There is no verification that the caller owns that Connect account. A party who knows another seller's acct_XXXX ID can overwrite that user's onboarding-complete flag. Impact is bounded (the written value is Stripe's true state, not attacker-chosen) but it is an unauthenticated cross-user profile write.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Require auth(), resolve the caller's profile, and only update the row when stripe_connect_account_id belongs to the authenticated user (add .eq('clerk_user_id', userId)).
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — auth() + ownership filter; helpers already used in other connect routes.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/connect/onboarding-return/route.ts:22-42; contrast onboarding-refresh/route.ts

#### [M-PAY13] checkout re-fetches platform_fee_percent that getAuction already loaded
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** The Buy Now / auction-winner checkout flow validates the listing, fetches the seller's Connect fields, then fetches the platform fee.
- **2. Issue identified:** getAuction(targetId) selects `*` from auctions — which already includes platform_fee_percent — yet checkout immediately issues a redundant second `.select('platform_fee_percent')` query for that exact column, plus a third query for the seller's Connect fields. Three serial DB reads on a latency-sensitive payment path; the platform_fee_percent read is pure waste.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Read platform_fee_percent from the already-loaded listing object (ensure transformDbAuction maps it), and join/select the seller Connect fields in the same query, reducing 3 reads to 1-2.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change (lower checkout latency, fewer Supabase reads) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/checkout/route.ts:72, 120-124, 137-141; src/lib/auctionDb.ts:295-305

#### [M-MK19] Connect status route calls accounts.retrieve on every load and scans all sold rows in JS
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** GET /api/connect/status fetches live Stripe account status on every request and reduces every sold auction in JS to total fees.
- **2. Issue identified:** connect/status/route.ts calls stripe.accounts.retrieve(...) on every status check, and pulls every sold auction and reduces in JS to compute totalFeesPaid. onboarding_complete is already mirrored in the DB and updated by the webhook and onboarding-return, so the live retrieve is redundant for the common case; the fee total is an unbounded row scan.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Serve onboarding/charges/payouts state from the DB mirror and only call accounts.retrieve on cache-miss or a short TTL (cache.ts profile prefix already exists at 5 min). Compute totalFeesPaid via a SQL SUM/aggregate or a stored running total.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Stripe API call volume and DB read load; $0 hard cost but reduces latency and rate-limit pressure as sellers grow _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Serving from the DB mirror + short-TTL cache is fully in-repo; a SQL SUM aggregate via RPC would need a migration.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/connect/status/route.ts:33-46, 61-63

#### [M-PR5] Cache below-threshold (no-data) eBay results in con-mode-lookup
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** When eBay returns listings but the converted priceData is null (below the survivable-listings threshold), con-mode-lookup returns averagePrice:null and persists nothing to DB or Redis.
- **2. Issue identified:** Every repeat lookup of a thin-market comic (common at conventions for obscure keys) re-hits the eBay Browse API because the negative result is never stored. /api/ebay-prices already caches {noData:true} for 1h; con-mode-lookup has no equivalent.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** When priceData is null but a search ran, cacheSet a {noData:true} sentinel under the ebayPrice key with a short TTL and check it at the top of the eBay branch — mirror the existing ebay-prices negative-cache pattern.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 cash; reduces repeated eBay Browse calls on thin-market comics (free, rate-limited) _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pattern already exists in ebay-prices route.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/con-mode-lookup/route.ts:190-194, 324-374; vs src/app/api/ebay-prices/route.ts:140-147

#### [M-PR6] Duplicate key-comics sources: 402-entry static file + seeded Supabase table, pricing reads only the static one
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · tech-debt · effort M
- **1. Description:** Key comic facts live in two places: static src/lib/keyComicsDatabase.ts (KEY_COMICS) and the Supabase key_comics table, seeded from the same data.
- **2. Issue identified:** lookupKeyInfoFromDb / isKeyComicFromDb have ZERO callers outside keyComicsDb.ts. The pricing/scan paths import the static lookup only. keyComicsDb is imported solely by submit/admin routes. So the entire community moderation system writes key_comics rows that Key Hunt and Scan never read, and the same 402 facts are maintained in duplicate.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Pick one source of truth: either have con-mode-lookup/analyze call lookupKeyInfoFromDb (DB-with-static-fallback) so approved community entries surface, or delete the unused DB read path and document the static file as authoritative.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — reduces maintenance and Supabase row drift _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Code swap is in-repo, but whether community-approved key info should reach pricing is a product decision.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/con-mode-lookup/route.ts:7; src/app/api/analyze/route.ts:27; src/lib/keyComicsDb.ts:37-88; supabase/migrations/20250117_key_info_seed.sql:1-3

#### [M-PR7] Three divergent normalizeTitle implementations can mismatch the same title across key-comics paths
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** Three normalizeTitle variants apply different rules. keyComicsDatabase.ts strips ALL non-alphanumerics including spaces and drops a leading 'the'; keyComicsDb.ts keeps spaces and does not drop 'the'; normalizeTitle.ts keeps spaces and hyphens.
- **2. Issue identified:** The static path and the Supabase path normalize titles differently, so a title matching in one will not match the other. If the DB fallback were ever wired into pricing, the two layers would key the same comic under different normalized strings. Latent correctness bug masked because pricing uses the static path exclusively today.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Standardize on one shared normalizeTitle for all key-comics matching and use it in keyComicsDb + the seed. Add a test asserting both paths normalize identically.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pure refactor + test.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/keyComicsDatabase.ts:37-43; src/lib/keyComicsDb.ts:26-32; src/lib/normalizeTitle.ts:2-8

#### [M-PR8] certLookup.ts (646 lines of fragile HTML-scraping regex) has zero tests, including detectGradingCompany
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · testing · effort M
- **1. Description:** The CGC/CBCS/PGX cert parsers in src/lib/certLookup.ts extract title, grade, year, etc. from raw HTML via long brittle regexes, and detectGradingCompany classifies a cert string to route the lookup. Only certHelpers.ts is tested.
- **2. Issue identified:** No tests for parseCGCResponse/parseCBCSResponse/parsePGXResponse or detectGradingCompany. detectGradingCompany is a pure classifier that decides which lookup path runs; a misclassification routes a cert to the wrong lookup. The parser regexes break silently when a grading site changes markup, feeding wrong/empty grades downstream.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add certLookup.test.ts with saved HTML fixtures per company (success + not-found + malformed), plus a table-driven detectGradingCompany test covering CGC, CBCS, PGX and ambiguous/null edge cases.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Parsers and detectGradingCompany are pure functions.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/certLookup.ts:90, 311, 491, 620-646; only src/lib/__tests__/certHelpers.test.ts exists

#### [M-PR9] cert-lookup scrapes grading sites with plain fetch + UA spoof, no JS-render/proxy fallback despite configured ZenRows
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · tech-debt · effort M
- **1. Description:** lookupCGCCert/CBCS/PGX fetch verification pages with a plain server-side fetch and a spoofed User-Agent, then regex the returned HTML.
- **2. Issue identified:** CGC/CBCS pages are increasingly Cloudflare-protected / client-rendered; a raw fetch can return a challenge or JS-shell page the regexes silently fail to parse. ZENROWS_API_KEY is configured for exactly this ('deferred post-launch'), but no proxy path exists, so cert-lookup reliability rides on un-proxied scraping.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Gate a ZenRows fallback behind the existing env var: on a challenge page or parse failure, retry through ZenRows. Keep it off by default but ready; at minimum add a code comment pointing at the deferred decision.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Neutral now; enabling ZenRows later adds metered proxy cost — keep gated/off until the ROI review _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Wiring is in-repo, but actually enabling ZenRows is an explicit deferred cost/ROI decision.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/certLookup.ts:55-57, 274-278, 459-461; CLAUDE.md ZenRows row

#### [M-PR10] Grade-skewed durable cache — see marketplace finding M-MK18
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** (Cross-listed) The convertBrowseToPriceData base-derivation interacts with a grade-omitting durable comic_metadata key, so the first grade looked up can fix a skewed base price for all grades.
- **2. Issue identified:** Detailed under M-MK18 — durable comic_metadata price-branch key omits grade while the eBay query injects grade, so the first lookup's grade can distort the persisted 9.4 base.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** See M-MK18 — include grade in the durable-cache decision or persist only near-9.4-derived bases; add a base-derivation test.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/ebayBrowse.ts:305-317; src/lib/cache.ts:161-163; src/app/api/con-mode-lookup/route.ts:277-298

#### [M-SC4] Guest scan limit is enforced inconsistently: server hard-caps at 5, client grants bonus scans up to 10
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** useGuestScans treats the guest limit as MAX_GUEST_SCANS (5) + bonusScans (5 after email capture) = totalLimit up to 10. addBonusScans is wired into scan/page.tsx. But /api/analyze hard-blocks any guest whose x-guest-scan-count header is >= GUEST_SCAN_LIMIT (5) with no awareness of bonus scans.
- **2. Issue identified:** A guest who earns 5 bonus scans via email capture will be told client-side they have scans remaining, but the server rejects scan #6 with guest_limit_reached. The bonus-scan reward appears functional in the UI yet is silently nullified by the server cap — broken UX that wastes the email-capture funnel.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Send the effective bonus allowance to the server with a trust mechanism (signed bonus token, or server-side tracking) and validate there, or at minimum align the server cap with the client totalLimit when bonus scans are present. Best solved alongside the server-side guest-tracking fix.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 direct; protects the email-capture conversion funnel _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Needs a product decision on how bonus scans are authenticated server-side.
- **Evidence:** src/hooks/useGuestScans.ts:10-11,161; src/app/scan/page.tsx:66,396; src/lib/subscription.ts:36; src/app/api/analyze/route.ts:240-251

#### [M-SC7] Remove the unused Haiku search-query call in cover-candidates — it produces a string that fetches nothing
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** POST /api/cover-candidates calls generateSearchQuery() (a Haiku messages.create call) to build an image-search query, then returns candidates: [] because no external image-search API is wired in.
- **2. Issue identified:** Every CSV-import cover-review fetch that misses the community DB spends a Haiku call (~$0.0008) plus two Redis writes (incr + expire) to generate a string that yields zero candidates. CoverReviewQueue stores the searchQuery but never uses it to fetch anything. Pure wasted spend and latency.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Remove generateSearchQuery() and the Haiku call entirely until an image-search backend exists; return candidates: [] (and the community result) directly. Also drops the SDK + Redis usage in this route.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves ~$0.0008/call + 2 Upstash commands/call on CSV-import cover reviews; small absolute dollars but 100% waste _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Self-contained dead-cost removal.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/cover-candidates/route.ts:49-60, :67-68, :101-107; src/components/CoverReviewQueue.tsx:70, :102

#### [M-SC8] Cover pipeline lacks the shouldRunPipeline gate, re-validating no-cover issues via a Gemini call on every scan
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** After identification, runCoverPipeline is called whenever the scan lacks coverImageUrl and has title+issue. For raw books it validates an eBay listing image with a Gemini vision call. Unlike con-mode-lookup (which gates with shouldRunPipeline), the analyze path has no such gate.
- **2. Issue identified:** The metadata cache carries coverImageUrl, so issues that previously found a cover are correctly skipped. BUT for issues where the pipeline previously ran and found NO cover (coverValidated=true, coverImageUrl=null), shouldRunPipeline applies a RETRY_AFTER_DAYS backoff — the analyze path ignores this and re-runs the full Gemini validation on EVERY scan of such issues.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Gate runCoverPipeline in the analyze route with shouldRunPipeline(cachedMetadata) exactly as con-mode-lookup does, so no-cover issues respect the retry backoff.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves ~1 Gemini vision call per repeat scan of an issue that previously found no cover; meaningful Gemini quota/cost reduction at scale _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Needs verification that the merged metadata reliably carries coverValidated/updatedAt on the analyze path before gating.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/analyze/route.ts:1136-1153; src/lib/coverValidation.ts:200-225, :431; src/app/api/con-mode-lookup/route.ts:208-212

#### [M-SC9] Skip redundant metadata re-save when scan was a cache hit
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** After every scan the route writes the comic metadata to BOTH Supabase and Redis, guarded only by `if (savePayload)`.
- **2. Issue identified:** The final save block runs Promise.all([saveComicMetadata(...), cacheSet(...)]) with no check on metadataCacheHit. When the non-slab cache-hit path merges cached data and falls through to this block, it performs a redundant Supabase upsert plus a redundant Upstash write of data just read from cache. With Upstash on the 10K commands/day free cap, an extra guaranteed write per cache-hit scan is wasteful churn.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Wrap the save in `if (!metadataCacheHit)` (or diff the payload against the cached value) so it only writes when the data is new or changed.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves ~1 Upstash write + 1 Supabase upsert per non-slab cache-hit scan; eases the Upstash 10K/day free cap. $0 today _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/analyze/route.ts:1160-1174; metadataCacheHit set at :526 and :972

#### [M-SC10] Add Anthropic prompt caching to the vision scan calls
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** The Anthropic image-analysis and slab prompts are large static text blocks sent verbatim on every API call with no cache_control marker.
- **2. Issue identified:** IMAGE_ANALYSIS_PROMPT is ~9.2KB (~2.3K input tokens) of fixed instructions, plus SLAB prompts, all re-sent on every scan with no cache_control: { type: 'ephemeral' } block. Two caveats: (1) Gemini is the configured primary, so Anthropic is only hit on fallback; (2) the message places the per-scan image block BEFORE the static text, so the prompt must be reordered (static text first) for the prefix to cache.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Reorder so the static instruction prefix comes first, mark it with cache_control: { type: 'ephemeral' }, and place the per-scan image bytes after. The instruction prefix then becomes a cache hit on the 2nd+ Anthropic call within the 5-minute window. Best done alongside the SDK bump.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves ~$0.001-0.0015 per Anthropic-fallback scan once warmed (~90% discount on the static prefix). Applies only when Anthropic is used (fallback today), so modest at current Gemini-primary config _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — SDK parameter + message-ordering change; verify with a token-count test.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/providers/anthropic.ts:25, :234-262, :305-334, :350-385; src/lib/models.ts:17

#### [M-SC11] Plan a major-version bump of @anthropic-ai/sdk (0.52 -> 0.104)
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · dependencies · effort M
- **1. Description:** @anthropic-ai/sdk is installed at 0.52.0 while the latest is 0.104.1 — roughly 50 minor releases behind on the SDK that drives the paid Sonnet cover-scan feature.
- **2. Issue identified:** Sitting this far behind misses newer model aliases, prompt-caching ergonomics, and bug/perf fixes. The version gap also widens the eventual forced-upgrade burden.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Review the @anthropic-ai/sdk changelog for breaking changes, bump to current 0.x, re-run anthropic.test.ts, then layer in cache_control on the static prompt prefix.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Neutral on its own (the SDK bump enables the separate prompt-caching saving but adds no cost) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — SDK bump is autonomous; verifying live vision-scan behavior against the new SDK needs a human test pass with a real API key.
- **Evidence:** package.json:31; installed 0.52.0, latest 0.104.1; src/lib/providers/__tests__/anthropic.test.ts exists

#### [M-SC12] estimateScanCostCents miscounts cost when cheap calls (slab detection, low-conf fallback) are made
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** estimateScanCostCents treats aiCallsMade>=1 as one full imageAnalysis cost and every additional call as a 'verification' cost. But the route increments aiCallsMade for slab detection (cheap), slab detail extraction, and the low-confidence re-scan (a full imageAnalysis).
- **2. Issue identified:** Cost analytics are systematically wrong on slab and low-confidence paths. A cert-first scan doing slabDetection + slabDetailExtraction is billed 2.1c when true Anthropic cost is ~0.7c. A low-confidence raw scan doing TWO full imageAnalysis calls is billed 2.1c when it spent ~3.0c. This corrupts COST_PROJECTIONS and any cost dashboards.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Pass an explicit per-call-type breakdown into estimateScanCostCents instead of a flat aiCallsMade, summing via the provider's estimateCostCents constants. The route already tracks slabDetectionMeta/slabDetailExtractionMeta — feed those in.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — accuracy of cost telemetry only, but unblocks accurate cost decisions _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pure in-repo refactor; per-call cost constants already exist in providers.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/analyticsServer.ts:60-81; src/app/api/analyze/route.ts:397, :550, :683

#### [M-SC13] p1 provider label is overwritten by slab detection, mislabeling cost provider on the cert-first path
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** p1 starts as the configured primary, is reassigned to slabProvider after slab detection, then to imageProvider after imageAnalysis — but the imageProvider reassignment lives inside if (!certFirstPath). On the cert-first path imageAnalysis never runs, so p1 stays as the slab-detection provider.
- **2. Issue identified:** Provider attribution in scan_analytics and PostHog can be wrong when slab detection and the main analysis are served by different providers. Combined with the flat cost model, provider-level cost reporting is unreliable.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Track provider per call (slabProvider, imageProvider, verifyProvider) rather than a single mutable p1, and compute the dominant/most-expensive provider for the summary field — or report the breakdown.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — telemetry accuracy only _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — per-call provider data already captured in slabDetectionMeta/callDetails.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/analyze/route.ts:368, :404, :636, :1184 & :1219

#### [M-SC14] barcode-reviews PATCH performs multi-table writes without a transaction or error checks
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** On approve/correct/reject, the PATCH handler updates admin_barcode_reviews and then separately updates or deletes the linked barcode_catalog row.
- **2. Issue identified:** The two writes are independent awaited statements with no transaction, and NONE of the .update()/.delete() return values are checked for error. If the second write fails, the review is marked resolved while the catalog row is left in the wrong state. Per the project's own debugging notes, a Supabase update matching zero rows returns no error — so silent inconsistency in the community barcode catalog is possible.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Wrap the paired writes in a Postgres function/RPC (transactional), or at minimum check the error/returned rows of each write and flag/roll back on failure.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — correctness/data-integrity _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — A clean transactional fix is a DB RPC requiring a migration; the error-check fallback is fully in-repo.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/admin/barcode-reviews/route.ts:127-199

#### [M-SC15] Broad empty catch {} in import-lookup silently swallows AI parse failures while still billing a scan
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** import-lookup parses Claude's JSON response inside a try block whose outer catch is `} catch {}` — fully empty.
- **2. Issue identified:** The outer catch {} swallows any error from JSON.parse, the regex match, or array handling with zero logging. A malformed AI response fails silently — the endpoint returns degraded data while still recording a scan/cost. There is no log signal to diagnose why enrichment stopped working.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace } catch {} with } catch (err) { console.error('[import-lookup] parse failed:', err); } so failures are observable in Sentry/console. The cosmetic empty catches at cover-candidates/route.ts:69 and CustomProfilePage.tsx:378 could be logged for consistency.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — One-line change.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/import-lookup/route.ts:113-151, scan recorded at 154-159; cover-candidates/route.ts:69; CustomProfilePage.tsx:378

#### [M-SC16] Make the desktop drag-and-drop upload zone keyboard-operable
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · accessibility · effort S
- **1. Description:** ImageUpload's desktop path is a div with onClick that opens a hidden (className="hidden") file input via ref.
- **2. Issue identified:** The drop-zone div is not focusable (no tabIndex/role/onKeyDown) and the file input is display:none which removes it from the tab order, so desktop keyboard users cannot initiate an upload from this zone. Scanning is a core flow.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Make the drop zone a real button (or role="button" tabIndex={0} with Enter/Space handler), and use a visually-hidden (sr-only) rather than display:none file input. The mobile path already uses real buttons.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Self-contained in ImageUpload.tsx.
- **Evidence:** src/components/ImageUpload.tsx:283-302; mobile real buttons at ~:224 and ~:246

#### [M-SC17] Dynamically import CSVImport on the scan page
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · bundle · effort S
- **1. Description:** The scan page statically imports CSVImport (679 lines), which is only rendered when showCSVImport is true — a bulk-entry feature most scanning sessions never use.
- **2. Issue identified:** CSVImport plus its dependency chain is loaded into the scan route's initial bundle even though the core scan flow (camera/upload) is the common path. CSVImport is imported by only this one file and the render is gated by showCSVImport state.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Wrap CSVImport in next/dynamic with ssr:false and a small loading state so its code downloads only after the user clicks 'Import CSV'.

- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · documentation · effort S
- **1. Description:** docs/plans/2026-02-25-cover-image-search-design.md and -plan.md specify a cover-image architecture built on the Google Custom Search JSON API and Anthropic Claude (Haiku) for query generation.

Issue identified:** That architecture was abandoned — 2026-02-26-bing-image-search-design.md records a 'Decision: Skip External Image Search' and the shipped route confirms 'No external image search API available.' Yet both Feb-25 docs still read 'Status: Approved' with full Google CSE code — unlike the sibling harvesting design doc which correctly carries a 'Superseded by ...' banner. A future reader could rebuild a dead, paid Google CSE path.

- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · documentation · effort S
- **1. Description:** docs/plans/2026-03-01-scan-resilience-plan.md is the implementation plan for multi-provider scan fallback. It specifies building an OpenAIProvider with OPENAI_API_KEY (83 OpenAI/GPT-4o mentions, 0 Gemini mentions).

#### [M-SO3] followDb.test.ts tests a stale re-implementation, not the real code
- **Area / Dimension:** social · testing · effort S
- **1. Description:** The followDb test file re-declares its own buildDisplayName and transformToFollowUser locally rather than importing from followDb.ts.
- **2. Issue identified:** The test's local buildDisplayName builds the name from first_name + last_name and its ProfileRow has first_name/last_name fields. The real followDb.ts buildDisplayName returns only profile.display_name and ProfileRow has no first_name/last_name. The tests therefore validate dead logic that does not match production and would still pass even if the real transform broke — false-confidence coverage.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Export buildDisplayName/transformToFollowUser from followDb.ts and import them in the test, or rewrite the fixtures to use display_name and assert the real exported functions. Remove the obsolete first_name/last_name fixtures.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — test-only change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/__tests__/followDb.test.ts:11-32; src/lib/followDb.ts:27-32, :46-48

#### [M-SO4] Blocked-users endpoint does N+1 profile fetches with over-selecting query
- **Area / Dimension:** social · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** GET /api/users/blocked loads all user_blocks rows then calls getSellerProfile(block.blocked_id) once per block inside Promise.all to enrich each block.
- **2. Issue identified:** Each getSellerProfile is a separate Supabase round-trip; a user blocking N people triggers N+1 queries. Promise.all parallelizes but still fans out N round-trips, each selecting the wide seller-profile column set (12 columns) when a blocked-list display only needs id, username, display_name, avatar.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace the per-block loop with a single .in('id', blockedIds) query against profiles selecting only the display fields, then map results back.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — fewer Supabase queries (lower DB load at scale) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/users/blocked/route.ts:32-43; src/lib/auctionDb.ts:2334-2341

#### [M-SO5] getFollowers/getFollowing issue a redundant unbounded following-IDs scan per page
- **Area / Dimension:** social · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** For an authenticated viewer, getFollowers and getFollowing call getFollowingIds(currentUserId) which selects ALL of the viewer's following rows to build an in-memory Set for the isFollowing flag.
- **2. Issue identified:** On a user who follows thousands of people, every paginated followers/following request pulls the viewer's entire following list (unbounded SELECT following_id) just to flag ~20 rows on the current page. This grows with the viewer's follow graph, not the page size, and runs on each Load More.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Scope the isFollowing lookup to the page: after fetching the page's profile rows, query user_follows where follower_id = currentUserId AND following_id IN (pageIds).
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — smaller, bounded queries _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/followDb.ts:189, :245, :298-309

The classes compile, so this is not a broken-token bug, but the mobile shell visually diverges from the desktop pop-art shell, so the two surfaces look like different apps — a gap against the project's own 'Lichtenstein Design Language' standard.

#### [M-TE1] Expand jest collectCoverageFrom beyond 3 files so coverage reports are meaningful
- **Area / Dimension:** testing · testing · effort S
- **1. Description:** jest.config.js limits collectCoverageFrom to three files: src/types/auction.ts, src/lib/subscription.ts, src/hooks/useGuestScans.ts.

The reported coverage number is misleadingly narrow and gives no visibility into gaps across src/lib (pricing, auctions, stripeConnect, messaging, etc.), undercutting the project's mandatory-test policy.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Broaden collectCoverageFrom to ['src/lib/**/*.ts','src/types/**/*.ts','src/hooks/**/*.ts'] with sensible excludes (__tests__, *.d.ts).
