### 🟢 Low Priority

#### [L-AD1] User-facing publisher-suggestion endpoint lives under /api/admin namespace
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** POST /api/admin/publishers inserts a 'Publisher suggestion' row into key_info_submissions with the caller's own user_id and status='pending'. It is invoked from a user-facing form when a typed publisher isn't in the dropdown.
- **2. Issue identified:** Despite living under /api/admin/, this is intentionally a user feature — it only checks auth() for a userId and writes the user's OWN suggestion. There is no real privilege escalation, but the placement breaks the otherwise-consistent contract that everything under /api/admin requires is_admin, which is confusing and could mask a genuine gap during future audits.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Move the route out of /api/admin/ to a non-admin path (e.g. /api/publishers/suggest) so the namespace contract holds. Update the single caller in ComicDetailsForm.tsx.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Single route file + single caller.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/admin/publishers/route.ts:13-30; caller at src/components/ComicDetailsForm.tsx:679

#### [L-AD2] Audit log spammed with read-only view events, drowning real actions
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · tech-debt · effort S
- **1. Description:** Read-only admin operations are logged: message-reports GET logs 'view_reports' on every load, and users/[id] GET logs 'view_profile' on every fetch.
- **2. Issue identified:** The users page calls loadUserDetails() again after every mutating action, so a single trial reset produces extra view_profile rows alongside the reset_trial row. Combined with the missing logs on actual mutations, the audit log is mostly low-value 'views' and missing high-value writes — the opposite of an accountability log — and grows the storage-constrained DB.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Stop logging plain GET/view events (or gate them behind a debug flag) and invest audit volume in the mutating actions instead.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves trivial Supabase DB growth on the 500MB free tier; primarily a quality fix _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/admin/message-reports/route.ts:57; src/app/api/admin/users/[id]/route.ts:39; re-fetch at src/app/admin/users/page.tsx:214,238,268

#### [L-AD3] Admin audit-log writes fail silently (accountability gap)
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** logAdminAction in adminAuth.ts inserts into admin_audit_log but only console.errors on failure; admin mutations rely on it for accountability.
- **2. Issue identified:** Per the project's own documented lesson, a Supabase .insert that errors can fail silently. logAdminAction swallows the error with only console.error, so a privileged admin action can complete with no audit trail.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Route logAdminAction failures to Sentry (already integrated), and consider failing the request for high-impact actions (suspend, grant_premium) if the audit insert errors.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Sentry is already integrated.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/adminAuth.ts:99-110

#### [L-AD4] flagged-users route runs an N+1 strike-count query per user
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** GET /api/admin/flagged-users lists bid-restricted users, then enriches each with a count of payment-expired strikes in the rolling 90-day window.
- **2. Issue identified:** It does this with Promise.all over the page (up to 50 users), issuing one auction_audit_log count query per user — a classic N+1. Up to 51 Supabase round-trips for one page load. Bounded by page size, but avoidable.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace the per-user loop with a single grouped query (one auction_audit_log query filtered to the page's actor_profile_ids and event_type within the window, aggregated in-memory or via a GROUP BY RPC).
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — reduces Supabase query volume per admin page load _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/admin/flagged-users/route.ts:76-98

#### [L-AD5] grant-premium grants access via trial fields, mislabeling user as 'trialing'
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** Admin 'Grant Premium' calls grantPremiumAccess(), which sets trial_started_at and trial_ends_at to a future date but never touches subscription_tier.
- **2. Issue identified:** Subscription logic treats trial_ends_at>now as isTrialing => unlimited scans, so this functionally grants premium-equivalent access. But subscription_tier stays 'free' and the user appears as 'trialing', not 'premium'. Granting to an existing paid premium user, or one mid-trial, overwrites their trial dates.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Either set an explicit comp/grant flag so UI and access reflect a true grant, or document that 'grant premium' issues a comped trial window and guard against overwriting an active paid subscription.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — could prevent accidentally resetting/extending access state for paying users _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Cleanest fix may add a comp flag/column (DB migration) and needs a product decision on grant semantics.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/adminAuth.ts:238-258; access logic at src/lib/subscription.ts:71,95-101

#### [L-AD6] Service limit constants and date-window logic duplicated across three usage routes
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · tech-debt · effort M
- **1. Description:** LIMITS, ALERT_THRESHOLDS, the Netlify account-id-hardcoded fetch, the bytes formatter, and the month/week boundary math are copy-pasted across usage/route.ts, check-alerts/route.ts, and alert-status/route.ts.
- **2. Issue identified:** Three sources of truth drift independently — alert-status's LIMITS omits cover/netlify, while two routes hardcode the Netlify account id inline. A limit change must be made in 3+ places, and the hardcoded account id should be an env var.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Extract LIMITS, ALERT_THRESHOLDS, the Netlify account id (to env), the bytes formatter, and the boundary helpers into a shared module (e.g. src/lib/usageLimits.ts). Add unit tests for the boundary math.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — maintainability; reduces risk of mis-set limits causing wrong cost alerts _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** usage/route.ts:11-35, check-alerts/route.ts:19-29, alert-status/route.ts:10-14; hardcoded id at usage/route.ts:351 and check-alerts/route.ts:190

#### [L-AD7] coverHeadCheck follows redirects on HEAD to arbitrary hosts (SSRF surface)
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · security · effort S
- **1. Description:** The cron HEAD-check fetches each stored cover image_url with default fetch settings.
- **2. Issue identified:** coverHeadCheck.ts uses fetch(row.image_url, { method: 'HEAD', signal: ... }) with no redirect:'manual' and no host allowlist. image_url values are sanitized on write and sourced from eBay/community submissions, so risk is low, but a malicious community submission that slips past review could point at an internal address, and redirects are followed automatically.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Set redirect:'manual' (or 'error') on the HEAD fetch and/or validate the host against an allowlist before fetching.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — security hardening _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/coverHeadCheck.ts:71-74; src/lib/coverImageUrlSanitizer.ts:31-59

#### [L-AD8] Replace three separate count queries with one grouped count in submission stats
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · database · effort S
- **1. Description:** getSubmissionCounts runs three parallel count:exact head queries against key_info_submissions, one per status.
- **2. Issue identified:** Three round-trips where one GROUP BY status would return all counts. Admin-only and parallelized, so impact is small, but it triples count-query load on every admin dashboard refresh.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Use a single query (select status, count, group by status) via an RPC or a single fetch + JS tally.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 direct; saves negligible Supabase query volume on admin refresh _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — A JS-tally rewrite is autonomous; a true GROUP BY count needs an RPC migration.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/keyComicsDb.ts:470-483

#### [L-AD9] searchKeyComics uses leading-wildcard ilike that bypasses the index
- **Area / Dimension:** admin · database · effort M
- **1. Description:** Admin key-comics search filters with .ilike('title_normalized', '%' + normalized + '%'), a leading-wildcard pattern.
- **2. Issue identified:** A leading % prevents the btree index idx_key_comics_lookup from being used, forcing a sequential scan of key_comics. Admin-only so low impact today, but degrades as the catalog grows.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** If prefix search suffices, drop the leading wildcard. For substring search, add a pg_trgm GIN index on title_normalized.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 direct; saves Supabase compute on admin search as the catalog grows _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — The code change is autonomous; the pg_trgm GIN index requires a DB migration.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/keyComicsDb.ts:539-541; index at supabase/migrations/20250117_key_info_community.sql:26-27

#### [L-AU10] Cancel-auction relies on cached bid_count instead of a real bid existence check
- **Area / Dimension:** auctions · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** cancelAuction blocks cancellation of auctions with bids by reading the denormalized auctions.bid_count column.
- **2. Issue identified:** bid_count is incremented in placeBid via a non-atomic read-modify-write, so concurrent bids can lose increments and understate the count. If bid_count drifts to 0 while real bids exist, a seller could cancel an auction that actually has bidders, voiding their bids.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Gate cancellation on an EXISTS check against the bids table (select id … limit 1, cheap given idx_bids_auction_id) rather than the cached counter, or maintain bid_count atomically.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/auctionDb.ts:618-621, :937

#### [L-AU11] Batch sequential notification creation in auction loops
- **Area / Dimension:** auctions · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** auctionDb.ts creates notifications inside for-loops with serial awaits when an auction/listing resolves: notifying all pending-offer buyers on cancellation, and all losing bidders when an auction ends.
- **2. Issue identified:** For an auction with many bidders/offers, each createNotification is awaited one at a time. These run in background cron so user-facing latency is low, but cron execution time and DB round-trips scale linearly with bidder count. The same file already uses the correct parallel pattern elsewhere.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Collect notification rows and issue them via Promise.all (or a single batched insert). Verify notification ordering isn't relied upon.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — faster cron execution / fewer serialized DB round-trips _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/auctionDb.ts:643, :2513; correct pattern at :3570-3571

#### [L-AU12] Trim auction barrel index.ts of dead/direct-import entries
- **Area / Dimension:** auctions · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** src/components/auction/index.ts re-exports 15 components as a barrel, but most are dead or imported directly from their files.
- **2. Issue identified:** Only AuctionCard, AuctionDetailModal, ListingCard, ListingDetailModal are consumed via the barrel. The other 11 barrel entries are either dead or imported directly from their files, not the barrel.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** After deleting the dead modal components (H-AU4), reduce the barrel to only the four components consumed via it, or remove the barrel entirely in favor of direct imports.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/components/auction/index.ts:1-15; barrel consumers shop/page.tsx:11-17, my-auctions/page.tsx:12-18, watchlist/page.tsx:11

#### [L-AU13] Consolidate duplicate default+named exports on Second Chance components
- **Area / Dimension:** auctions · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** SecondChanceInboxCard.tsx and SecondChanceOfferButton.tsx each export both a named export and a default export of the same component.
- **2. Issue identified:** Both components have a named export AND a default export. This already causes divergence at call sites: transactions/page.tsx imports SecondChanceInboxCard as DEFAULT, while AuctionDetailModal.tsx imports SecondChanceOfferButton as NAMED. Dual exports create import ambiguity and one of the two is always dead.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Pick one export style per component (the project predominantly uses named exports). Drop the default export and update the single consumer of each.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** SecondChanceInboxCard.tsx:47 & :165; SecondChanceOfferButton.tsx:26 & :111; consumers diverge: transactions/page.tsx:16, AuctionDetailModal.tsx:36

#### [L-CO23] Select All selects only the visible subset, and stale hidden selections persist across filter changes
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · ux · effort S
- **1. Description:** Selection "Select All" operates on visibleIds (the filtered/sorted list), not the whole collection, and selectedIds is never pruned when filters change.
- **2. Issue identified:** selectAllVisible is fed filteredCollection ids, so with an active filter "Select All" silently selects only the matching subset with no clarifying copy. Worse, there is no effect that prunes selectedIds when filters change, and bulk handlers act on Array.from(selectedIds) directly — so a user can select items, change the filter to hide them, and a bulk delete/sold/list action still applies to the now-hidden ids.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Label the action "Select all N visible", and prune selectedIds (or show a count of selected-but-hidden items) whenever the filtered set changes.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/collection/page.tsx:329, 804-805, 440; src/lib/bulkActions.ts:36-39

#### [L-CO24] show_financials preference fetched via a dedicated request on every collection mount
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** The collection page fires GET /api/settings/preferences on mount solely to read the showFinancials boolean.
- **2. Issue identified:** This is a separate network round-trip per visit to /collection just to learn one boolean. It is independent of the main collection fetch, adds latency, and causes a flicker because financial cards default to true then may flip after the request resolves.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Fold showFinancials into the existing collection/profile load (return it alongside collection data, or read from a cached user-settings context).
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves a small number of Supabase/API requests per page view; negligible $ now _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/collection/page.tsx:174-187, :109, :180

#### [L-CO25] updateCollectionItem/removeFromCollection callbacks depend on full collection array, defeating useCallback
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** Two useCollection mutation callbacks close over the entire collection array for rollback snapshots.
- **2. Issue identified:** updateCollectionItem and removeFromCollection list `collection` in their useCallback deps to capture prevCollection/removedItem for rollback. This recreates the callbacks on every collection change, cascading re-renders in consumers that rely on referential stability.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Capture the previous state inside setCollection(prev => ...) for both the optimistic update and rollback, removing `collection` from the dependency arrays.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/hooks/useCollection.ts:171-194, :196-219

#### [L-CO26] Unused removeFromCollection destructure in collection page
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** The collection page destructures removeFromCollection from useCollection but never calls it.
- **2. Issue identified:** removeFromCollection is pulled out yet all deletions go through the bulk-delete fetch in handleRemove. The dead binding is misleading — it suggests the hard-delete hook path is used when the page actually soft-deletes.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Remove the unused destructure (or prefix with _) to make the deletion path unambiguous and pass the lint/deadcode gate.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/collection/page.tsx:87, :476

#### [L-CO27] Bulk endpoint business rules have no test coverage despite mandatory test policy
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · testing · effort M
- **1. Description:** Pure helpers (bulkActions, csvParsing, statsCalculator, etc.) are tested, but the bulk endpoints' extractable business rules are not.
- **2. Issue identified:** The bulk-delete deletable-vs-active-listing partitioning and the bulk-add-to-list duplicate-skip logic are real business rules with no unit coverage. CLAUDE.md mandates tests for business logic; the project defers API-route tests, but these rules are extractable into tested pure helpers.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Extract the bulk-delete partitioning and bulk-add dedup logic into pure functions in src/lib and unit-test edge cases (all-active-listings, partial ownership, all-duplicates, empty input).
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/comics/bulk-delete/route.ts:50-68; src/app/api/comics/bulk-add-to-list/route.ts:62-78

#### [L-CO28] storage.ts assumes a fixed 5MB localStorage quota for warnings
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** getStorageUsage hardcodes total = 5MB and computes percentage/warning against it.
- **2. Issue identified:** Browsers vary (Safari ~5MB, Chrome up to ~10MB, private mode much less). The warning and the StorageQuotaError messaging will be inaccurate on devices that deviate. Guest collections living entirely in localStorage are the at-risk group.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Use navigator.storage.estimate() when available to get the real quota, falling back to the 5MB constant. Keep the QuotaExceededError catch as the hard backstop.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — guest UX accuracy _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/storage.ts:18-35, 59-71

#### [L-CO29] offlineCache uses deprecated String.prototype.substr for ID generation
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** Offline-queue and history entry IDs are built with Math.random().toString(36).substr(2, 9).
- **2. Issue identified:** .substr() is a deprecated (Annex B) string method. It still works but is flagged by linters; crypto.randomUUID() is already used elsewhere.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace the timestamp+substr ID scheme with crypto.randomUUID() (or .slice(2, 11)) in both addToOfflineQueue and addToKeyHuntHistory.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — code hygiene _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/offlineCache.ts:235, 370; src/lib/coverHarvest.ts:210

#### [L-CO30] offlineCache getCachedLookup writes to localStorage on every read
- **Area / Dimension:** collection · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** getCachedLookup updates lastAccessed and re-serializes the entire lookup cache to localStorage on every cache hit.
- **2. Issue identified:** Each read does removeExpiredItems + find + saveCachedLookups (full JSON.stringify) purely to bump an LRU timestamp. On rapid Con/Key-Hunt browsing this is repeated synchronous main-thread serialization for a non-critical metadata update.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Debounce or batch the lastAccessed write (only persist on cacheLookup or on a timer), or skip the write if the timestamp delta is small.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — client CPU on mobile _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/offlineCache.ts:100-114

#### [L-CC20] isUserSuspended adds an uncached duplicate profile fetch on watchlist routes
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** Watchlist (and other) routes call getProfileByClerkId to resolve Clerk id to the internal profile, then separately call isUserSuspended(userId).
- **2. Issue identified:** getProfileByClerkId is Redis-cached, but isUserSuspended issues a separate, UNCACHED supabaseAdmin read of the same profiles row on every watchlist GET/POST/DELETE, doubling the per-request profile lookup. (is_suspended could be read off the already-cached profile.)
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Fold is_suspended/suspended_reason into the cached profile returned by getProfileByClerkId and check it from there, or cache isUserSuspended's result.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — negligible; one fewer Supabase read per watchlist mutation _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/db.ts:65-86; src/lib/adminAuth.ts:290-308; src/app/api/watchlist/route.ts:46,79

#### [L-CC21] Profile reads use select(*) on the wide profiles table and cache the full row
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · database · effort S
- **1. Description:** getOrCreateProfile, getProfileByClerkId, and getPublicProfile all use select("*") on the profiles table. getProfileByClerkId additionally caches the entire wide row in Redis under a 5-minute TTL.
- **2. Issue identified:** The profiles table has grown wide (stripe ids, Connect fields, location, reputation, moderation flags). select(*) on hot/public paths fetches more than needed; getProfileByClerkId caches the full row even though CachedProfile enumerates only ~22 fields. At scale this increases Supabase egress and Upstash cache payload size.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace select("*") with explicit column lists matching the CachedProfile/PublicProfile shapes.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 direct; saves marginal Supabase egress and Upstash cache payload size at scale _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/db.ts:14, :73-87, :40-63, :778

#### [L-CC22] user.updated webhook can null out a valid username on Clerk-rejected payloads
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** On user.updated, the handler sets updates.username = sanitizeUsername(username) whenever username !== undefined, including when sanitizeUsername returns null.
- **2. Issue identified:** Supabase is source of truth for usernames. But if a user.updated event arrives with a username Clerk allows but Supabase rejects, sanitizeUsername returns null and the webhook overwrites the user's valid Supabase username with NULL. Edge case (requires a Clerk-side username edit failing the regex).
- **3. Recommended optimization:** On user.updated, only write username when sanitizeUsername returns a non-null value (skip the field otherwise).
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — correctness fix _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/webhooks/clerk/route.ts:143, :37-41

#### [L-CC23] getOrCreateProfile bypasses the profile Redis cache
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** getProfileByClerkId reads/writes a 5-minute Redis cache, but getOrCreateProfile always hits Postgres directly with a fresh SELECT.
- **2. Issue identified:** getOrCreateProfile does a select on every call with no cache check. Used in several write-path routes (bulk-add/delete/update, billing/checkout, undo-delete, useCollection). The real win is avoiding a Supabase SELECT on each separate authenticated mutation request.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Have getOrCreateProfile attempt cacheGet first (reusing the 'profile' prefix), populate the cache on create, and invalidate via invalidateProfileCache on email backfill. Weigh against freshness risk in write paths.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves a small number of Supabase reads across separate auth requests; marginal at beta volume _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/db.ts:10-38, 65-87

#### [L-CC24] incrementComicLookupCount silently no-ops when the row does not exist
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · database · effort S
- **1. Description:** incrementComicLookupCount issues a Supabase .update() touching updated_at to fire a DB trigger that increments lookup_count.
- **2. Issue identified:** A Supabase .update() with no matching row returns no error. If the metadata row was never created (e.g. lookup before saveComicMetadata), the increment silently does nothing and analytics undercount, with no signal.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Either upsert-then-increment, or check the returned row count and log a breadcrumb when zero rows are affected. Add a unit test for the no-row case.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — analytics correctness _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/db.ts:640-650

#### [L-CC25] Redis cache uses GET+SET round trips with no batching on hot paths
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** Every cache miss in cacheGetOrSet performs one Upstash GET then one SET (2 commands); profile lookups do a GET per authenticated request.
- **2. Issue identified:** Upstash free tier is 10K commands/day shared between rate-limiting AND caching. The profile cache costs 1 command per authenticated request even on a hit; analyze/ebay paths cost 2 on miss. Fine at beta volume, but there is no command-budget analysis and no pipelining for multi-key reads.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Where multiple keys are read in one request, use redis.pipeline()/mget. Document the per-request command budget so the 10K/day cap is monitored.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Upstash commands at scale; defers paid Upstash tier _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Pipelining is autonomous; the budget/monitoring threshold needs product input.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/cache.ts:52-90; src/lib/db.ts:65-87; src/lib/rateLimit.ts:16-56

#### [L-CC26] Batch PostHog server events instead of flushing one-per-event
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** The server-side PostHog client is configured with flushAt: 1 and flushInterval: 0.
- **2. Issue identified:** flushAt:1 / flushInterval:0 forces an immediate individual HTTP request to PostHog per captured event. Each scan triggers a server event, so this is one extra synchronous network round-trip per scan. PostHog's free tier is generous, so there is no dollar cost; the impact is per-request latency.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Raise flushAt (e.g. 20) and use waitUntil/ctx-deferred flush where available; confirm events still deliver in the serverless model.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change (within PostHog free tier); reduces per-scan network overhead _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Config change in analyticsServer.ts; validate event delivery in serverless context.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/analyticsServer.ts:9-10

#### [L-CC27] coverImageUrlSanitizer accepts any http/https host with no allowlist
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · security · effort S
- **1. Description:** sanitizeCoverImageUrl validates scheme, length, and query-string length but does not restrict the host.
- **2. Issue identified:** Any http(s) URL under 2048 chars passes, so an attacker-controlled cover_image_url could embed a tracking pixel or point Stripe product images / public collection renders at an arbitrary host. The sanitizer is the documented single write-boundary guard, so it is the right place to enforce a host policy. It also still permits http:// in production.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add an optional host allowlist (Supabase storage domain, ebayimg.com, openlibrary covers) to the sanitizer, or at minimum reject non-https in production.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — security hardening _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Needs product confirmation of the full set of legitimate cover host domains before enforcing an allowlist.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/coverImageUrlSanitizer.ts:28-59

#### [L-CC28] db.ts transform functions rely on ~34 unchecked `as` casts with no test coverage
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · tech-debt · effort M
- **1. Description:** transformDbComicToCollectionItem and transformCollectionItemToDbComic cast every Supabase column with `as string`/`as boolean`/etc from Record<string, unknown>.
- **2. Issue identified:** Dozens of unchecked casts mean a schema/column rename produces undefined at runtime with no type error. No src/lib/__tests__/db.test.ts exists, so the transform round-trip is untested despite CLAUDE.md mandating tests for transform functions.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Generate Supabase types or add a Zod schema for the comics row and parse in the transform. At minimum add a unit test covering transformDbComicToCollectionItem.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — type safety / regression prevention _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/db.ts:656-742; no src/lib/__tests__/db.test.ts present

#### [L-CC29] No unit tests for rateLimit or notificationsCache helpers
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · testing · effort M
- **1. Description:** Several in-scope infra libs with branching logic have no test files.
- **2. Issue identified:** src/lib/__tests__ has tests for cache, concurrency, coverImageDb, etc., but NONE for rateLimit.ts (getRateLimitIdentifier fallback chain) or notificationsCache.ts (version-mismatch discard, per-profile keying). CLAUDE.md mandates tests for business-logic helpers.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add focused tests: getRateLimitIdentifier(userId/ip/anonymous) precedence; notificationsCache version-mismatch returns null and removes the key.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — regression safety _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/rateLimit.ts:108-117; src/lib/notificationsCache.ts:48-64; no tests for either

#### [L-CC30] keyComicsDb.ts re-creates its own Supabase admin client instead of reusing supabaseAdmin
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** src/lib/supabase.ts exports a singleton supabaseAdmin service-role client used by other lib modules.
- **2. Issue identified:** keyComicsDb.ts imports createClient directly and constructs its own service-role client from process.env, duplicating env-var boilerplate and creating a second admin client instance.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace the local createClient block with import { supabaseAdmin } from './supabase' to match the established pattern.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/keyComicsDb.ts:12, :18-23; src/lib/supabase.ts:19-20

#### [L-CC31] Mock @/lib/supabase in DB-helper tests to stop live GoTrueClient instantiation and warning spam
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · testing · effort M
- **1. Description:** src/lib/supabase.ts creates two live Supabase auth (GoTrue) clients at module load. Many test files import DB helpers that pull this in without mocking it.
- **2. Issue identified:** supabase.ts instantiates clients eagerly at import time with no lazy init or test guard. Only 4 of 45 lib test files mock supabase; the rest construct live clients, producing repeated 'Multiple GoTrueClient instances detected' warnings. (An earlier claim of an intermittent SIGSEGV crash could NOT be reproduced.) The real issue is log-noise and minor per-worker overhead.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Either add a default jest.mock('@/lib/supabase') shim in jest.setup.js, or make supabase.ts lazily create clients via a getter. Standardize mocking @/lib/supabase in DB-helper tests.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — In-repo setup/mocking change.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/supabase.ts:8 and :19-21; analyticsServer.test.ts imports analyticsServer (no supabase mock); only 4/45 lib test files mock supabase

#### [L-CC32] Replace deprecated Sentry disableLogger option in next.config.mjs
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · tech-debt · effort S
- **1. Description:** next.config.mjs passes disableLogger: true to withSentryConfig to tree-shake Sentry logger statements.
- **2. Issue identified:** In the installed Sentry SDK v10, disableLogger is marked @deprecated with the note 'Use webpack.treeshake.removeDebugLogging instead.' This emits a build-time deprecation warning and will break when the option is removed in a future major.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace `disableLogger: true` with `webpack: { treeshake: { removeDebugLogging: true } }`. Pair with the @sentry/nextjs within-major bump.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** next.config.mjs:33; node_modules/@sentry/nextjs/build/types/config/types.d.ts:487-489

#### [L-CC33] Reduce repeated auth()+401 scaffolding across API routes with a shared guard
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · tech-debt · effort M
- **1. Description:** API route handlers under src/app/api each begin with the same Clerk auth() call and 401 response boilerplate.
- **2. Issue identified:** 53 route files repeat 'const { userId } = await auth()' and there are 95 'status: 401' sites across src/app/api. The pattern is copy-pasted, so error message/shape drift is possible.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Introduce a small helper (requireUser() or a withAuth(handler) wrapper) in src/lib and adopt it incrementally.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** grep: 53 route files with auth() destructure; 95 'status: 401' sites; e.g. flagged-users/route.ts:30-34

#### [L-CC34] Declare dotenv as a devDependency (used by 4 scripts, only present transitively)
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · dependencies · effort S
- **1. Description:** Four scripts in scripts/ import dotenv to load .env.local, but dotenv is not declared in package.json.
- **2. Issue identified:** Four debug/migration scripts `import { config } from 'dotenv'`. dotenv works today only because it is installed transitively. If that transitive provider changes, all four scripts break. (tsx is also used via npx in CI and is undeclared.)
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add dotenv to devDependencies (and optionally pin tsx). If any debug script is obsolete, delete it instead.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Adding the dep is autonomous, but choosing add-vs-delete for the debug scripts depends on whether they are still wanted.
- **Evidence:** scripts/debug-notification-prefs.ts:6-7; dotenv used in 4 scripts; not in package.json; .github/workflows/model-health-check.yml uses 'npx tsx'

#### [L-CC35] Pin Node version for reproducible Netlify builds (.nvmrc or engines)
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · tech-debt · effort S
- **1. Description:** There is no .nvmrc, no .node-version, no engines field in package.json, and no NODE_VERSION in netlify.toml.
- **2. Issue identified:** Next 16 + Turbopack + sharp are sensitive to Node version. Without a pinned version, a Netlify default-Node change can silently shift the build runtime, causing native-module (sharp) build failures that are hard to diagnose.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add an engines.node field (e.g. '>=20 <23') in package.json and/or a .nvmrc matching the local dev/Netlify Node version.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Can add the file, but the exact target Node version should be confirmed against the actual local/Netlify Node version.
- **Evidence:** No engines field in package.json; no .nvmrc/.node-version; netlify.toml has no NODE_VERSION

#### [L-CC36] Lint and typecheck scope excludes netlify/functions and scripts directories
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** The lint script runs `eslint src/` only, while there is meaningful TypeScript in netlify/functions/ (6 scheduled cron functions) and scripts/ (13+ files).
- **2. Issue identified:** netlify/functions/*.ts (cron jobs touching money/auctions/usage) and scripts/*.ts are never linted by the quality gate. Bugs in this server-side cron code ship unlinted. depcheck also ignores these dirs.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Extend the lint script to include netlify/ and scripts/ (e.g. `eslint src/ netlify/ scripts/`) and verify they pass typecheck.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** package.json:11; netlify/functions/ has 6 .ts cron functions; scripts/ has 13+ files

#### [L-CC37] Add role=status/aria-busy to loading skeletons
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · accessibility · effort S
- **1. Description:** Skeleton.tsx and the page-level skeletons provide visual loading placeholders with a shimmer animation.
- **2. Issue identified:** Skeletons have no role="status"/aria-busy/sr-only 'Loading' text, so screen-reader users get silence during loads with no indication that content is pending.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Wrap skeleton containers in role="status" aria-busy="true" with an sr-only 'Loading…' label, and mark the decorative shimmer bars aria-hidden.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Self-contained.
- **Evidence:** src/components/Skeleton.tsx:7-103; used at src/app/collection/page.tsx:615

#### [L-CC38] Review faded text/placeholder contrast (text-pop-black/40, gray-400)
- **Area / Dimension:** cross-cutting · accessibility · effort S
- **1. Description:** Several controls use low-opacity black for placeholder/secondary text (e.g. placeholder:text-pop-black/40 on the collection search) plus 190 text-gray-400 usages on light backgrounds.
- **2. Issue identified:** 40% black on white is roughly 3:1 contrast, which fails WCAG 1.4.3 (4.5:1 for normal text) for placeholders and small secondary text. The pop-art cream background can push some gray-400 small text below threshold too.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Bump placeholder/secondary text to at least text-pop-black/60 (or gray-500/600) where it conveys real content, and spot-check gray-400 small text against the cream background.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Code change is easy, but final color values are a visual/brand decision worth a quick owner sign-off.
- **Evidence:** src/app/collection/page.tsx:824; grep: 4 text-pop-black/40 + 5 text-pop-black/50, 190 text-gray-400 usages

Separately, test-email hardcodes a personal recipient (chrispatton716@gmail.com) with a stale 'from the screenshot' comment.

- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/email-preview/route.ts:8; src/app/api/test-email/route.ts:8, :21

#### [L-EM12] Extract a shared constant-time cron-secret verifier (reduce duplicated auth check)
- **Area / Dimension:** email · tech-debt · effort S
- **1. Description:** The email cron routes authorize callers by comparing the Authorization header to `Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}` with plain `!==`, copy-pasted across routes with no shared helper.
- **2. Issue identified:** Primary issue is duplication: the same check is hand-rolled in send-feedback-reminders and send-trial-reminders, so any change must be made everywhere. The timing-attack angle is minor for a high-entropy bearer secret.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Extract verifyCronSecret(request) using crypto.timingSafeEqual on equal-length buffers, reused by all cron routes.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/cron/send-feedback-reminders/route.ts:16; src/app/api/cron/send-trial-reminders/route.ts:11

#### [L-EM13] DISPOSABLE_DOMAINS blocklist is tiny and hardcoded — weak abuse gate for bonus scans
- **Area / Dimension:** email · tech-debt · effort S
- **1. Description:** emailValidation.ts blocks disposable emails using a hardcoded 15-entry Set, used to gate the 5-bonus-scan claim.
- **2. Issue identified:** Thousands of disposable-email domains exist; a 15-domain static list is trivially bypassed. Mitigating context: the IP-claim check and email-verification-token gate (plus MX check in prod) are the real defenses, so the blocklist is a best-effort first layer.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Vendor a maintained disposable-domains JSON list (a few hundred KB, $0) loaded at runtime, or document the list as best-effort and lean on the IP + verification gate.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves variable Anthropic scan spend (~$0.015/scan) only if abuse occurs; magnitude unknown/contingent _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Can vendor a static JSON list; no external service needed.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/emailValidation.ts:11-27; used at src/app/api/email-capture/route.ts:58

#### [L-EM14] Hand-rolled bonus-scan emails bypass the template system (maintainability + branding drift)
- **Area / Dimension:** email · tech-debt · effort M
- **1. Description:** The bonus-scan verification email (email-capture POST) and the confirmation email (verify route) are raw inline HTML strings using an off-brand style instead of the pop-art templates in email.ts.
- **2. Issue identified:** These two emails ignore emailHeader/emailFooter, the brand palette, the tagline, and the LLC/Privacy/Terms footer present in every other email. They also duplicate the Resend wiring and won't get any centralized HTML escaping.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add templates (e.g., bonus_scan_verify, bonus_scan_granted) routed through sendNotificationEmail so they inherit branding, footer, escaping, and future unsubscribe headers, then delete the inline HTML.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/email-capture/route.ts:133-151; src/app/api/email-capture/verify/route.ts:98-117

#### [L-EM15] Public waitlist endpoint lacks rate limit / honeypot (inconsistent with email-capture)
- **Area / Dimension:** email · security · effort S
- **1. Description:** POST /api/waitlist adds the submitted email to a Resend audience via resend.contacts.create, validated with zod, but has no rate limit and no anti-spam honeypot.
- **2. Issue identified:** Unlike /api/email-capture (which has checkRateLimit and a honeypot field), the waitlist route can be scripted to flood the Resend audience and burn Resend API calls. This writes to a Resend audience, NOT a Supabase table — the abuse surface is the Resend contacts API.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Mirror the email-capture protections: add checkRateLimit (e.g. rateLimiters.analyze) and an optional honeypot field.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — protects the Resend contacts/audience (free 3K emails/mo tier) from abuse _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pattern already implemented in email-capture route.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/waitlist/route.ts:13, :30; contrast email-capture/route.ts:37-41, :49

#### [L-EM16] PostgREST join result cast with `(data as any)` hides column-name drift in email path
- **Area / Dimension:** email · tech-debt · effort S
- **1. Description:** getListingComicData selects a foreign-table join and reads it via const comic = (data as any).comics.
- **2. Issue identified:** The as any bypasses type checking on the nested join shape. If the FK alias or nested column names change, comic becomes undefined and the email silently shows 'Unknown'/'' with no signal — the same silent-join-failure class documented as a lesson. The existing optional chaining prevents a crash, so impact is degraded content.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Type the select result (a small interface for the joined row) instead of as any.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/email.ts:253, fallback at 255-256

#### [L-EM17] Cross-link near-duplicate welcome-email and email-overhaul doc sets
- **Area / Dimension:** email · documentation · effort S
- **1. Description:** The email feature is documented across four same-day files: welcome-email-design + welcome-email plan, and email-overhaul-design + email-overhaul plan.
- **2. Issue identified:** The email overhaul functionally encompasses the welcome email, yet the welcome-email pair is not cross-linked to or folded under the overhaul docs. Both pairs read 'Status: Approved' for shipped behavior. A reader can't tell whether the standalone welcome-email docs are still authoritative.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add a one-line note to the welcome-email docs pointing to the email-overhaul design as the current reference, and archive the welcome-email plan alongside other shipped plans.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** docs/engineering-specs/2026-04-01-welcome-email-design.md:4; docs/superpowers/plans/2026-04-01-welcome-email.md; email-overhaul-design.md; shipped at src/lib/email.ts

#### [L-FE15] Fix off-brand manifest short_name and generic description
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · documentation · effort S
- **1. Description:** The PWA manifest description and short_name are generic and off-brand.
- **2. Issue identified:** manifest.json sets description 'Track your comic book collection' (not the approved tagline/mission) and short_name 'Collectors' (truncated oddly — drops 'Chest'). The installed PWA home-screen label reads 'Collectors' and the install prompt shows the generic description.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Align description with the approved tagline 'Scan comics. Track value. Collect smarter.' and set short_name to something that reads as the brand (e.g. 'Chest' or 'CC Chest').
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_

#### [L-FE16] Add aria-labels to the homepage insight-card buttons
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · accessibility · effort S
- **1. Description:** Homepage insight cards (Biggest Increase / Best Buy / Biggest Decline) are <button> elements whose only text is a truncated title plus a figure; cover images use alt=''.
- **2. Issue identified:** The insight cards have no aria-label describing the action (each opens a detail modal). A screen-reader user hears only the truncated title and the figure, with no announcement of what activating the button does.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add aria-label to each insight-card button (e.g. 'View biggest increase details').
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_

#### [L-FE17] Add aria-current to active navigation items and label both navs
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · accessibility · effort S
- **1. Description:** Both desktop Navigation and the mobile bottom nav (MobileNav) compute an isActive state and style the current route differently.
- **2. Issue identified:** Active state is conveyed by color/weight only, with no aria-current="page" (aria-current count is 0 across src). Screen-reader users get no announcement of which nav item is the current page, and the bottom <nav> is unlabeled vs. the top nav.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add aria-current="page" to the active Link in MobileNav and Navigation, and add aria-label to distinguish the navs.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Self-contained.
- **Evidence:** src/components/MobileNav.tsx:205, :218-231; grep: 0 aria-current across src/

#### [L-FE18] Extract duplicated nav link tables into a single shared module
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · tech-debt · effort M
- **1. Description:** Navigation.tsx and MobileNav.tsx each define their own near-identical guest/registered primary + secondary link arrays.
- **2. Issue identified:** The two components maintain parallel link tables and already differ subtly (desktop 'More' includes Following / Key Hunt / About; the mobile drawer omits Following and Key Hunt and lacks About). This divergence is how nav drift bugs arise.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Extract the nav link definitions into one shared module (e.g. src/lib/navLinks.ts) consumed by both components.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/components/Navigation.tsx:303-340; src/components/MobileNav.tsx:156-189

#### [L-FE19] Avoid silent mid-session service-worker swaps; add a controlled update flow
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · tech-debt · effort M
- **1. Description:** ServiceWorkerProvider registers sw.js and calls registration.update() hourly; sw.js calls skipWaiting on install and clients.claim on activate.
- **2. Issue identified:** self.skipWaiting() runs unconditionally on install and self.clients.claim() on activate, so a new SW takes control immediately and silently mid-session. Combined with cache-first for /_next/static, an open tab can end up running new JS chunks against old HTML after the hourly update, risking ChunkLoadError. sw.js has a SKIP_WAITING message handler but ServiceWorkerProvider never posts it and there's no controllerchange reload.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Either drop the unconditional skipWaiting/clients.claim and prompt the user to refresh (postMessage SKIP_WAITING on user action, then reload on controllerchange), or keep skipWaiting but reload clients on controllerchange.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/components/ServiceWorkerProvider.tsx:16-21; public/sw.js:25, 43, 80-97, 102

#### [L-FE20] Version the SW static cache so stale build chunks get evicted on deploy
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** sw.js cache-first stores every /_next/static/ asset into a single persistent CACHE_NAME ('key-hunt-cache-v1') and never evicts old build chunks.
- **2. Issue identified:** Each deploy emits new hashed chunk filenames; the SW keeps adding new chunks but never deletes old ones. The activate cleanup only deletes caches whose NAME differs from CACHE_NAME, and CACHE_NAME is static across deploys, so old chunks accumulate unboundedly. The cache name 'key-hunt' is also misleading for global static caching.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Version the static cache name per build (or use a max-entry/LRU strategy) and prune entries not in the current build manifest on activate. At minimum bump CACHE_NAME each deploy.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** public/sw.js:2, 80-97, 29-44

#### [L-FE21] Reconcile service worker offline.html: file does not exist
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · tech-debt · effort S
- **1. Description:** public/sw.js lists '/offline.html' in STATIC_ASSETS and defines OFFLINE_CACHE_NAME, implying an offline fallback page.
- **2. Issue identified:** public/offline.html does not exist. The install handler explicitly filters it out with STATIC_ASSETS.filter(url => !url.includes('offline.html')), so the listed asset is never cached and the implied offline fallback never works — offline navigation returns a plain-text 503 instead.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Either create a real branded public/offline.html and serve it as the navigation fallback, or remove the '/offline.html' entry and its filter.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** public/sw.js:8, 21; public/offline.html absent on disk

#### [L-FE22] Convert header logo (ChestIcon) from raw <img> to next/image
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** ChestIcon renders the brand emblem with a plain `<img src='/icons/emblem.png'>` and an eslint-disable for no-img-element.
- **2. Issue identified:** The logo is in the global Navigation, so it loads on every route and is a likely header LCP element. Raw <img> skips next/image optimization, priority hints, and modern format transcoding.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Convert ChestIcon to next/image with priority for the header logo, or pre-optimize emblem.png to WebP/AVIF.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change (negligible bandwidth win) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/components/icons/ChestIcon.tsx:1, 10-16

#### [L-FE23] Add error logging to ShareCollectionModal settings-load catch block
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** The settings fetch catch block binds the caught error but ignores it and sets a generic message.
- **2. Issue identified:** In fetchSettings, `catch (err)` ignores `err` (no console, no Sentry) and shows a hardcoded 'Failed to load sharing settings'. The load path loses all diagnostic signal, making share-link bugs hard to triage.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Log the caught error (console.error or Sentry capture) before showing the friendly message.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/components/ShareCollectionModal.tsx:51-52

#### [L-FE24] Raise tsconfig target above ES2017 for leaner modern output
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · bundle · effort S
- **1. Description:** tsconfig.json sets compilerOptions.target to ES2017.
- **2. Issue identified:** Targeting ES2017 can force down-leveling of async/await, optional chaining, nullish coalescing, and spread into older, larger forms. The win is marginal because Next's SWC compiler applies its own browserslist transforms to client bundles independent of tsconfig target; the main effect is on emitted server/script transpilation.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Raise target to ES2020 (or ES2022). Re-run build and a quick smoke test.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — minor/marginal bundle/perf win _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** tsconfig.json:29

#### [L-FE25] Throttle per-second AuctionCountdown intervals on grid views
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** AuctionCountdown runs an independent setInterval(…, 1000) per instance, each recomputing time and triggering a state update + re-render every second.
- **2. Issue identified:** On the shop grid or my-listings grid, each AuctionCard renders an AuctionCountdown, so dozens of 1-second timers fire independently, each re-rendering its card. For auctions days/hours out, 1-second granularity is wasted main-thread work.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** For non-urgent auctions (>1h remaining) tick once per minute; only sub-10-minute auctions need 1s resolution. Optionally drive all visible countdowns from a single shared interval.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/components/auction/AuctionCountdown.tsx:18-30; AuctionCard.tsx:128

#### [L-FE26] Fix exhaustive-deps and pass an explicit limit on the my-auctions listings fetch
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · code-quality · effort M
- **1. Description:** The my-listings page calls loadListings() from a useEffect whose dependency array omits the (non-memoized) loadListings, and fetches the seller's listings with no explicit limit.
- **2. Issue identified:** The useEffect depends only on [isSignedIn] but invokes loadListings, which is recreated each render — a latent exhaustive-deps violation. loadListings calls /api/auctions?sellerId=me with no limit; getActiveAuctions caps at the default 50 and the page filters active vs ended client-side. A prolific seller with >50 listings gets a silently truncated list.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Wrap loadListings in useCallback (or move it into the effect), and either pass an explicit limit and paginate, or split active vs ended into separate paged fetches.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change (query is already server-capped at 50) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/my-auctions/page.tsx:47-51, :82-96, :107-112; src/lib/auctionDb.ts:425-428 & :505

#### [L-FE27] Shop tab refetches the full list on every filter toggle with client-side search
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** The Shop page reloads auctions/listings whenever sort or a filter toggle changes, while the text search box filters purely client-side.
- **2. Issue identified:** Each filter toggle (Ending Soon, Buy It Now, Following) re-runs the load effect with no caching, refetching the dataset repeatedly; rapid toggling spams /api/auctions with no debounce or result caching.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Cache fetched result sets keyed by the filter signature (or use SWR/stale-while-revalidate), and skip refetch when a toggle can be applied client-side.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change (fewer API calls / Supabase reads) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/shop/page.tsx:173-191

#### [L-FE28] Trades page lacks memoization/caching, refetches on every active↔history toggle
- **Area / Dimension:** frontend · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** TradesPageContent uses two useEffects keyed on activeTab — one loads trades for active/history, one loads matches for the matches tab.
- **2. Issue identified:** loadTrades/loadMatches aren't memoized, and loadTrades refetches /api/trades every time the user toggles between Active and History even though the only difference is a status filter that could be cached. (The two effects each guard internally, so only one body runs per tab.)
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Cache the active vs history result sets so toggling between them doesn't refetch, and wrap loadTrades/loadMatches in useCallback.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/trades/page.tsx:50-66, 83-93

#### [L-MK20] Make mark-shipped idempotent with a conditional update guard
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** mark-shipped reads shipped_at, branches if already set, then later UPDATEs shipped_at without re-checking in the WHERE clause.
- **2. Issue identified:** Two concurrent mark-shipped requests can both pass the `if (auction.shipped_at)` guard and both run the update + send duplicate shipped/rating-request notifications. The comic clone is protected by its own sold_at idempotency, so the realized harm is duplicate buyer notifications.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add `.is('shipped_at', null)` and `.select('id')` to the auction UPDATE; only fire notifications/audit when a row was actually updated.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/auctions/[id]/mark-shipped/route.ts:79-84 and :118-125

#### [L-MK21] Transactions Offers tab duplicates getBuyerOffers logic with a different shape
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · tech-debt · effort S
- **1. Description:** Buyer offers are fetched two different ways: GET /api/offers via getBuyerOffers (Offer[] with auctions(*)) and the /api/transactions?type=offers branch with its own bespoke select + OfferRow mapping.
- **2. Issue identified:** Two independent queries/transforms for 'this buyer's offers' that can drift in field selection, status filtering, and ordering. Maintaining both doubles the surface for bugs and schema drift.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Consolidate to one buyer-offers query helper returning a shared shape and have both endpoints consume it.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/auctionDb.ts:1775-1785; src/app/api/transactions/route.ts:185-212

#### [L-MK22] getBuyerOffers over-fetches with auctions(*)
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** GET /api/offers returns the buyer's offers with the full joined auction row.
- **2. Issue identified:** The select is `*, auctions(*)` — pulling every column of both the offer and the joined auction when the UI only needs amount, status, listing id, comic title/cover. Wasted egress and serialization on every Offers view.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace `*, auctions(*)` with an explicit column projection limited to fields the Offer type and UI consume.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change (smaller payloads) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/auctionDb.ts:1776-1780

#### [L-MK23] Sales page computes summary stats over full unpaginated array on every render
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · performance · effort M
- **1. Description:** The Sales History page derives total sales, total profit, and avg profit by reducing over the entire sales array from useCollection.
- **2. Issue identified:** All sales are loaded client-side and reduced on every render (not memoized) just to render three summary cards and an unpaginated table.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** At minimum wrap the reduce results in useMemo; ideally compute aggregates server-side and paginate the table.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Server-side aggregation depends on the data source outside this file; the useMemo + page pagination is autonomous.
- **Evidence:** src/app/sales/page.tsx:46-48

#### [L-MK24] Watchlist categorization calls calculateTimeRemaining 3x per item per render
- **Area / Dimension:** marketplace · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** The Watchlist page splits items into endingSoon / others / ended groups.
- **2. Issue identified:** Three separate .filter() passes each independently call calculateTimeRemaining(w.auction.endTime) for the same item, recomputing it up to 3x per item, and all three filters re-run every render with no memoization.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Compute the remaining-time bucket once per item in a single useMemo pass producing the three groups.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/watchlist/page.tsx:65-80

#### [L-PR11] ebay-prices GET handler fabricates a mock NextRequest to re-invoke POST
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** The GET handler constructs a synthetic NextRequest with a JSON body and calls POST(mockRequest).
- **2. Issue identified:** Re-entrant handler invocation via a fabricated Request is brittle (relies on POST reading exactly request.json(), no header/auth/streaming parity) and makes adding rate-limiting/instrumentation harder. It also forces field-mapping duplication.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Extract core logic into a plain async fetchEbayPrices(params) function; have GET and POST both validate their own inputs then call it.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pure refactor.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/ebay-prices/route.ts:166-187

#### [L-PR12] Negative-cache TTL (1h) is 12x shorter than positive TTL (12h) in eBay price routes
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** ebay-prices and refresh-value cache a successful price for the full 12h ebayPrice TTL but cache {noData:true} for only 1h.
- **2. Issue identified:** Thin-market comics (the common no-data case) are re-queried against eBay 12x more often than priced comics — exactly backwards, since their market state changes slowly. Net effect is avoidable eBay quota burn on the lookups least likely to change.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Raise the negative-cache TTL (e.g. 6-12h) or align it with the positive TTL, in both ebay-prices and refresh-value.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 cash; reduces eBay Browse calls on no-data comics (free, rate-limited) _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — One-line TTL change in two places.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/ebay-prices/route.ts:141, :132; src/app/api/comics/[id]/refresh-value/route.ts:107-110

#### [L-PR13] Stale 'GoCollect' reference in variantResolver AI prompt
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · documentation · effort S
- **1. Description:** The variant-resolver AI prompt asks for the canonical variant name 'in collector databases (CovrPrice, GoCollect, GCD, CGC census, etc.)'.
- **2. Issue identified:** GoCollect's API program was discontinued. Though this is free-text rather than an API call, naming a defunct source is documentation drift and can subtly bias the model.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Drop 'GoCollect' from the prompt list (keep CovrPrice, GCD, CGC census) or replace with an active source.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — One-word prompt edit.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/variantResolver.ts:248

#### [L-PR14] cacheGetOrSet caches failed/empty fetcher results for the full TTL
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** cacheGetOrSet caches whatever the fetcher returns, including error/empty sentinels, with the prefix's default TTL.
- **2. Issue identified:** There is no way to skip caching a 'failed' fetch result. If a fetcher returns an empty/error object (e.g. eBay returned nothing transiently), that empty result is cached for the full TTL (eBay 12h, aiAnalyze 30 days), suppressing retries and degrading data quality. The set is fire-and-forget so a write failure is also invisible.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add an optional `shouldCache(data): boolean` predicate so callers can avoid persisting empty/error results, especially for the 30-day aiAnalyze and 12h ebay prefixes.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Neutral to slightly cost-saving (avoids re-fetch storms while preventing long-lived bad cache) _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pure in-repo signature + caller update.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/cache.ts:108-133, 27-37

#### [L-PR15] Grade float equality (`e.grade === grade`) relies on exact-literal matching
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** Several lookups compare numeric grades with strict === (gradePrice exact find, ebayBrowse GRADE_MULTIPLIERS.find, con-mode-lookup, offlineCache).
- **2. Issue identified:** These work today because grades come from a fixed enumerated set so the float literals are identical. But any path that derives a grade via arithmetic (e.g. parseFloat of user input) could yield a value like 9.399999… that never === 9.4, silently falling through to a wrong/lower estimate. Speculative until such a path exists.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Compare with a small epsilon (Math.abs(a-b) < 0.001) or snap grades to the canonical scale before comparing.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Localized helper/epsilon change.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/gradePrice.ts:57; src/lib/ebayBrowse.ts:311; src/app/api/con-mode-lookup/route.ts:111,204; src/lib/offlineCache.ts:380

#### [L-PR16] parseFloat on eBay price string can produce NaN that flows into median math
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** parseBrowseResponse parses each listing price with parseFloat(priceObj?.value ?? '0') with no finite-number guard; these prices feed filterOutliersAndCalculateMedian.
- **2. Issue identified:** parseFloat returns NaN for a malformed price string. A NaN price enters the prices array, and a NaN in the sorted/filtered set can corrupt the median (Math.round(NaN*100)/100 = NaN), surfacing as 'NaN' or a $0 estimate.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Wrap the price parseFloat with Number.isFinite and skip the listing (or default to 0 and filter) when not finite, before it enters the median computation.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Add an isFinite guard at the price parse site.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/ebayBrowse.ts:346, 424, 425/261-266

#### [L-PR17] Flag PRICECHARTING_PROPOSAL.md as a not-yet-decided $499/yr commitment (proposal, not shipped)
- **Area / Dimension:** pricing · documentation · effort S
- **1. Description:** docs/PRICECHARTING_PROPOSAL.md ('Aponte Decision Brief') proposes integrating PriceCharting's $499/yr Legendary Sub as the sold-listing pricing partner.
- **2. Issue identified:** The doc reads as an actionable recommendation ('Recommend subscribing for one year') but carries no decision/status header, and there is no PriceCharting integration in src (the live pricing path is the free eBay Browse API). Without a 'Status: Proposed — not adopted' marker, this $499/yr proposal can be misread as committed.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add a 'Status: Proposed — NOT adopted (current pricing = free eBay Browse API)' line at the top. No change to the GoCollect/Marvel narrative.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 today; clarifies a deferred $499/yr decision so it isn't accidentally treated as committed _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** No — Whether to mark it 'rejected', 'deferred', or 'under review' is a product/business decision the user must make.
- **Evidence:** docs/PRICECHARTING_PROPOSAL.md (no status header); no pricecharting in src; live pricing src/app/api/ebay-prices/route.ts

#### [L-PAY14] Nine payments routes each re-instantiate the Stripe SDK with a hardcoded duplicated API version
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** Every payments route constructs its own new Stripe(...) with apiVersion '2025-12-15.clover' inline.
- **2. Issue identified:** The same conditional Stripe-init block with the literal apiVersion string is duplicated across 9 files. Bumping the API version requires editing the literal in every file; drift means routes could pin different versions.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Create a single src/lib/stripe.ts exporting a shared, lazily-initialized Stripe instance (or null) with the apiVersion in one place, and import it everywhere.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — maintainability _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** billing/checkout/route.ts:22-26; checkout/route.ts:26-30; connect/status/route.ts:6-10; connect/onboarding-return/route.ts:6-10; webhooks/stripe/route.ts:21-25 (and 5 more)

#### [L-PAY15] Deprecated purchase route retained as a 410 stub
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · tech-debt · effort S
- **1. Description:** src/app/api/listings/[id]/purchase/route.ts is a dead handler that only returns 410 Gone, kept for stale clients after the Apr 22 switch to direct Stripe Checkout.
- **2. Issue identified:** It is a permanent route stub for a flow that no longer exists, still shipping as a serverless function and adding maintenance surface.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Confirm via logs/analytics that the endpoint sees no traffic, then delete the route. If retention is desired, add a removal date to BACKLOG.

#### [L-PAY16] invoice.payment_succeeded skips amount_paid===0, which would also skip future 100%-coupon invoices
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · tech-debt · effort S
- **1. Description:** handleInvoicePaymentSucceeded early-returns when invoice.amount_paid is 0 to skip $0 trial invoices.
- **2. Issue identified:** route.ts:492 returns early on amount_paid===0, with an inline comment already flagging that this also silently skips a 100%-discount coupon invoice. If promo/100%-off coupons are ever added, a fully-discounted renewal would not flip status to 'active', leaving a discounted user stuck in past_due.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Gate the early-return on invoice.billing_reason (e.g. subscription_create trial) rather than amount_paid alone, or check invoice.discount before skipping.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 today; prevents future support/churn if 100% coupons are introduced _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:491-492

#### [L-PAY17] 30-day promo trial eligibility is client-controlled — trivially abusable for a longer free trial
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · security · effort M
- **1. Description:** The 30-day promo trial is signaled by a localStorage flag and sent as a boolean in the checkout request body.
- **2. Issue identified:** promoTrial.ts stores cc_promo_trial in localStorage; choose-plan reads getPromoTrialFlag() and useSubscription sends { promoTrial } in the body. The server grants the 30-day trial whenever promoTrial && !trialUsed — it only re-verifies one-trial-per-account, never QR provenance. Any user who never started a trial can POST {promoTrial:true} directly and receive 30 days instead of 7.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** If the 30-day trial should be QR-exclusive, gate it server-side — a signed promo token issued by /join/trial, or a per-account promo-eligibility flag — not by trusting the request body.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves giving 30-day unlimited-scan premium (vs intended 7-day) to anyone who crafts the request — ~4x the trial AI-scan exposure per abuser _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Designing the server-side promo token / eligibility flag is a product decision and may need a DB column migration.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/promoTrial.ts:7-25; src/app/choose-plan/page.tsx:39; src/hooks/useSubscription.ts:235; src/app/api/billing/checkout/route.ts:107-108

#### [L-PAY18] Checkout routes collapse all errors to generic 500s, hiding actionable Stripe errors
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · ux · effort S
- **1. Description:** Both billing/checkout and marketplace checkout catch all errors and return a flat 'Failed to create checkout session'.
- **2. Issue identified:** Both routes collapse every exception to a 500 with a generic message. Stripe throws typed errors (StripeInvalidRequestError for a missing/misconfigured price, StripeAuthenticationError) that are swallowed. A misconfigured STRIPE_PRICE_* env var surfaces as a vague failure, with no differentiation between client-fixable and server faults.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Inspect error instanceof Stripe.errors.StripeError and map known types to appropriate status codes/messages while still logging the full error server-side.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — UX/observability _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/billing/checkout/route.ts:175-178; src/app/api/checkout/route.ts:207-210

#### [L-PAY19] Extract and test useSubscription scan-limit derivations
- **Area / Dimension:** payments · testing · effort M
- **1. Description:** src/hooks/useSubscription.ts derives canScan, scansRemaining, and isUnlimited and exposes useScanLimits()/useFeatureAccess() that gate scanning and premium features.
- **2. Issue identified:** No useSubscription test file exists. These limit/permission derivations are the front-line enforcement of the free-vs-premium scan cap but live inline in the hook with no extracted, testable helper. Hook state-calculation logic is on the project's MUST-test list. (The underlying tier/limit math in subscription.ts IS tested; the gap is the hook-level derivation.)
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Extract the pure derivation (given {tier, scansUsed, monthlyLimit} -> {remaining, canScan, isUnlimited}) into a helper and unit-test boundary cases.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — In-repo extraction + tests.
- **Evidence:** src/hooks/useSubscription.ts:339-357, :355, :322; no useSubscription test file exists

#### [L-SC20] Remove unused openai dependency (OpenAI fallback never activated)
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · dependencies · effort S
- **1. Description:** The openai SDK (installed 6.25.0) is declared as a runtime dependency. AI cover recognition uses Anthropic Sonnet primary and Gemini fallback; OpenAI is not wired in anywhere.
- **2. Issue identified:** Grep for 'openai', 'OpenAI', and 'gpt-' across src/, scripts/, and netlify/ returns zero source matches. The dependency is dead weight that adds to install size and npm-audit surface, and it is NOT in the depcheck ignore list. (Root cause: the scan-resilience plan originally targeted OpenAI but shipped as Gemini.)
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Remove openai from package.json dependencies. If an OpenAI fallback is ever planned, add it back when the provider code is implemented.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no recurring cost change; slims node_modules/install only _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
