#### [L-SC21] Low-confidence fallback re-scan can double the most expensive call with no provider-cost guard
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** When the primary imageAnalysis returns confidence 'low', the route re-runs the full analyzeImage on the OTHER provider. With Gemini primary, the fallback is Anthropic — the most expensive vision call (~$0.015) — triggered purely on the model's self-reported 'low' confidence string.
- **2. Issue identified:** A single scan can incur two full vision calls, the second on the pricier provider, gated only by a soft confidence label. There is no cap on how often this fires, and 'low' confidence is common on foil/vintage covers — exactly the books users scan most hopefully.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Keep the quality benefit but bound the cost: only escalate for signed-in/premium users, escalate to the cheaper provider when possible, or A/B whether the extra call materially improves accept rates. At minimum record it as a distinct call type.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves up to ~$0.015 (Anthropic vision) on each low-confidence scan that currently escalates; magnitude depends on low-confidence rate _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — Tuning accuracy-vs-cost tradeoff and any tier gating is a product decision.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/analyze/route.ts:672-722

#### [L-SC22] Lower the slab-detection image cost by downscaling before the first AI call
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** Slab detection runs as a lightweight first AI call but receives the same full-resolution base64 image as the main analysis.
- **2. Issue identified:** executeSlabDetection is a lightweight call (max_tokens 128) whose only job is isSlabbed + cert number, yet it is passed the identical full-res base64Data. Vision input tokens scale with image resolution; a much smaller image (~512-768px) would cost fewer image tokens for a pure slabbed-vs-not classification.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Generate a downscaled variant (server-side sharp, ~512-768px) for the slab-detection call only, keeping full-res for analysis/harvest. Measure detection accuracy before rolling out. (Redundant with H-SC1 if slab detection is removed from the raw path.)
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves a fraction of slab-detection image-token cost on slab scans (~$0.001-0.003 range); modest, applies only on slab scans _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — sharp already available; needs an accuracy check on the smaller image.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/analyze/route.ts:390-396; src/lib/providers/anthropic.ts:305-334

#### [L-SC23] Slab detail extraction sends the full creator prompt when only creators are skippable, then discards the creator output
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · cost · effort S
- **1. Description:** On the cert-first path with complete slab data, executeSlabDetailExtraction runs with skipCreators=true and skipBarcode=false. The harvest-only prompt (smaller, max_tokens 128) is only selected when BOTH skip flags are true, so this call uses the full SLAB_DETAIL_EXTRACTION_PROMPT (max_tokens 384) asking for creators — which the route then discards.
- **2. Issue identified:** When only creators are skippable, the model is still sent the large creator-extraction instructions and asked for fields the route throws away, paying for extra input+output tokens.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add a barcode+harvest-only prompt variant (no creator instructions) selected when skipCreators is true regardless of skipBarcode.
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves a small fraction of the slabDetailExtraction call cost on cert-first scans (fewer prompt + output tokens) _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Add one prompt variant + selection branch.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/providers/anthropic.ts:354-358; src/lib/providers/gemini.ts:160-164; src/app/api/analyze/route.ts:534-535, :560-564

#### [L-SC24] posthogServer.shutdown() is awaited on every server scan event, adding a synchronous flush to the response path
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** trackScanServer() calls posthogServer.capture(...) then immediately await posthogServer.shutdown(). shutdown flushes and tears down the client on every single scan, defeating any batching within a warm container.
- **2. Issue identified:** Each scan triggers a full flush+shutdown. trackScanServer is invoked fire-and-forget at the call site, but the internal await shutdown still forces a synchronous network round trip before the promise resolves and prevents batching.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Use posthogServer.flush() instead of shutdown(), or rely on flushAt/flushInterval batching and only shutdown on process exit. Drop the await.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no PostHog cost change; minor latency/throughput improvement _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Self-contained change to analyticsServer.ts.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/analyticsServer.ts:41-47

#### [L-SC25] Image-hash cache hits bypass the barcode_catalog and variant resolver, serving stale variant names
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · correctness · effort M
- **1. Description:** On cache write the code stores variantSource, but the typed cacheGet declares a narrower shape. On a cache HIT, usedCache=true, which SKIPS the barcode_catalog lookup (gated by !usedCache) and the variant resolver (only in the non-cache branch).
- **2. Issue identified:** Cache hits bypass the barcode_catalog and variant resolver entirely, so a later admin-approved catalog variant name will never apply to images already cached — the 30-day TTL keeps serving the variant frozen at first scan. This undermines the variant feature's 'catalog compounds as admins approve' design.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Either (a) don't include barcode/variant in the image-hash cache and always run the cheap, free catalog lookup + resolver even on cache hits, or (b) store a catalog version stamp and re-resolve on hit.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — correctness of variant freshness; catalog lookup is free _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Partial — interacts with the unshipped two-pass-barcode work and variant UX — worth product confirmation on freshness behavior.
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/analyze/route.ts:345-359, :374, :812, :738

#### [L-SC26] transformDbComicToCollectionItem never reads variantSource back from the DB
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · correctness · effort S
- **1. Description:** addComic depends on item.comic.variantSource to decide catalog contribution, but the DB-to-item transform never populates variantSource.
- **2. Issue identified:** addComic uses item.comic.variantSource to set the catalog source, but transformDbComicToCollectionItem does not map any variant_source field. A grep confirms `variant_source` appears nowhere in repo — the comics table has NO such column, so variantSource is request-scoped only. On any flow that loads a comic from Supabase and re-catalogs it, variantSource defaults are re-derived.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Document explicitly that variantSource is request-scoped (not persisted) and ensure re-catalog flows don't run on DB-loaded comics, OR persist variant_source as a column and map it in both transforms.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — avoids minor admin-queue pollution _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Confirmed variant_source is not a persisted column; documenting the request-scoped contract is fully in-repo.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/db.ts:144-160, 656-701; grep: no `variant_source` in repo

#### [L-SC27] getCommunityCovers uses .single() which throws PGRST116 on every cache miss
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** getCommunityCovers chains .limit(1).single() and treats any error as 'no cover found'.
- **2. Issue identified:** .single() raises a PGRST116 error whenever 0 rows match (the common case for an uncached comic). The code swallows it, so it works, but it generates needless error objects on the hot scan path and conflates 'not found' with real DB failures. Same pattern at lookupBarcodeCatalog.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Replace .single() with .maybeSingle() so a 0-row result returns { data: null, error: null } cleanly.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — cleaner error handling, marginally less work on misses _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/coverImageDb.ts:35-47; src/lib/db.ts:1197-1216

#### [L-SC28] imageOptimization compresses by data-URL string length, not decoded byte size
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** compressToTarget compares dataUrl.length against a base64-inflated target instead of the actual decoded byte size.
- **2. Issue identified:** It multiplies the byte target by 1.37 and compares to dataUrl.length, which also includes the 'data:image/jpeg;base64,' prefix. estimateBase64Size already computes the precise decoded size but isn't used in the loop, so compression targets are slightly off.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Use estimateBase64Size(dataUrl) in the while-loop condition for an exact comparison against targetSize, removing the 1.37 fudge factor and prefix skew.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — minor storage/payload accuracy _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/imageOptimization.ts:86-97, 225-229

#### [L-SC29] metadataCache mergeMetadataIntoDetails mutates its input object in place
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** mergeMetadataIntoDetails mutates the passed details object and also returns it.
- **2. Issue identified:** The function both mutates `details` in place and returns it (the doc comment acknowledges 'mutates in place'), a surprising dual contract. Callers that pass shared/state-derived objects (React state) risk subtle aliasing bugs if they assume the return is a fresh copy. (metadataCache.test.ts exists but does not assert the mutation contract.)
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Either rename to make mutation explicit (mergeMetadataIntoDetailsInPlace) or return a new object ({ ...details, ...filled }) and stop mutating. Document the chosen contract and add a test.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — clarity / bug prevention _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/lib/metadataCache.ts:55-85

#### [L-SC30] Hard-coded slab/detail cost constants in the route duplicate and diverge from the provider cost table
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · tech-debt · effort S
- **1. Description:** The route hard-codes slab-detection cost inline (anthropic ? 0.003 : 0.001) and slab-detail cost (anthropic ? 0.005 : 0.002), while providers define estimateCostCents(callType) and analyticsServer defines PROVIDER_COSTS. Three separate sources of truth for AI call costs.
- **2. Issue identified:** Cost constants are duplicated across three files and already disagree: provider.estimateCostCents says slabDetection=0.2c (Anthropic)/0.05c (Gemini), but the route's slabDetectionMeta.cost stores $0.003/$0.001. When prices change, copies will be missed.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Centralize all per-call, per-provider cost constants in one module and reference it everywhere — route meta, analyticsServer, and providers. Delete the inline literals.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — maintainability; reduces risk of future cost-reporting drift _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/analyze/route.ts:402,555; src/lib/providers/anthropic.ts:397-408; src/lib/providers/gemini.ts:194-206; src/lib/analyticsServer.ts:51-54

#### [L-SC31] Gemini verifyAndEnrich omits the v1beta apiVersion used by every other Gemini call
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · tech-debt · effort S
- **1. Description:** In gemini.ts, analyzeImage, detectSlab, and extractSlabDetails all create the model with { apiVersion: 'v1beta' }, but verifyAndEnrich creates it with no apiVersion override, defaulting to v1.
- **2. Issue identified:** Inconsistent API version across calls on the same provider. If the pinned model id behaves differently or is only resolvable under v1beta, the verification fallback can fail or behave differently, silently degrading the fallback path.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add { apiVersion: 'v1beta' } to the verifyAndEnrich getGenerativeModel call, or centralize model creation in one helper.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — correctness/consistency of the Gemini fallback path _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — One-line consistency fix.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/providers/gemini.ts:108 vs :78, :128, :168

- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · code-quality · effort M
- **1. Description:** cover-candidates, con-mode-lookup, and variantResolver (constructed per enrichment call) each build their own new Anthropic({...}), in addition to AnthropicProvider in providers/anthropic.ts.

- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · code-quality · effort S

- **2. Issue identified:** Lines 431-436 export six ConMode aliases of the KeyHunt functions, plus a ConModeHistoryEntry type alias. Grep confirms none of these alias names are consumed anywhere outside offlineCache.ts. These are leftover compatibility shims from a Con-Mode/Key-Hunt rename.

#### [L-SC34] Clarify the two key-comics modules (static keyComicsDatabase vs DB-backed keyComicsDb)
- **Area / Dimension:** scanning · tech-debt · effort M

Issue identified:** Near-identical names are easy to confuse. keyComicsDb wraps keyComicsDatabase and layers a DB lookup, but the static file carries a @deprecated internal marker indicating an unfinished migration. #### [L-SO6] Reputation routes run reputation + feedback as two serial round-trips
- **Area / Dimension:** social · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** GET /api/reputation and /api/reputation/[userId] call getUserCreatorProfile then, if includeFeedback, await getUserFeedback sequentially.
- **2. Issue identified:** When includeFeedback=true the two independent Supabase reads run one after another instead of in parallel. getUserFeedback itself also does a separate COUNT then a SELECT.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Run getUserCreatorProfile and getUserFeedback with Promise.all. Optionally fetch feedback rows and total count in one query using { count: 'exact' }.

#### [L-SO7] submitFeedback duplicates the existing-feedback check
- **Area / Dimension:** social · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** submitFeedback first calls checkFeedbackEligibility (which queries transaction_feedback for an existing row) and then immediately runs a second SELECT on transaction_feedback for the same row.
- **2. Issue identified:** The existence of prior feedback is checked twice against the same table in the same call path, an extra Supabase round-trip. There is also no unique-constraint fallback handling even though unique_reviewer_transaction exists — concurrent double-submits rely solely on these read checks.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Remove the redundant second SELECT and rely on the unique_reviewer_transaction constraint, handling error.code 23505 to return 'already submitted'.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — one fewer query per submission _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes

#### [L-SO8] Block endpoint does not validate the blocked target exists
- **Area / Dimension:** social · security · effort S
- **1. Description:** POST /api/users/[userId]/block validates the userId is a UUID and prevents self-block, but inserts a block row for any UUID even if no such profile exists.
- **2. Issue identified:** No existence check before insert lets the user_blocks table accumulate orphan/garbage block rows; a non-existent target silently 'succeeds'. The insert does correctly ignore 23505. Low severity since the route is authenticated and self-block is prevented.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Before inserting, confirm blocked_id exists in profiles and return 404 otherwise.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — adds one cheap existence query _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/users/[userId]/block/route.ts:32-44

#### [L-SO9] Username route runs validation twice (standalone regex then validateUsernameComplete)
- **Area / Dimension:** social · code-quality · effort S
- **1. Description:** The username route runs USERNAME_REGEX.test(normalized) and then validateUsernameComplete(normalized) (which internally runs the same length+charset regex) in both GET and POST.
- **2. Issue identified:** Redundant validation: validateUsernameComplete already enforces 3-20 chars, charset, start/end/consecutive-underscore, profanity, and reserved checks. The separate USERNAME_REGEX pre-check duplicates length+charset and the two can drift — the local regex allows leading/trailing underscores that validateUsernameFormat then rejects with a different message.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Drop the standalone USERNAME_REGEX checks in the route and rely solely on validateUsernameComplete.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — code simplification _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/app/api/username/route.ts:17,70-84,138-150; src/lib/usernameValidation.ts:128-177

#### [L-SO10] Avatar upload accepts up to 5MB unoptimized images for an 80x80 render
- **Area / Dimension:** social · cost · effort M
- **1. Description:** CustomProfilePage avatar upload allows any image up to 5MB and passes it straight to Clerk user.setProfileImage; the avatar is rendered with next/image unoptimized.
- **2. Issue identified:** No client-side downscale/compression before upload means full 5MB photos are stored (Clerk CDN) and served, then rendered unoptimized at 80x80. Avatars only ever display tiny, so the large file wastes bandwidth. The image is hosted by Clerk, not the Supabase bucket, so it does not pressure the Supabase cap.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Downscale/compress to ~256px square via canvas before setProfileImage (the codebase already does client cover-image compression elsewhere).
- **4. Cost analysis:** Saves Netlify/Clerk bandwidth at scale; ~$0 today _(saved)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/components/CustomProfilePage.tsx:469-478, :707

#### [L-SO11] Settings page fetches follow counts via the heavier follow-status route
- **Area / Dimension:** social · performance · effort S
- **1. Description:** CustomProfilePage fetches follow counts by calling GET /api/follows/{profileId} (the follow-status endpoint), which also runs auth() + getProfileByClerkId + own-profile comparison.
- **2. Issue identified:** The account page only needs followerCount/followingCount, but it hits the per-user follow-status route. For the own-profile case the route short-circuits before checkFollowStatus, so the overhead is modest — but the page already separately calls /api/username/current, which loads the same profile row. Two profile-loading round-trips where one could carry counts.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Expose follower_count/following_count on /api/username/current (CachedProfile would need those two fields added) or add a dedicated counts endpoint.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — one fewer request per settings load _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes
- **Evidence:** src/components/CustomProfilePage.tsx:241-259, :177; src/app/api/follows/[userId]/route.ts:84-128; src/lib/db.ts:40-63

#### [L-SO12] Test creatorCreditsDb.transformFeedback row->type transform
- **Area / Dimension:** social · testing · effort S
- **1. Description:** src/lib/creatorCreditsDb.ts exports transformFeedback(row) mapping a DB FeedbackRow into a TransactionFeedback type.
- **2. Issue identified:** No creatorCreditsDb test file exists. transformFeedback is a DB-row transform — on the project's mandatory-test list — and is consumed in 5+ places. Wrong field mapping (snake_case->camelCase, null handling) would silently surface incorrect feedback data.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add a small unit test asserting transformFeedback maps each field and handles null/missing values correctly.
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Pure transform; trivial test.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/creatorCreditsDb.ts:71; no creatorCreditsDb test file exists

#### [L-SO13] Give notification badges screen-reader text
- **Area / Dimension:** social · accessibility · effort S
- **1. Description:** Unread-count badges render as a small colored circle with '9+' or a number on the desktop 'More' link and the mobile 'More' button.
- **2. Issue identified:** The badges have no accessible name/sr-only text describing what they count, so a screen-reader user hears only '9+' (or the bare number) with no context that these are unread notifications.
- **3. Recommended optimization:** Add an sr-only span like '{unreadCount} unread notifications' (or an aria-label on the parent control).
- **4. Cost analysis:** $0 — no cost change _(neutral)_
- **5. Can I handle autonomously:** Yes — Self-contained.
- **Evidence:** src/components/MobileNav.tsx:246-250; src/components/Navigation.tsx:395-399

## Coverage Notes

**Areas reviewed (feature-by-feature):** scanning/AI cover recognition, pricing (eBay Browse, cert lookup, key-comics), collection management, auctions, marketplace (fixed-price listings, offers, trading), email/notifications, payments (Stripe subscriptions + Connect), social (follows, reputation, blocking, usernames), messaging, admin tooling, frontend/PWA shell, and all documentation (README, ARCHITECTURE, TECHNICAL_FEATURES, EVALUATION, COST_PROJECTIONS, CLAUDE.md, SUBSCRIPTION_TIERS, EMAIL_TEST_CASES, docs/plans, docs/engineering-specs, docs/features, strategy briefs). Plus cross-cutting concerns: dependencies/supply-chain (npm audit), auth/RLS boundaries, caching (Upstash), rate-limiting, accessibility, bundle size, and the test suite/coverage configuration.

**Dimensions reviewed:** cost, correctness, security, performance, accessibility, database, bundle, dependencies, code-quality, tech-debt, testing, documentation, ux.

**Strong areas with few or no material findings (a good sign):**
- **Pure-helper test culture:** auction math (auction.test.ts), csvParsing, statsCalculator, bulkActions, uploadCoverImage/uploadLimits, certHelpers, secondChance, snipingProtection, coverValidation, and metadataCache are genuinely tested. The gaps found are concentrated in DB-coupled orchestrators and a few untested pure modules (contentFilter, validation, emailValidation, csvExport, certLookup parsers).
- **API security boundary:** the real authorization boundary on admin and most authenticated routes is correctly enforced server-side via getAdminProfile/auth(); the findings are defense-in-depth (page shells, one fail-open cron, one IDOR) rather than a broadly broken model.
- **Design system & responsiveness:** the Lichtenstein/pop-art system is consistent across most surfaces (MobileNav being the notable divergence), and mobile responsiveness is broadly handled.
- **Caching infrastructure:** the Upstash/Redis cache and 12h/negative-cache pattern are well-designed where applied; most pricing findings are about extending the existing pattern to one bypassing consumer rather than fixing the pattern itself.

**Caveats and verification notes:**
- **DB-dependent findings need a live-DB check before remediation:** several correctness items (increment_field double-count H-SO1, find_trade_matches columns H-MK8, estimated_value column M-MK11, audit-log count integrity) depend on the actual deployed Postgres schema/functions, which may differ from the tracked migrations. Verify against the live DB before applying.
- **Cost figures are estimates at stated assumptions.** Per-scan AI costs assume the documented Gemini-primary config; absolute monthly savings depend on real scan/import/marketplace volume, which is pre-launch/beta. The provider-order doc drift (H-DOC2/H-DOC3) means existing internal cost projections are themselves overstated.
- **One earlier claim was downgraded after failing to reproduce:** the alleged intermittent SIGSEGV from live GoTrueClient instantiation in tests (L-CC31) could not be reproduced; the real issue is log-noise/overhead, not a crash.
- **Migration-gated items are marked Partial autonomy:** every fix requiring a Supabase migration (unique indexes, RPCs, new columns, functional indexes, COMMENT changes) is flagged because applying migrations is a human-in-the-loop step in this project.
- **Several findings are intentionally cross-listed** (e.g. server-side cover WebP appears in both scanning and collection scope; the next/image wildcard appears in cross-cutting H-CC4 and frontend M-FE8; scan-resilience doc consolidation in L-DOC28/L-SC35) because they surfaced from independent agents reviewing different feature slices; they describe the same underlying change.
